But the *live, already-shipped* module id has always been `dashboard` (singular) — baked into `require_module("dashboard")`, `CORE_MODULES`, `deepLinks.js`, every role's `disabled_modules`/`features.json` entry. `module_registry.py` hard-enforces `manifest.id == directory name`, so creating `module_packages/dashboards/` with `id="dashboards"` would have been a genuine id RENAME — the same weight as Home Assistant's `home`→`home_assistant` move, needing its own carry-forward migration for every already-stored `disabled_modules`/role entry. Resolved by keeping `id="dashboard"`, directory `module_packages/dashboard/` — a zero-rename move, matching Automations'/Calendar's own "no rename requested" precedent, confirmed correct by direct grep rather than following the plan doc's own directory-name choice blindly.
  **`dashboards_service.py`/`dashboard_templates_service.py`/`dashboard_index.py` all stay core — the strongest "stays core" case of any conversion so far.** Every prior conversion's own service stayed core because *another router* imported it directly (Household/Team importing `task_service.py`, `user_deletion_service.py` importing `notes_service.py`). Dashboards adds a structurally stronger reason: `migrations/runner.py`'s own core migrations (m010 seed-home, m011/m012 grid-unit rescales) call `dashboards_service` directly, and migrations run **before** `discover_manifests()`/`register_routers()` even exist in boot order — a module package's own service literally cannot be imported that early, full stop, independent of how many routers import it. Two OTHER modules' own carry-forward migrations (household's m023, home_assistant's m018) independently import `dashboards_service._all_stores()` too, reinforcing the same conclusion from a second direction. Confirmed by direct grep before assuming — the original plan doc's own file list didn't even mention `dashboard_index.py` at all, a real, substantial 225-line file it had simply missed.
  **Zero block types owned — the first locked module with no `backend/dashboard_block.py` at all.** Dashboards is the container every other module's blocks render inside, never an entry in `dashboard_blocks/registry.py`'s own `REGISTRY` — confirmed by direct read that `dashboard_blocks/` (registry.py, render.py, every resolver) has genuinely zero FastAPI imports anywhere, the same "plumbing every module runs on top of, owned by none" category as `module_registry.py` itself, exactly as the original rollout plan's own closing note already anticipated.
  **Frontend: 6 of 19 `components/dashboard/` files moved, confirmed real by direct import-grep, not name-pattern-guessing — including one genuine surprise.** `CreateDashboardModal.jsx`/`DashboardAccessModal.jsx`/`DashboardSettingsModal.jsx`/`DashboardSwitcher.jsx`/`DashboardTemplateManager.jsx`/`DashboardHero.jsx` moved into `module_packages/dashboard/frontend/` — each reached only from `pages/Dashboard.jsx` or another Dashboards-owned file. `DashboardGrid.jsx`/`BlockRenderer.jsx`/`blockRegistry.js`/`blocks.jsx`/`actionKinds.js`/`BlockPicker.jsx` and 5 generic field-kind pickers stay core — real, verified cross-module consumers (`DashboardGrid`/`BlockRenderer` are imported directly by `Chat.jsx`'s own live pending-write preview; `blocks.jsx` is imported by every converted module's own block components). **`DashboardPicker.jsx`, despite its name, is NOT page-owned** — reached only via `BlockPicker.jsx` → `ModuleAndRecordPicker.jsx`'s `dashboard: DashboardPicker` kind-map entry (configuring a `nav_button` block whose target is a specific dashboard), never imported by `Dashboard.jsx` itself. Exactly the kind of thing name-pattern-matching alone would have gotten wrong — confirmed by direct grep of every real importer before moving anything.
  **A genuinely new structural problem no prior conversion needed to solve: the app's own home route.** `App.jsx`'s `<Route path="/" element={<Dashboard />} />` was never wrapped in `ModuleRoute` — confirmed by direct read, and confirmed why: `ModuleRoute` redirects to `/` when a module is in the user's disabled set, so a user with `dashboard` disabled would hit a self-targeting redirect loop while already AT `/` if this route were fed through the generic `MODULE_PACKAGES.map()` loop the normal way. No prior converted module ever claimed the root path, so this exact problem never came up before. Fixed by keeping the hardcoded, unwrapped route (import path updated only) and filtering the generic loop (`MODULE_PACKAGES.filter(pkg => pkg.to !== '/')`) so it never generates a second, wrapped route for `/`. A related, smaller decision: the manifest.js deliberately declares **no `loadPage` field at all** — every other converted module's page loads via `lazy(pkg.loadPage)` inside that now-filtered loop, but since App.jsx imports `Dashboard.jsx` eagerly instead, a `loadPage` here would just define a dynamic `import()` call site nothing ever invokes — Vite's bundler creates a real, separate chunk for that syntax regardless of whether it's ever called, so declaring it would have shipped a genuinely orphaned, never-fetched `Dashboard-*.js` chunk alongside the page's real code (already inlined into the main bundle via the static import). Confirmed by inspecting the actual `vite build` output before and after — no such orphaned chunk exists.
  **Same enforcement-gap shape as Chat's/Notes' own conversions, no new mechanism gap this time.** All 10 dashboard AI tools lived unfiltered in `agent_service.py`'s static `_USER_TOOLS` list — closed by moving them into `module_packages/dashboard/backend/agent_tools.py` (owned, filtered by `disabled_modules` for the first time). No `owned_brain_paths` gap existed here the way it did for Chat/Notes — Dashboards data is JSON, not markdown, the same structural category as Tasks, so `"Dashboards"` was added to the *unconditional* structural skip set (`routers/brain.py`'s `_ALWAYS_SKIP`, `agent_service.py`'s `_brain_skip()`) alongside `"Tasks"`, not the conditional per-user one Chat's `"Chats"`/Notes' `"Notes"` use — `owned_brain_paths=["Dashboards"]` is still declared on the manifest too, redundant but honest, exactly matching Tasks' own precedent for the identical reason. Notes' own workspace-threading fix from the day before already covered every dashboard tool's real need (all 10 were already correctly workspace-threaded inline pre-move) — confirmed by direct read, no further dispatch-mechanism change needed here, just the relocation.
  **A real, previously-undocumented design difference found while writing router tests, not a bug**: unlike pool Notes/Tasks (workspace-visible by default to any member), a pool dashboard's non-admin access comes *only* from an explicit `contributors` grant (`dashboards_service._contributor_access()`) — a plain household/team member sees nothing about a newly-created pool dashboard until one is added, even though an admin always gets `edit` on it immediately. First caught as a failing test assertion (assumed Notes' own pool-default-read behavior applied here too); confirmed by direct read of `_contributor_access()` that this is Dashboards' own real, intentional design, not a bug this conversion should "fix" — the test was corrected to match reality instead.
  Full suite: 950 passing (up from 936), +14 net (8 new router tests + 6 new conversion-machinery tests; the 19 moved AI-tool tests net to zero since they just relocated in full — unlike Notes, no core-staying exception tool exists here to split off).

- **2026-08-26 — Notes converted into `module_packages/notes/` — the first conversion to genuinely test the sidecar-share-index sharing pattern, and the first to expose a real gap in the module-tool-dispatch mechanism itself (not just a per-module enforcement hole).** Owner, after confirming the compact-density composer fix worked: "that was it. commit and move on." — committed Chat, then proceeded directly into Notes per the tracked rollout order, same upfront research pass every increment gets (an Explore agent covering the full file inventory, the sharing mechanism specifically since this sets precedent for Assets/Contacts/Finance, every cross-module consumer of `notes_service.py`/`notes_index.py`, and existing test coverage).
  **`notes_service.py`/`notes_index.py` both stay core** — confirmed by direct grep, not assumption: `services/user_deletion_service.py` imports both directly, alongside the equivalent `assets_service`/`assets_index`, `contacts_service`/`contacts_index`, `finance_service`/`finance_index` pairs, treating all four sharing-capable stores uniformly during account deletion; `main.py`'s `_warm_share_index()` unconditionally rebuilds `notes_index` at every boot the same way it does for the other three. Same "real external consumers keep a service in core" pattern as `task_service.py`/`events_service.py`.
  **The sharing mechanism, confirmed precisely before touching anything, since this sets precedent for three future conversions**: sidecar `Notes/_shares.json` per store (content stays plain `.md`), folder-cascade access resolution via an ancestor-walk (`_entry_for_path()` — most-specific wins, same pattern archiving's own `_is_archived_path()` reuses), two audience shapes mutually exclusive per path (`shared_with` for named-user handshake shares, `contributors` for pool shares — no handshake, since pool = already workspace-visible by construction), `read < contribute < edit` access rungs, and a derived owner→workspace share-routing cache (`notes_index.py`, explicitly modeled on `contacts_index.py`) so `list_visible_notes`/`find_note_store` never scan every user's sidecar file on every request. Structurally the simplest of the four sharing modules (no per-field caps, no comments/audit-log, no cross-book transfer complexity) — a deliberate, good first proof of the pattern before Assets/Contacts/Finance's own future conversions have to reckon with their own added complexity on top of it.
  **Gap #1 — all 7 note AI tools lived in `agent_service.py`'s unfiltered static `_USER_TOOLS` list.** `_get_tools()` only ever filtered *module-owned* tool schemas by `disabled_modules` — a user with Notes disabled could still list/read/create/update/delete/move notes via chat, since these tools weren't module-owned yet. Moved into `module_packages/notes/backend/agent_tools.py` (`list_notes`/`read_note`/`create_note`/`update_note`/`delete_note`/`move_note`/`create_note_folder`), closing the gap as a natural side effect of the move — the same shape as Chat's own `/chat/runs` gating gap, found during the same upfront research pass that confirms every conversion's file inventory, not introduced by the conversion. `search_brain` stays core, deliberately not moved — it also rglobs journal/memory/profile files, genuinely cross-cutting, not notes-owned.
  **Gap #2 — chat's own Brain folder pattern repeated: no `owned_brain_paths` entry meant a disabled user's `Notes/` folder had zero protection.** Closed by declaring `owned_brain_paths=["Notes"]` — the generic gap-closing machinery built once for Journal (increment 1) closes a real hole in a completely different module the moment its manifest finally declares what it owns, with zero new code, same as it did for Chat's `Chats/` folder the day before.
  **Gap #3 — `note_embed`'s dashboard block had no `module=` gate at all**, the exact `pool_tasks`-before-Household/Team situation: disabling Notes didn't remove it from the block picker or lock its rendering for other users. Closed by adding `module="notes"` to its `BlockSpec` when `_notes.py` moved to `module_packages/notes/backend/dashboard_block.py`, and declaring `owned_block_types=["note_embed"]` on the manifest — the already-proven-generic catalog-filtering/`render_block()` gating mechanism (built for Household/Team, tested via synthetic fake modules in `test_dashboard_block_module_gating.py`) just needed the real manifest to say what it owns.
  **A genuinely new mechanism gap, not a per-module enforcement hole — the module-tool-dispatch call site never threaded `workspace` through.** `_module_tool_dispatch()`'s fallback call site (`execute_fn(name, inputs, user)`, 3 args) had no way to pass the caller's active workspace to a module's own `execute()`. Harmless for every prior module: Household/Team's tools always operate on their fixed pseudo-user regardless of workspace; Journal/Home Assistant have no workspace concept at all; Tasks' own agent_tools.py docstring already documented its 9 tools as implicitly personal-only, "not a regression introduced by this move; not fixed here either" — none of them ever *used* workspace even before their own conversions moved them out of inline code. Notes' tools are different: the pre-conversion inline code in `agent_service.py` genuinely threaded `workspace` through every single `notes_service` call (a note lives in either the personal or business `Notes/` folder) — silently dropping it during extraction would have been a real regression, not a no-op, unlike every prior case. Fixed at the root, matching this project's own established philosophy for exactly this class of discovery: widened the dispatch signature to `execute(name, inputs, user, workspace)` and updated the call site (`agent_service.py`'s `case _:` fallback) plus all four existing `agent_tools.py` modules (household/tasks/journal/home_assistant) to accept the new parameter with a `"personal"` default, unused — signature parity without changing any of their own documented behavior. Tasks' own workspace-blindness stays exactly as documented, deliberately not fixed as a side effect of an unrelated module's conversion.
  **Test-file basename collision, same fix as Household+Team's own precedent**: `module_packages/notes/tests/test_agent_tools.py` collided with `module_packages/household/tests/test_agent_tools.py` (neither directory has an `__init__.py`) — renamed to `test_notes_agent_tools.py`. Full suite: 935 passing (up from 916), +19 net (13 new router tests + 6 new conversion-machinery tests; the 6 moved AI-tool tests net to zero since they just relocated).
  **Same-day follow-up — a fourth gap, found by directly answering the owner's own verification question rather than by rote research.** Owner asked, before authorizing commit: "does removing notes cause any issues with any other module storing data." Direct answer: no — `user_deletion_service.py`/`dashboard_blocks/_actions.py`'s `notes_service` calls are unconditional by design (Brain export's own "deliberate no-op" philosophy: the underlying data was never meant to be install-state-gated, only the browse/AI-tool *paths to it* are), and pool notes live entirely inside Notes' own per-pool store, structurally separate from Household's/Team's own `task_service`/`events_service` data. But answering it properly meant re-reading `search_brain`'s full implementation rather than trusting the "unconditional, unaffected by this conversion" claim already written into that test file's own docstring — and that re-read found a real, narrower gap the docstring's claim had missed: `search_brain` has two halves, a plain rglob over the caller's own files (correctly gated by `_brain_skip()`, which now includes "Notes" when disabled, exactly as intended) and a second loop reaching shared-to-them/pool notes directly via `notes_service.list_visible_notes()` — which bypassed `_brain_skip()` entirely, gated by nothing. A user with Notes disabled could still search content **shared with them** (not their own) through chat, even though their own Notes/ folder was correctly hidden. Fixed by skipping the second loop outright when `"Notes" in skip` — same skip-set the first loop already computes, no new logic surface. **How to apply**: when a tool/endpoint reaches the same conceptual data through two different code paths (a generic file walk plus a dedicated cross-store lookup), confirm BOTH paths are gated the same way — a docstring or comment asserting "this is already handled" is a claim to verify by re-reading the code, not a fact to inherit, especially when answering a real correctness question the owner asked directly. **Verified**: new test `test_search_brain_hides_shared_note_content_when_notes_disabled_for_viewer`, full suite 936 passing.

- **2026-08-26 — Chat converted into `module_packages/chat/` — the second LOCKED (`uninstallable: True`) module after Tasks, and the first conversion where the module's own orchestration engine needed zero changes because it was already confirmed permanent core infrastructure.** Owner: "good. then that coversion looks good. lets move on" (confirming Household+Team) — proceeded directly into Chat per the tracked rollout order, same upfront research pass every increment gets.
  **`services/agent_service.py` stays 100% core — confirmed by direct code read, not assumed from the plan doc.** Zero imports from any router anywhere in the file, zero HTTP-shape assumptions (no `Request`/`HTTPException`/`Depends`), zero module-gating logic of its own (`require_module` is purely the router's job). Independent, pre-existing confirmation this split was already correctly anticipated: `module_registry.py`'s own `register_routers()` docstring had already named "Tasks, Chat, Dashboards" as deeply load-bearing modules before this conversion touched a single line. Only `routers/chat.py` (675 lines, a thin HTTP wrapper around `agent_service.py`) moved to `module_packages/chat/backend/router.py` — the smallest single-file move of any conversion so far, since there was no service file of Chat's own to leave behind or bring along.
  **Session/pending-turn/presence bookkeeping stays core as one unit with the orchestration loop itself, not just because of "who calls it."** `save_pending_turn()` is called from **inside** `run_agent()`'s own step loop (3 call sites) — you cannot split session bookkeeping out from the loop that produces it without breaking the loop. Confirms `agent_service.py` was never a candidate for even partial conversion.
  **Gap #1 — two of Chat's own 11 endpoints were never actually gated on the module.** `GET /chat/runs` and `GET /chat/runs/{run_id}` used plain `Depends(get_current_user)` instead of `Depends(_require_chat)` — the only two exceptions in the file, found during the same upfront research pass that confirms every other conversion's file inventory. A user with chat disabled via their feature role could still read past agent tool-use run history through these two. Fixed by switching both to `_require_chat`, matching the other 9 endpoints. A real pre-existing bug this conversion happened to be positioned to catch, not something the conversion itself introduced or was scoped to hunt for.
  **Gap #2 — chat's own Brain folder had zero protection, unlike every other module's owned data.** `routers/brain.py`'s skip-list (`_ALWAYS_SKIP = {"Tasks", "Business"}`, unconditional/structural) was never the whole story — `brain_paths_for_disabled()` unions it with every *disabled* module's own `owned_brain_paths`, conditional per user, the same mechanism Journal/Home Assistant/Notes/etc. already use to hide their folders from the Brain browser and the AI's own `list_brain_files`/`read_brain_file`/`search_brain` tools when disabled. Chat's manifest had declared no `owned_brain_paths` at all before this conversion — meaning a disabled user's own `Chats/` archive folder was **fully readable both ways** regardless of chat's module state, a real, previously-open gap (not a new one this conversion created). Closed by declaring `owned_brain_paths=["Chats"]` — the module system's generic gap-closing machinery (built once, for Journal, in increment 1) closed a real hole in a completely different module the moment its manifest finally declared what it owns, with zero new code.
  **Dead code found and removed, not left as a TODO**: `routers/suggestions.py` defined `_require_chat = require_module("chat")` but never called it anywhere in that ~100-line file — a leftover from an earlier draft, confirmed by exhaustive grep before removing both the assignment and the now-unnecessary `require_module` import.
  **Test-file split, same pattern as Household's `test_agent_member_resolution.py` move**: `tests/test_chat_sessions.py` (24 tests before this conversion) split into `module_packages/chat/tests/test_chat_router.py` (14 tests exercising the moved router: `_write_chat_archive`, `POST /chat`'s session/resume/error paths, `GET /chat/pending/{chat_id}`, presence-gated notifications) and a trimmed `tests/test_chat_sessions.py` (10 tests, pure `agent_service.py` session-index/presence coverage, no router involved). The `brain` fixture's Tasks-era fix (auto-installs every `uninstallable=True` module) paid off immediately here too — zero explicit `mark_installed` calls needed anywhere in the moved test files, confirming that fix was genuinely generic, not tuned to Tasks specifically. Full suite: 916 passing (up from 911), the +5 coming entirely from the new `test_chat_module_conversion.py`.

- **2026-08-25 (fifth) — Household and Team converted together into `module_packages/household/` and `module_packages/team/` — the first two-module increment, surfacing two real, generic gaps in the module system itself, both fixed generically rather than patched per-module.** Owner: "lets move on to the next one" — proceeded per the tracked rollout order (Team+Household were always scheduled right after Tasks, specifically because neither owns its own service file). Confirmed by direct code read before starting: two structurally separate routers/pools/pseudo-users, "no data can cross between the two" (AGENTS.md) — so two real module packages, not one merged package, exactly matching the plan's own "Team + Household" framing as a scheduling grouping, not a merge instruction.
  **`task_service.py`/`events_service.py`/`routers/_task_models.py`/`routers/_event_models.py` all stay core** — both routers import them directly for their own pool data, same "real external consumers keep a service in core" pattern as every prior conversion, just with two dependents pointing at the same core services now instead of one.
  **Gap #1 — a shared dashboard block couldn't be gated once both pools became real modules.** The old `pool_tasks` block type served both `_household` and `_team` via one resolver keyed off `ctx.workspace`, registered with no `module=` at all (unowned, ungated) — the deliberate, correct call at Tasks' own conversion, since splitting it prematurely (before Household/Team existed as real modules) would have been guesswork. Now that both are real, `BlockSpec.module` — confirmed by direct read of `registry.py`, a single optional string — cannot express "gate on household if personal, team if business" for one block type. Split into `household_tasks` (`module="household"`, `workspace="personal"`) and `team_tasks` (`module="team"`, `workspace="business"`), each in its own module's `dashboard_block.py`, following `home_assistant_favourites`'s own `workspace=` field as direct precedent. This also closed a second, independent, previously-undocumented bug: `resolve_pool_tasks` never checked that `ctx.workspace` actually matched the pool it was about to read — a business-workspace viewer requesting a stale/mixed-workspace dashboard block could have read household data (or vice versa) with zero gate. Splitting into two statically-scoped resolvers closes this for free, since each one now only ever handles its own fixed pool. A migration (household's own `m023`, run once, rewrites `pool_tasks` → `household_tasks`/`team_tasks` **per-dashboard, based on that dashboard's own workspace** — not a flat 1:1 rename like `home_assistant`'s `m018`, since the same old type string meant different things depending on where it was found) carries forward any dashboard that already had the old block. `migrations/runner.py`'s core `m010_seed_home_dashboards` (which seeds every new user's first dashboard) also had its own hardcoded `"pool_tasks"` literal for business-workspace users — found and fixed the same way `m010`'s `home_favourites` hardcode was caught and fixed during Home Assistant's own rename; same zero-risk reasoning (the function only ever runs once, per-instance, and has already run on every existing instance).
  **Gap #2 — the module system had no way to mark a tool admin-only.** Household owns 5 shared-task-management AI tools (4 admin-only + `complete_shared_task`, member-accessible) — the first tools any converted module has owned that need restricting to admins. `_get_tools()`'s existing module-dispatch path offered every `owned_agent_tools` schema to any module-enabled user regardless of role (correct for every prior module, since none owned an admin-only tool) — moving these 4 via that mechanism unchanged would have OFFERED their schemas to non-admin household members (the executor's own `if user.get("role") != "admin"` check would still have blocked actual execution, so no real data-access hole, but a real behavior regression from today, where they're core `_ADMIN_TOOLS` and never shown to non-admins at all). Fixed generically: `ModuleManifest` gained `admin_agent_tools: list[str]`, and `module_registry.admin_agent_tool_names()` unions it across active modules — mirrors `read_only_agent_tools`/`read_only_agent_tool_names()`'s exact shape, added for journal's read/write distinction. `_get_tools()` now filters module-contributed schemas through both unions.
  **A real, previously-invisible gap in the `brain` test fixture, round two.** Tasks' own conversion (this same day) taught the fixture to auto-install every LOCKED module — but Household/Team are optional, not locked, so 3 more pre-existing tests broke for a related-but-distinct reason: `test_new_tools_are_write_gated_by_default` and `test_get_effective_disabled_workspace_keyed_dict` both used `"team"`/`"add_shared_task"` as arbitrary example strings, written before either was a real discovered module — once `"team"` became real, `get_effective_disabled()`'s not-installed union started marking it disabled in EVERY workspace (since this bare test brain never marks it installed), breaking an assertion that was never actually about Team's own semantics. Fixed the two tests at the root, not around it: the former now explicitly installs household before asserting on `add_shared_task`'s gating (a real, meaningful thing to test, now that household is real); the latter swapped its placeholder module name from `"team"` to `"finance"` — a CORE (non-`module_packages/`) id that can never collide with this class of bug again, regardless of what converts next.
  **Zero router-level test coverage existed for `routers/shared.py`/`routers/team.py` before this conversion** — confirmed by grep across the whole test suite, a real, pre-existing gap this conversion closes (not scope creep beyond it, since these routers' own code is what's actually moving). New tests call the endpoint functions directly with a pre-resolved user dict (this suite's established convention, see `test_mod_store_router.py`), covering CRUD + attribution + 404/400 + — the real guarantee AGENTS.md documents — that a write to one pool is provably invisible to the other pool's own query. Deliberately does NOT attempt to test the `require_module`/`require_pool_edit` dependency chain itself (untestable without a `TestClient`, which has zero precedent anywhere in this suite) — a systemic gap across dozens of routers, not something unique to this conversion to fix.
  **Goals-style precedent held without needing a new mechanism.** `admin/Household.jsx`/`admin/Team.jsx` (single-purpose admin config pages, pool priorities + `PoolBankConnections`) call zero household/team-owned endpoints — confirmed by direct read, only the generic `priorities` client. Left them exactly where they are in core `pages/settings/admin/`, the same reasoning as Tasks' own Goals decision: nothing about the routing mechanism actually needed a module to declare a second route, so no new manifest field was invented for a case that didn't require one.

- **2026-08-25 (fourth) — Tasks converted into `module_packages/tasks/` — the first LOCKED (`uninstallable: True`) module conversion, proving that mechanism end-to-end for real (not just a synthetic test fixture) for the first time, and the largest/highest-blast-radius conversion yet.** Owner: "commit it. then move forward" — proceeded directly into Tasks per the tracked rollout order, doing the same upfront research pass (an Explore agent) every increment gets before writing code, since this was a materially bigger, more foundational module than any prior one (Team/Household import its service directly; 5 dashboard block types; 14 AI-tool call sites; TaskModal/TaskPicker reused by 6 other components).
  **`task_service.py`, `priority_service.py`, `recurring_service.py`, and `routers/_task_models.py` all stay in core** — confirmed by direct grep, not inference: `routers/shared.py` (Household) and `routers/team.py` both import `task_service`/`_task_models` directly for their own pool tasks (against the `"_household"`/`"_team"` pseudo-users), and `agent_service.py`/`suggestions_service.py`/`dashboard_blocks/{_actions,_assets,_pool}.py` all depend on `task_service` directly too — none of those ever convert. Same "real external consumers keep a service in core" pattern as `ha_service.py`/`n8n_service.py`/`events_service.py`, just with more dependents than any prior case.
  **A real bug found before it could ship, not after**: `_tasks.py` (the old dashboard-block resolver file) defined `_scoped_target()`, a small helper that decides whether a `scope_configurable` block renders as the viewer or the dashboard owner — and despite living in a file named `_tasks.py`, it was never task-specific. Direct grep found **7 other files** importing it: 3 core resolvers (`_actions.py`, `_assets.py`, `_ai_usage.py`) plus every one of the 4 already-shipped converted modules' own `dashboard_block.py` (journal, home_assistant, automations, calendar). Moving `_tasks.py` into the module package without extracting this first would have broken every converted module's own scope-configurable blocks at import time — the whole app's dashboard system, not just Tasks'. Fixed as step zero, before any file moved: extracted to `dashboard_blocks/registry.py` as `scoped_target()` (public, no underscore — genuinely shared infra, same tier as `BlockRenderCtx`/`BlockSpec` it already lives next to), updated all 7 dependent imports in one pass, verified every one still imports cleanly before touching `_tasks.py` itself.
  **A second, frontend-side version of the exact same mistake, caught by ESLint before it shipped**: the first draft moved `TaskRow` (the shared row-renderer 4 of Tasks' 5 block components use) into `module_packages/tasks/frontend/TaskRow.jsx` too — `eslint --max-warnings 0` immediately flagged `'TaskRow' is not defined` in core `blocks.jsx`'s `LinkedTasksBlock` (an Assets-owned block, unrelated to Tasks), which also used it. Worse than a missed dependency: moving it there would have created a genuine circular import (`TaskRow.jsx` needs `BlockActionButtons` from `blocks.jsx`; `blocks.jsx` would have needed `TaskRow` from `TaskRow.jsx`) — exactly the shape `actionKinds.js` was originally extracted to avoid between `blockRegistry.js` and `blocks.jsx`. Fixed by keeping `TaskRow` in core `blocks.jsx` (exported, like `BlockActionButtons`), with Tasks' own 4 block files importing it back cross-package — the same direction/pattern `BlockActionButtons` itself already uses, once `TaskRow` was recognized as equally cross-cutting rather than task-exclusive.
  **Goals (`/goals`) deliberately did NOT get its own manifest entry.** Confirmed by direct code read: no `require_module("goals")` exists anywhere in the backend — `App.jsx`'s `/goals` route has always used `moduleId="tasks"`, the same permission as `/tasks` itself. `goals` is a purely frontend nav-only id (its own `CORE_MODULES` entry, its own `content/help.json` section, its own feature-role toggle) that happens to share Tasks' permission gate — it was never manifest-driven to begin with, so converting Tasks didn't require inventing a new "multiple pages per module" mechanism the way it first looked like it might. Only the *file* (`Goals.jsx`) moved into `module_packages/tasks/frontend/`; its nav entry, help section, and role-toggle semantics all stayed exactly where they were, and `App.jsx`'s hand-written `/goals` route just points at the new file path.
  **The `brain` test fixture had a real, previously-invisible gap, exposed only by the first locked module.** `conftest.py`'s `brain` fixture never ran migrations or marked any module installed — harmless for every optional module (a fresh instance genuinely starts without journal/home_assistant/automations/calendar installed), but Tasks becoming `module=`-gated on its dashboard blocks and `disabled_modules`-filtered on its AI tools meant 7 pre-existing, module-agnostic tests (generic dashboard-scope tests, agent approve-mode tests) that happened to use a task block/tool as their example started failing with `module_disabled`/silently-missing-tool. Root cause: in REAL production, `main.py`'s `lifespan()` always runs pending migrations — which mark a locked module installed unconditionally, no existence-guard — before the app ever serves a single request, so no running instance can ever actually reach the state the bare fixture was implicitly testing. Fixed the fixture itself (not each test individually): it now marks every discovered `uninstallable=True` module installed, mirroring the one guarantee that's always true the moment any real boot completes. Two more tests needed a small, honest follow-up fix — `test_mod_store_service.py`'s history-log tests asserted an exact event count/list that the fixture's own legitimate `tasks: install` history write now contributes to; filtered to the specific module id each test is actually about, rather than asserting on the whole log.
  **`m021`'s upgrade migration has no existence-guard at all** — genuinely simpler than every prior conversion's guarded migration (journal's/automations'/calendar's all check `_system/features.json` existence to tell "upgrading instance" from "fresh install"). Tasks was never optional, on a brand-new instance exactly as much as an upgrading one, so it just calls `mark_installed()` unconditionally — correct specifically because every boot, first-ever or thousandth, runs pending migrations before `register_routers()`, so this self-heals identically regardless of when it first runs.
  **9 personal-scope AI tools moved (`list_tasks`/`add_task`/`update_task`/`delete_task`/`get_top3_tasks`/`get_scored_tasks`/`get_task_history`/`create_tasks`/`get_week_snapshot`); 5 household-pool ones deliberately did not** (`list_shared_tasks`/`add_shared_task`/`update_shared_task`/`delete_shared_task`/`complete_shared_task`) — same reasoning as `dashboard_blocks/_pool.py`'s `PoolTasksBlock` staying core and unowned until Household/Team's own future conversion: conceptually Household's domain, just implemented via `task_service` against `"_household"`, not Tasks' own.

- **2026-08-25 (third) — Calendar converted into `module_packages/calendar/` — the cleanest conversion yet (no id/directory/route/display-name change at all), the first with a real single-record deep link, and the first to ship a new cross-module visibility feature alongside the move.** Owner, after being asked to interview first rather than just diving in: "keep as calendar" — unlike Home Assistant's full internal rename or Automations' display-name-only rename, nothing about calendar's own naming changed; the conversion is a pure code relocation. Confirmed by direct code read (not assumed from the plan doc) before starting: Household's/Team's own pool calendars are functionally independent of this module's install state — `routers/shared.py`/`routers/team.py` gate their event endpoints on `require_module("household")`/`require_module("team")` only, never `require_module("calendar")` — so `events_service.py` and `routers/_event_models.py` both stayed in core (same "real external consumers keep a service in core" pattern as `ha_service.py`/`n8n_service.py`), and a dedicated test (`test_household_calendar_is_independent_of_calendar_module_state`) exercises the guarantee at the service layer.
  **New feature riding along, from the same interview turn**: the owner noticed mid-conversation that the personal Calendar's household/team pool-events toggle would need to hide itself once Household/Team eventually convert too — "probably should make a clause where if household/team module isnt installed then the button in calendar to show household/team events/tasks is hidden." Implemented as `poolModuleVisible` in `Calendar.jsx`, the exact same stacked installed+active check already used by `ModuleRoute` and `Hosting.jsx`'s `homeInstalled`/`automationsInstalled` (`!disabledModules.includes(id) && (!isPackageModule(id) || activeModuleIds.includes(id))`). Since Household/Team haven't converted yet, `isPackageModule('household'|'team')` is currently `false` and the check trivially passes today — but it's already correct for the moment they do convert, needing zero rework then. Gates three things together: the pool-data fetch in `load()` (skips the network call entirely, not just the render), both task/event merge computations, and the toggle button's own visibility — deliberately not `onEditEvent`'s guard, since pool events are already absent from the merged list (and so never reach that handler) once the gate trips.
  **New generic mechanism**: Calendar is the first converting module with an actual `?<param>=<id>` deep-link handler (`?event=<id>`, opens that event's edit modal). `deepLinks.js`'s `RECORD_PARAM` had no merge mechanism for this (unlike `MODULE_ROUTES`, which already merged `to` from every module's manifest) — added a `recordParam` field to the frontend manifest shape and generalized `RECORD_PARAM` the same way, so no future converted module needing its own record deep link has to touch `deepLinks.js` by hand either.
  **Two core shared components needed a cross-package import for the first time**: `EventModal.jsx` and `EventPicker.jsx` (both used by Household.jsx/Team.jsx for pool events too, so they stay in core, not the module) import `calendarApi` from `module_packages/calendar/frontend/api.js` — the same cross-package pattern the two extracted dashboard block components (`UpcomingEventsBlock`/`SingleEventBlock`) use importing `BlockActionButtons` back from core `blocks.jsx`. Neither direction is a cycle (core never imports from `module_packages/`), so this is safe and expected to recur as more modules convert.

- **2026-08-25 (second) — A shared display field with many consumers needs a second, narrower field when exactly one of those consumers has a real layout constraint the others don't — not a rename of the shared field itself.** Real owner feedback after pinning "n8n Automation" to their phone's shortcut bar: it wrapped to two lines in `Layout.jsx`'s fixed mobile bottom nav (a tight flex row of ~5 items, default `align-items: stretch`), stretching the whole row taller than its siblings. Owner's own diagnosis + fix request: shorten *only* that one surface, keep every other UI (desktop sidebar, Shortcuts picker, role editor, the "All Modules" drawer, `nav_button`'s module picker) showing the full name — all of which read the same `ALL_MODULES`/manifest `label` field this bug lived in.
  **Why not just shorten `label`**: every one of those other consumers reads the identical field — shortening it would have silently shortened all of them too, which the owner explicitly didn't want. Traced the actual layout mechanism before choosing a fix, not just applied the requested text change blindly: the desktop "Pinned" sidebar section and the drawer both render pinned items as independent vertical-list/grid entries (one item wrapping doesn't stretch its siblings), so only the horizontal flex bar genuinely had the bug — confirmed there was exactly one real consumer to special-case, not a guess.
  **How to apply**: added `navLabel` (optional, undefined for every module that doesn't need it, falls back to `label` wherever it's not explicitly read) to the module manifest shape — `module_packages/automations/manifest.js` sets `navLabel: 'Automations'`, threaded through `constants.js`'s `MODULE_PACKAGES` merge, read (with `|| label` fallback) only in `Layout.jsx`'s mobile bottom-bar rendering. The general pattern: when a request is "surface X should show something different from everywhere else," check first whether X is one of several *independent* consumers of a shared field (add a second, optional, narrowly-read field) versus a single rendering path serving multiple call sites (a local override there instead) — don't reach for a blanket rename or a prop drilled through everything on the assumption that "the one place that asked" is the only place that matters.
  Also: re-swept the module's own files for leftover bare "Automations" text after being asked "was there anything you didn't do" — found and fixed two real gaps the original conversion missed (the page's own `<h1>` heading, a picker's own "unavailable" fallback string) that a full-codebase grep at conversion time should have caught but didn't, since both were plain hardcoded JSX strings, not derived from any manifest field. A reminder that "I renamed the manifest" is not the same claim as "I renamed everywhere the old name was hardcoded" — the two require separately verifying, every time.

- **2026-08-25 — Automations converted into `module_packages/automations/` (n8n Automation) — the first module with two dashboard block types (generalized manifest.js's schema for every converted module), the first managing its own Docker container, and a deliberately narrower rename than Home Assistant's (display name only, at the owner's explicit instruction).** Owner: "do this one the same as home assistant" but "probably fine to leave backend names alone and just change forward facing names" — id/directory/routes/router prefix/AI-tool-names/block-type-strings all stayed exactly "automations"/`workflow_status`/`inbox_summary`; only `display_name`/`label`/catalog name/help title changed to "n8n Automation" (matching Hosting.jsx's own pre-existing header text, not re-invented). No migration needed for the rename itself as a result — contrast with Home Assistant, where the id itself changed and needed m017/m018.
  **Two services stayed in core, mirroring `ha_service.py`'s precedent but with more dependents**: `n8n_service.py` (`scheduler.py`'s boot/cron jobs, `infisical_loader.py`'s secrets pipeline, and a dashboard-blocks action resolver all import it directly) and `automations_config.py` (`routers/contacts.py` and `routers/assets.py` depend on it for their own, unrelated automation-token endpoints — nothing to do with n8n specifically). `automation_inbox_service.py` (renamed `inbox_service.py`) did move — its only real consumer is the block being converted, same shape as journal's own service.
  **`manifest.js`'s `block:` (singular) generalized to `blocks:` (array), across every converted module, not just the new one** — automations is the first module owning two block types (`workflow_status`, `inbox_summary`), which the existing singular-key schema had no way to express. Updated journal's and Home Assistant's manifest.js files too (wrapping their one block in a one-item array) rather than adding a second, parallel schema shape only automations uses. Caught a real bug in my own first draft of `blockRegistry.js`'s generalized merge loop: hand-picking `icon`/`label`/`defaultLayout`/`Component` to copy across silently dropped `shape`/`chromeless`/`recordKind` — fields some *existing* block types already relied on, caught immediately because `inbox_summary` itself needs `shape: 'list'`. Fixed by spreading every field a manifest block declares instead of naming them, which is also now correct for any field a future block might need without another manual update.
  **`automations_business` removed, not converted** — a role-editor-only checkbox (`_CORE_MODULE_IDS`/`_BUSINESS_MEMBER`/`constants.js`) that an exhaustive grep (spawned as an Explore agent, given the file count) confirmed was *never checked anywhere in the backend router* — every scope has always gone through one single `require_module("automations")` gate, `?scope=`/workspace-distinguished, not a second module id. Keeping a checkbox that does nothing would be more misleading after the real module exists, not less honest to leave alone. Also found (and fixed) genuinely stale AGENTS.md/API.md text claiming this toggle hid a business tab client-side — it never did.
  **A bug found by accident, not by design**: mid-conversion, `git status` showed `brain/USERS/_template/Home/.gitkeep` deleted and a new `_template/HomeAssistant/` folder — investigated rather than assumed, and found a real `uvicorn` dev server has been running against this checkout's actual `brain/` folder since earlier in the session (via the `run` skill), auto-reloading on every save. That means Home Assistant's m016/m017 migrations had genuinely executed against real local data, not just pytest fixtures — and m017's `USERS/*.iterdir()` folder-rename loop had a real bug: it treated `_template/` (the setup scaffold, an ordinary directory) as if it were a real user, incorrectly "migrating" it. Fixed by excluding `_`-prefixed names from that loop, matching journal's own `on_install()` convention (which already excludes them, for the same reason) — and cleaned up the already-mis-renamed template folder by removing it entirely, matching journal's own no-scaffold-needed precedent, since favourites are lazily created either way.
  **How to apply**: when the owner says "leave backend names alone, just change forward-facing ones," that's a real, narrower scope than a full id rename — resist defaulting to the bigger pattern just because the last module got the bigger treatment; match what was actually asked. When a schema (like manifest.js's block config) was designed against N=1 real example, a second real example is the moment to check whether it actually generalizes, not just extend it as a special case. An unexpected `git status` diff during unrelated work is worth investigating immediately, not dismissing — it can reveal that supposedly-isolated pytest-only work has actually been exercised for real by something else running concurrently.

- **2026-08-24 (seventh) — Before writing a migration that mutates data validated against a live in-process registry, verify the actual boot-order dependency directly rather than assume the safe-but-uncertain path; a stored `type`/tool-name string needs the exact same rename-migration treatment as a stored id, discovered by grepping the old string across the WHOLE codebase, not just the module being renamed.** Owner asked to finish the id-consistency pass from the sixth entry below — rename the four things deliberately left alone last round (frontend route, help-section anchor id, the 4 AI tool names, the `home_favourites` dashboard block type) — "take extra care that they are done correctly... i want it all to match."
  **Route (`/home` → `/home-assistant`) and help anchor (`home` → `home-assistant`)**: checked this app's own naming convention before picking one rather than guessing — existing multi-word routes (`/settings/admin/mod-store`, `/settings/admin/contact-fields`) and the one existing multi-word help-section id (`"getting-started"`) both hyphenate, not underscore. Matched that, not the module id's own snake_case.
  **AI tool names** (`get_home_state`→`get_home_assistant_state`, `control_home_device`→`control_home_assistant_device`, `trigger_home_automation`→`trigger_home_assistant_automation`; `activate_scene` untouched, it never said "home"): no migration needed — tool schemas are sent to the LLM fresh every request, never persisted as a live lookup key anywhere. The one edge case (a chat paused mid-tool-approval at the exact deploy moment) is a risk any code deploy already carries, not something this rename adds.
  **Dashboard block type** (`home_favourites`→`home_assistant_favourites`) is the one genuinely data-risky piece, same category as last round's Brain-folder rename: `block.type` is persisted in every saved dashboard/template, and `dashboards_service._validate_blocks()` raises on any type not in the live `dashboard_blocks` REGISTRY. Rather than assume REGISTRY might not be populated yet at migration-run time (migrations run before `register_routers()`), **verified the actual boot order directly**: `main.py`'s own top-level `from routers import ... dashboards` transitively imports `dashboard_blocks/render.py`, whose module-level `_load_all_resolvers()` call is what populates REGISTRY — and that import happens while Python is still loading `main.py`, before FastAPI's `lifespan()` (hence before `run_migrations()`) is ever reached. So REGISTRY genuinely is populated by migration time today, and the validated `update_dashboard()` path would have worked. Chose the more robust option anyway — new migration `m018` uses raw `read_json`/`write_json` throughout, with zero dependency on that import chain staying in this order — matching `m016`/`m017`'s own style. Swept every real per-user dashboard (both workspaces), both pool dashboards (`_household`/`_team`, via `dashboards_service._all_stores()`), every per-user template, and the global admin-curated template store.
  **A second bug found only by grepping the OLD type string across the entire codebase, not just the module's own directory**: `migrations/runner.py`'s `m010_seed_home_dashboards` — a *core* migration, predating the Mod Store system entirely, that seeds every brand-new user's first default dashboard — hardcodes `"type": "home_favourites"` in its own seed data. Already applied (and tracked as such) on any existing instance, so harmless there, but would have silently seeded an unresolvable block for every genuinely fresh install going forward if left unfixed. One-line fix, zero risk to existing instances since that function body only ever runs once per instance, already spent for anyone upgrading.
  **How to apply**: (1) When a module's id/type/name is renamed, the search for "what else references the old string" must cover the WHOLE codebase, not just the module's own package — the real second bug here (m010) lived in core, nowhere near `module_packages/home_assistant/`. (2) Before writing a migration that must pass validation against a live in-process registry/cache, verify — don't assume — whether that registry is actually populated by the time migrations run; if verifying shows it's genuinely uncertain or order-dependent, prefer the raw-storage-layer write that doesn't need the registry at all, the same conclusion this entry reaches even after confirming the registry-timing risk didn't actually exist today, because it's more robust to a future refactor changing that order.

- **2026-08-24 (sixth) — Renaming an already-shipped module's id is a real data migration, not a find-and-replace; a migration's own tracking key must survive the rename unchanged even when the module around it renames.** Owner asked to rename the module's *internal* id too (`home` → `home_assistant`, not just the display name from the fifth entry below), explicitly accepting that it might need migrations. One clarifying question asked first, since it was the one piece with genuine data risk rather than pure code: should the on-disk Brain folder (`USERS/{name}/Home/`) rename too, given that moves real files on the live instance rather than just renaming a JSON key? Recommended against (nobody sees a raw Brain folder name; zero benefit for real risk) — owner chose to rename it anyway, an explicit, informed call on their own data, respected and implemented carefully rather than re-litigated.
  **What renamed**: module id, both `module_packages/home/` directories, the API router prefix (`/api/v1/home` → `/api/v1/home_assistant`, every frontend `api.js` call site with it), `owned_brain_paths`, the Mod Store catalog entry, `features_service.py`'s hand-authored `_BUSINESS_MEMBER` entry. Two real bugs caught only by grepping the renamed directory for any leftover hardcoded `"home"` string rather than assuming the rename was complete: `router.py`'s `require_module("home")` and `dashboard_block.py`'s `BlockSpec(module="home")` — both are access-control/gating checks that would have silently stopped matching anything after the rename, quietly widening access (a disabled/uninstalled state would no longer correctly gate the router or the block) rather than throwing an error.
  **What deliberately did NOT rename, and why that's not an inconsistency**: the frontend route (`/home` — a materially more user-visible thing, a URL people navigate to/bookmark, than an internal id), the help section's own anchor id, the 4 AI tool names (`get_home_state` etc. — descriptive names for a "smart home" concept, not literally the module's id), the `home_favourites` dashboard block type. None of these *are* the module's id; renaming them would add real blast radius (routes, saved dashboard block configs, chat tool-calling names an AI has already seen in a conversation) for no benefit the owner actually asked for.
  **The migration (`m017`), and the trap avoided**: four independently-idempotent steps — rename the `"home"` key inside `installed_modules.json`, inside every role's map in `features.json`, inside every user's `disabled_modules` in `auth.json` (both the flat-list and workspace-keyed-dict shapes), and the real `USERS/{name}/Home/` folder via `Path.rename()` (atomic, not copy-then-delete) for every user who has one. Critically, **m016's own migration key was kept exactly as `"home:m016_..."`**, unchanged, even though it now lives inside the renamed `home_assistant/manifest.py` and its body now calls `mark_installed("home_assistant", ...)`. Confirmed first via `module_registry.py`'s collision check that a migration key is only ever checked for uniqueness, never for matching the module's current id. Renaming m016's key too would have made it look unapplied on the owner's real instance (which already ran it once), causing a silent re-run on next boot — and since m016's own logic re-checks `ha_config.json` and calls `mark_installed()` if configured, that re-run could have **silently re-installed a module the owner had explicitly uninstalled since**, with no error or visible signal that it happened.
  **How to apply**: renaming any already-shipped module's id is not a text find-and-replace — audit every place the OLD id string is used as data (installed-state files, role maps, per-user overrides, real Brain folders) versus as code (manifest fields, gate calls), and write a real carry-forward migration for the former. Before renaming ANY migration's own tracking key, ask whether that migration may have already run on a real instance — if the risk is "it silently redoes something a human explicitly undid since," keep the key and only change what the function does when it (rarely) runs fresh. After any id-touching rename, grep the whole renamed unit for the literal old string one more time — don't trust "I already changed the manifest" to mean every consumer picked it up; `require_module()`/`BlockSpec(module=...)` calls are exactly the kind of gate that fails silently-open rather than loudly when the id it's checking stops matching anything.

- **2026-08-24 (fifth) — Home Assistant's config form relocated from `Household.jsx` to `Hosting.jsx` (following an existing precedent, n8n's config, not a new one); module renamed "Home Assistant"; real logo rejected for a trademark + missing-feature reason, not just aesthetics.** Owner, after clicking through increment 2, proposed moving the HA config form out of Household.jsx (a different, not-yet-converted module's page) since it was flagged as increment 2's one real complication — asked "does that resolve all the issues?" Researched before answering: moving the *frontend* form does resolve the Household.jsx entanglement, but does **not** change why `services/ha_service.py` stays in core (that's `agent_service.py` needing `is_configured()` directly — a backend consumer, unrelated to frontend placement). Presented that distinction, then asked (AskUserQuestion, per explicit instruction to research first) where the form should live. Owner's answer, a third option I hadn't proposed: keep the rest of the module self-contained, but put the config form specifically in **Admin → Hosting**.
  **Why this checks out, not just "sure, why not"**: `Hosting.jsx` already hosts n8n's own connection config (URL + API key) even though Automations hasn't converted either — a real, already-shipped precedent for "an external integration's connection settings live on the Hosting page regardless of whether its owning module has converted yet," not a new pattern invented just for this. `HomeAssistantSection` (renamed from `SmartHomeSection`) + its installed-state gate moved there wholesale; `Household.jsx` now has zero Home Assistant references. This is the *first* section on that page gated on a module's installed state (n8n's isn't, since Automations hasn't converted) — a working template for that conversion whenever it happens.
  **Rename**: display name → "Home Assistant" everywhere a user sees it (nav, Mod Store catalog, dashboard block picker, `help_section`, admin menu subtitles). `id: "home"` deliberately unchanged — it's what disabled-modules lists/role maps/`owned_brain_paths` already reference; renaming it would be a real breaking change for zero user-facing benefit, since the id is never displayed.
  **Logo, declined with reasons, not just "no"**: owner asked about using Home Assistant's actual logo. Two independent problems, not one: (1) it's a registered trademark (Open Home Foundation) — using it in a commercial product without checking their brand-usage terms risks implying an official partnership that doesn't exist; (2) the Mod Store catalog has zero image-asset support today — `entry.icon` renders as plain text everywhere (row, nav, catalog) — so a real logo isn't a field swap, it's a new rendering mechanism across several files. Owner agreed to keep the existing emoji (💡) once both points were laid out.
  **How to apply**: when a not-yet-converted module's admin page already hosts a DIFFERENT external integration's connection config (Hosting.jsx/n8n is the one example so far), that's real precedent for putting a newly-converting module's own external-service config there too, instead of defaulting to "give every module its own admin page" or leaving it entangled with whatever page happened to have it first. Check for that precedent before proposing a brand-new page.

- **2026-08-24 (fourth) — Home converted into `module_packages/home/` — Mod Store increment 2 complete. A shared service consumed outside a module's own frontend/backend should stay in core, not move with the rest of the module.** Moved (not copied): `routers/home.py` → `module_packages/home/backend/router.py` (bundled together as-is, including the admin `/status`+`/config` endpoints — see below for why), `services/dashboard_blocks/_home.py` → `.../backend/dashboard_block.py` (now `module="home"`), its 4 agent tool schemas + executor cases → `.../backend/agent_tools.py` (`TOOL_SCHEMAS` + `execute()`), its `content/help.json` section → `manifest.help_section`, `pages/Home.jsx` → `module_packages/home/frontend/Home.jsx`, `HomeFavouritesBlock` out of `components/dashboard/blocks.jsx` → its own file (default export, `Empty` helper inlined rather than exported from core just for this one caller), the `home` API client out of `lib/api.js` → `module_packages/home/frontend/api.js`. Deleted from core: 2 `main.py` registration lines, `"home"` from `features_service._CORE_MODULE_IDS`, `_home` from `dashboard_blocks/registry.py`'s hardcoded resolver tuple, home's `content/help.json` section, `home` from `constants.js`/`deepLinks.js`/`lib/api.js`/`App.jsx`.
  **Real complication journal didn't have, found during this increment's own verification pass (per the plan's own "each increment starts fresh" rule)**: Home's admin config form (HA URL/token) lives inside `pages/settings/admin/Household.jsx` — a *different*, not-yet-converted module's page, not a dedicated Home admin page (despite `content/help.json`'s old text claiming "Admin → Smart Home"; corrected to "Admin → Household" in both the moved help text and `Home.jsx`'s own in-app error messages). Two deliberate decisions this forced:
  1. **`services/ha_service.py` stayed in core** rather than moving to `module_packages/home/backend/service.py` like journal's service did. Unlike `journal_service.py` (exactly one consumer: journal's own router + tools), `ha_service.py` has two consumers outside "Home's own control surface": `Household.jsx`'s config form and `agent_service.py`'s `_get_tools()` ha-configured gate. Moving it would force either core (`agent_service.py`) importing from a module — the exact pattern journal's conversion established as wrong (its `journal_service` import was deleted outright, not redirected to the new path) — or inventing a new generic manifest mechanism for one module's special case. Keeping it in core sidesteps both: modules importing core services is normal and expected; the reverse direction is what's avoided.
  2. **`Household.jsx`'s Smart Home section is now gated on Home's installed state** (`SmartHomeNotInstalled` fallback when `disabledModules.includes('home')` or, per `ModuleRoute`'s exact same stacked-check pattern, installed-but-not-yet-active), even though Household itself hasn't converted. A page belonging to one module rendering a section owned by a different, converted module now needs to check that module's install state directly — a new but small, consistent extension of the same "installed" gate every converted module's own route already needs.
  **`m016`'s migration guard deliberately differs from journal's `m015`**: keyed on `brain/_system/ha_config.json`'s existence *and* a real, non-blank url+token (not journal's blanket "the instance existed before, so it had the feature" signal) — Home was always opt-in before this system existed, so only instances that had *actually connected* HA get auto-installed on upgrade; an instance that never configured it correctly starts uninstalled. `_get_tools()`'s HA-configured check (`ha_ok`) now applies as a final filter pass over the *combined* core+module tool list, rather than only filtering `_USER_TOOLS` — installed and configured are two independent gates, and a module tool schema needs the same configured-check a hardcoded one used to get.
  **A real, easy-to-miss asymmetry preserved, not carried by the generic mechanism**: `get_home_state` is safe to run without approval (`_READ_TOOLS`) but deliberately excluded from the narrower research-mode tool set (`_RESEARCH_TOOLS`) — predates this conversion, reason not documented at the time it was added. The generic `ModuleManifest.read_only_agent_tools` union (built for journal) would have added it to *both* sets uniformly, silently widening research mode. Left `read_only_agent_tools=[]` on Home's manifest and kept `_READ_TOOLS = _RESEARCH_TOOLS | {"get_home_state", "ask_user_question"}` as a hardcoded name in `agent_service.py`, unchanged — it works identically regardless of which list a tool's schema originates from, since these sets only ever check tool *names*.
  **`features_service._BUSINESS_MEMBER` needed a comment, not a removal**: unlike `_PERSONAL_MEMBER` (derived via comprehension over `_CORE_MODULE_IDS`, so home dropped out automatically once removed from that list), `_BUSINESS_MEMBER` is hand-authored, and `load_features()` defaults any module *missing* from a role map to `True`. Home's `"home": False` entry had to stay explicit there (with a comment explaining why) or a fresh business-profile instance would silently default Smart Home to enabled.
  **Verified**: full backend suite green (857 passed = 847 + 10 new: `test_home_module_conversion.py`'s 4 migration/round-trip tests, `test_home_agent_tools.py`'s 6 covering the double-gate and the research-mode asymmetry specifically), `npm run build`/`eslint --max-warnings 0` clean (also caught and fixed 2 pre-existing unescaped-apostrophe lint errors in `ModStore.jsx`/`Household.jsx` that had never been linted before), genuine code-splitting confirmed (`Home-*.js`/`HomeFavouritesBlock-*.js` as separate chunks). `git grep "home"` outside `module_packages/home/` turns up only the deliberate, commented exceptions above — same "zero exceptions" bar journal's conversion set.

- **2026-08-24 (third) — "Correct after a full page reload, broken on the in-place React update" is the signature of a stale `backdrop-blur` compositing layer; the mechanism-agnostic fix is to key the element so React remounts it instead of mutating it in place.** Third real bug in the same Mod Store admin UI session, again only found by the owner clicking through their real instance: switching a row between install/uninstall/busy states left the badge/button text visually "cut short" or with its "bottom... cut off," fixed only by a hard page reload. This is this app's *third* known `backdrop-filter` rendering bug (see the 2026-08-18 popover-clipping entry above on `ContactRow`, and a `position:fixed` containing-block bug on `assetDisplay.jsx`'s lightbox) — `.card` (`bg-.../80 backdrop-blur-sm border ...`) is used on every per-row element in `ModStore.jsx`, and its row never unmounts across a state transition (`key={entry.id}` stayed constant through every state), so React mutates the same DOM node — and its GPU compositing layer — in place every time.
  **Why:** A fresh DOM mount always forces the browser to paint a brand-new compositing layer from scratch; an in-place mutation of an existing `backdrop-filter` element's content/size is what triggers stale-layer bugs on some mobile WebKit/Chromium builds. "Works on reload, breaks on live update" is close to a diagnostic signature for this class of bug specifically — a pure layout bug (wrong flexbox/width math) would be wrong on reload too.
  **How to apply:** Don't reach for this as a first resort — the two earlier `backdrop-filter` bugs in this app were each fixed by identifying and correcting the actual specific mechanism (stacking-context promotion via `relative z-*`; a `createPortal` to escape a containing block), which is the more precise, lower-blast-radius fix when the mechanism is knowable. Reach for keying-to-force-remount specifically when: the element uses `.card`/backdrop-blur (or similar `backdrop-filter`/`filter`/`transform` layer-promoting property), its content/shape changes via React state while the same key persists across the transition, and the reported symptom matches "fine on reload, broken live" — in that situation, a remount is a legitimate, low-cost, mechanism-agnostic fix (`key={`${id}:${...anything that changes the row's visual shape}`}`), not a hack to be embarrassed about.

- **2026-08-24 (second) — A UI that triggers its own backend's restart must poll until the restart actually lands, not fetch once right after the request returns; a status badge sharing a flex-wrap line with a title can visually clip on mobile instead of just wrapping.** Continued real-instance testing of the Mod Store admin UI (same session as the restart-race fix below) surfaced two more bugs. (1) After the restart-race fix deferred the actual container restart by ~2s plus real boot time, `ModStore.jsx`'s `doRestart()` still called `load()` exactly once, immediately — so it always re-showed the same pending state, and the "Restart Now" banner never disappeared until the admin manually left and reopened the page (worse after the crash fix than before it, since the deferral lengthened the stale window). (2) The row's badge lived inline with the name/version in one `flex items-center gap-2 flex-wrap` line — on a narrow mobile viewport a long badge label could wrap onto its own line in a way that read as "pushed down and overflow hidden" rather than a clean second line.
  **Why:** Any endpoint that restarts its own backend process is inherently async from the caller's perspective no matter how the backend defers the kill signal — the response returning "ok" only means the restart was *scheduled*, never that it *happened*. A UI that treats that response as "done" will always show stale state for however long the real restart takes. Separately, `flex-wrap` is the wrong tool for "this element must never wrap, but something in this row might be too long" — it lets the *wrong* element (the fixed-shape badge) be the one that moves, when the variable-length element (the title) is the one that should give way.
  **How to apply:** After triggering a self-restart, poll the state that's supposed to change (here: `catalog()`+`active()` on a 2s interval, 60s deadline) until it actually reflects the change, keeping the trigger button disabled for the *entire* poll window — not just the initial request — so a second click can't land mid-restart. Treat fetch failures during the poll as expected (the container really is briefly unreachable between the old process dying and the new one answering), not as an error to surface. For a row with one fixed-shape element (a badge, an icon, a count) and one variable-length element (a title, a description) that must share a line on narrow viewports: put the fixed element in its own `shrink-0` position via `justify-between`, and let the variable element `truncate`/shrink instead — never `flex-wrap` both into the same line and hope the short one doesn't wrap.

- **2026-08-24 — A self-restart endpoint must never call the actual restart synchronously in the request handler.** Owner report from the real production-ish instance: uninstalled journal (worked), reinstalled it, got an error, and the whole app appeared to crash ("said check logs"). Root cause, found by re-reading `routers/mod_store.py`'s `POST /mod-store/restart` (not reproducible in this sandbox — no real Docker/socket-proxy here): the handler called `docker_sdk.from_env().containers.get("logcore-app").restart()` inline, then returned a response — but that call restarts the very container this request is running in, so Docker can SIGTERM the process before the HTTP response finishes reaching the browser, even on a fully normal restart. That reads exactly like "returned error, then the app crashed."
  **Why:** Restarting a *sibling* container (e.g. `n8n_service.py`'s `restart_n8n()`, the precedent this endpoint was modeled on) is safe to call inline — nothing about the caller's own process is at risk. Restarting *your own* container from inside its own request handler is categorically different: the kill signal can land mid-response, and this specific risk was never exercised because no environment used this session had a real Docker daemon to test the restart call against — it was verified only as a 502 (no container present), never as a real, successful restart.
  **How to apply:** Fixed by keeping the Docker connectivity/container-lookup check synchronous (`containers.get(...)` — a real config/permission failure still 502s immediately, so error visibility isn't lost) but deferring the actual `.restart()` call into a FastAPI `BackgroundTasks` job with a short `time.sleep()` first — Starlette only runs background tasks after the response has been sent. Regression test: `tests/test_mod_store_router.py::test_restart_defers_container_restart_to_a_background_task` asserts the fake container's `.restart()` was **not** called by the time `restart()` returns, only after the deferred task itself is invoked — this is the exact shape the old, buggy synchronous version would have failed. Any future endpoint that restarts the app's own container (not a sibling service) needs this same synchronous-check-then-deferred-kill split. Also fixed alongside this: `pages/settings/admin/ModStore.jsx`'s `pending_install`/`pending_uninstall` badge labels were long unbroken strings inside a `flex-wrap` row with no `whitespace-nowrap`, which the owner saw as a "glitched/half-hidden" badge — shortened both labels and pinned them to one line.

- **2026-08-18 (second) — Ten-item real-usage bug report on the seven-item pass below, dominated by one tightly-interconnected Dashboard workspace/loading cluster; a background Explore agent researched that cluster while the other six independent items were fixed directly.** Owner tested the shipped work live and sent 10 items in one message: (1) confirm dashboards are already correctly hidden from the picker by workspace — they were, no bug, purely a question; (2) dashboards need a toggle for "this workspace only" vs. "both," even for a user entitled to both; (3) the Dashboard module shows "Dashboard not found" on every first open, never on subsequent picks; (4) switching workspaces doesn't reload/clear the dashboard from the other one; (5) add an auto "last opened dashboard" restore within a time window the owner explicitly delegated ("or make it longer than 1 minute... whichever you think is best"); (6) the Core Values pill input auto-zooms on mobile tap; (7) dashboard block buttons *still* don't stick right, re-reported after the fix in the entry below; (8) the Assets blank-custom-field select-options pill input has the same zoom bug as (6); (9) a blank asset's typed custom fields don't display outside edit mode at all; (10) desktop needs the same module-pinning shortcut mechanism mobile already has. Items 1-5 are one interconnected feature (Dashboards' workspace-visibility/loading model) and were handed to a background Explore agent (~15 min) before any code was touched, while items 6-10 were independent enough to fix directly from direct code reads. The research surfaced two things beyond the literal report: item 3's exact deterministic root cause (a `useEffect` depending on `[workspace, idParam]` while also calling `setSearchParams` inside itself — self-mutating a dependency guarantees a double-fire), and a genuine, independently-reachable backend bug never mentioned by the owner (pool dashboard creation for `_team` wrote to a storage path no read path ever checks, so a team dashboard created via the AI chat tool vanished immediately — same bug class Assets had already fixed for its own pool-creation branch, now fixed here in both the router and the agent tool handler). Design choices made and explicitly recorded rather than silently decided: the new `cross_workspace` toggle mirrors Contacts' own mechanism exactly (including the same native-workspace-not-viewer's-ambient-workspace `resolve_access()` deviation) but is gated on `edit` access specifically — stronger than the `contribute` tier the rest of `PATCH /dashboards/{id}` uses, matching how every other sharing/visibility change in this module is edit-gated as categorically different from block-content edits; 30 minutes was chosen for the last-opened window (owner delegated the exact number) reasoning that it's long enough to survive a normal round-trip through other modules but short enough that "why am I back on an old dashboard" stays rare. Items 6-9's fixes each independently caught a second, un-reported instance of the same bug class while looking for the first: the mobile-zoom fix (6/8) is one shared component (`TagInput.jsx`) covering both reports at once, plus two more instances found by checking for the same pattern elsewhere (`PriorityList.jsx`, `TemplateManager.jsx`); item 7's "still broken" report turned out to be a genuinely different bug from the one fixed in the entry below (a flex item without its own `flex-1` sizing to content instead of stretching, so `ml-auto` had nothing to push against), not a failed fix; item 9 was a real regression from the entry below's own blank-asset-custom-fields work — the edit flow was fully wired, the read view was never updated to look anywhere but the (always-empty, for a blank asset) template fields.
- **2026-08-18 — Seven-item polish pass across Assets, Contacts, and Custom Dashboards, planned as one batch.** Owner sent 7 items in one message: (1) blank-asset custom fields need the same typed picker Templates use, plus a "convert to template" button; (2) the last-seen popover is partially hidden behind the contact below it; (3) dashboard block buttons don't consistently stick to the right; (4) Core Values should be pills, not a comma-split field; (5) career history needs to allow inserting a past role with real dates, not just archive-current-add-new; (6) self-contact sections (Values & Principles, Family, Career, Address, Personal, Priorities) need an owner-only "hide from others" toggle per section; (7) dashboard blocks need per-instance show-card/show-header toggles, default on, plus a note telling the agent it can set them. Three parallel Explore passes read every relevant file in full (Assets templating, Contacts sections/popover/career-history, Dashboard block buttons/chrome) before any design decision — grounded in exact file/line reads, same research-first discipline as every other multi-item pass this project has taken.
  **Item 1 — typed custom fields + "Save as template"**: `_validate_fields()` gained an optional `custom_defs` param — when a blank asset has `custom_field_defs` (same shape a Template's own `fields` use, validated by the existing `_validate_field_defs()`), a matching key gets real `_validate_value()` type-checking instead of the old freeform-string-only path; an undefined key still falls back to freeform, since the frontend defines a field's shape and fills its value in the same row, not two separate phases. New `assets_service.attach_template()` + `POST /assets/{id}/attach-template` (self-service, edit-access) is a deliberate, narrow exception to `template_id` otherwise being immutable post-creation — reachable only through the explicit "Save as template" action, which first calls the *existing*, unmodified `POST /assets/templates` to create the template from the asset's own defs, then attaches it back. **Deviation from the plan's own stated approach**: the plan called for extracting a shared `FieldDefRow` component reused unchanged by `TemplateManager.jsx`; implementation found the two contexts' key-handling genuinely differs (TemplateManager exposes a raw technical key input, since a Template's key is a stable identifier a technical admin author types deliberately; a blank asset's field key is purely internal storage for one asset, auto-slugified from the label instead — exposing a second technical input there would be pure friction) and the row layouts don't share cleanly across that difference. Kept the FIELD_TYPES list and the backend validators shared (genuinely reusable); left the frontend markup separate rather than forcing an abstraction that didn't fit both layouts. `TemplateManager.jsx` itself was not touched.
  **Item 2 — presence popover clipping**: root cause was `.card`'s `backdrop-blur-sm` creating a new stacking context on every `ContactRow` — the popover's own `z-50` only ever won comparisons inside its own row, so the next sibling row (a later, un-elevated stacking context) painted over it regardless. Fixed by having `ContactAvatar` fire an optional `onPopoverToggle` callback, which `ContactRow` uses to add `relative z-20` to its own row while its popover is open — promoting that row's whole stacking context above its neighbors. Deliberately not a portal (no existing precedent in this codebase; `EmojiPicker`/`BlockPicker`'s swatch popover are both plain absolute-positioned, same pattern extended here rather than introduced fresh).
