  **Item 3 — block button alignment**: `BlockActionButtons` itself had zero alignment; each of 9 call sites was independently responsible, and 2 (`CollectionBlock`'s Kanban view, `NoteEmbedBlock`) didn't. Fixed at the source — `ml-auto` on `BlockActionButtons`'s own wrapper, self-sufficient in any flex row regardless of whatever alignment trick (if any) the row already has (confirmed harmless-redundant, not conflicting, where `justify-between`/a `flex-1` sibling already worked). `NoteEmbedBlock` needed one extra wrap (`flex justify-end`) since its parent is `flex-col`, where `ml-auto` only affects the cross axis.
  **Item 4 — Core Values pills**: `core_values` moved from `_BASIC_LONG_FIELDS`'s generic string-capping loop into its own `_validate_core_values()` (list, trimmed/deduped/capped 30×60 chars) — also accepts a raw comma-separated string as input, since the AI's `update_contact`/`update_profile` tools call `update_contact()` directly with a raw fields dict, bypassing `ContactUpdate`'s Pydantic `list[str]` type entirely; without that fallback a string would silently iterate into individual characters (`for v in values` on a `str`). `key_constraints` has an identical old implementation but was deliberately left untouched — only `core_values` was named by the owner. New migration `m014_core_values_to_list` (idempotent, every real user's personal+business store plus both pools) converts existing string data — caught its own dedup bug via the test written for it (naive comma-split without a "already in the list" check let `"Growth, Growth"` produce two entries), fixed before it shipped. `ContactDetail.jsx`'s `ProfileSection` helper was generalized to render an array value as pills instead of flat text (not special-cased just for this field — any future list-shaped profile field gets the same treatment for free).
  **Item 5 — career history past roles**: confirmed entirely a frontend gap — `_validate_career_history()` already accepted any well-formed entry independently and never enforced "exactly one current entry" (its own docstring already says so). Added a second inline form (`editingPast` state) for adding/editing a past entry directly with explicit `start_date`/`end_date`, alongside the existing "archive current" quick action. Past entries now display sorted by `start_date` descending, in both the editor and the read view — pure display-time sort, no backend change.
  **Item 6 — owner-only section hiding**: a genuinely new mechanism, `hidden_sections: list[str]`, deliberately distinct from the fixed `_PRIVATE_FIELDS` set — `_HIDEABLE_SECTIONS` maps 6 section keys (`values_principles`/`family`/`career`/`address`/`personal`/`priorities`) to field sets, company-only sections excluded since a self-contact is permanently `type: "person"`. `_strip_hidden_sections()` mirrors `_strip_private()`'s exact owner-short-circuit shape and is called right after it in both `annotate()` and `find_contact()` (same defense-in-depth reasoning already established there). Write-side: `hidden_sections` rides along in `update_contact()`'s existing third-party-viewer stripping branch — only the record's own owner can ever change it; no separate field-level write guard was needed beyond that, since self-contacts already cap every non-owner at `contribute`, and `contribute` on Contacts never permits core-field edits regardless. "Address" had no dedicated heading before this (inline among always-shown fields for every contact) — gained a small new isSelf-only one purely to give its toggle somewhere to live; every other contact's address field is completely untouched.
  **Item 7 — block chrome toggles**: `show_card`/`show_header`, same `sort_mode`-style lifecycle (declared once, mirrored frontend/backend, read via `.get()`/`!== false`). **Explicit, deliberate default-appearance change, not a narrow opt-in, stated for the record because it was flagged as a real judgment call before implementation began (and the plan was approved with this flagged)**: unset reads as `true` unconditionally, not "whatever `shape` already decided" — so every existing `shape:'list'` block starts showing a card, and every block's header starts showing in view mode too, the instant this ships, across every dashboard, with no per-block migration. `show_header` only ever governs the *view-mode* header — edit mode keeps showing it unconditionally, since that's the only way to reach a block's ✎/✕ controls. Chromeless types (`nav_button`/`status_button`) never gain either field — a stronger, non-overridable guarantee. Introduced this app's first `'boolean'` `BlockPicker.jsx` field kind. Backend has no `chromeless` concept at all (confirmed: `BlockSpec` carries zero chrome-related fields — chrome is entirely frontend), so `agent_schemas.py` hardcodes the same two-type chromeless set the frontend's registry declares, mirroring rather than sharing. `get_dashboard_block_catalog`'s existing config_fields merge now goes through a shared `getConfigFields()`/`get_config_fields()` helper on both sides instead of raw dict lookups — avoids repeating the two chrome fields into ~20 individual schema array literals by hand, and makes every non-chromeless type "configurable" (gets a ✎ affordance) even ones with no other config at all, like `streaks`, which is new and correct, not a bug.
  **Verified**: full backend suite green (766 passed, up from 750 — one unrelated, pre-existing failure flagged separately below, not caused by this pass), `black`/`isort` clean, frontend `lint`/`build` clean. New tests: Assets (`custom_field_defs` type-checking + rejection, `attach_template` success/already-templated/unknown-template), Contacts (`core_values` list validation + legacy-string fallback, `hidden_sections` strip-for-non-owner/visible-to-owner, rejects-unknown-key, write-guard strips for a non-owner viewer, the household-vs-team parametrized must-pass pair matching the existing `_PRIVATE_FIELDS` test's own shape, `m014` migration including the dedup bug caught by its own test), Dashboards (`get_dashboard_block_catalog`'s merged config_fields — an existing test hardcoding the old pre-chrome-fields shape needed updating, expected churn). Items 2/3/5 are UI-only (or, for 5, entirely frontend) with no automated coverage — this codebase's standing convention, code-review-level verification, flagged for the owner's own click-through.
  **Pre-existing, unrelated test failure found during this pass, not fixed (out of scope)**: `test_finance_transfers.py::test_transfer_leg_never_false_matches_a_recurring_bill` fails on its own, confirmed unrelated to any of these 7 items (Finance/recurring-bills, nothing this pass touched) — hardcodes `next_due: "2026-08-14"` and checks a 30-day `upcoming_recurring()` window; now that real wall-clock time has passed that date, the bill likely falls out of the "upcoming" bucket the test expects it in. A real, currently-broken test, but not this pass's to fix.

- **2026-08-17 (fifth) — Convert a personal contact to pool, single + bulk.** Owner: "i dont see a button or anything that can convert personal contacts to pool ones? also would like a way to bulk convert since i have so many." No new transfer mechanism was needed — `contacts_service.transfer_ownership(store_user, workspace, contact_id, *, new_owner, by)` already existed and already did exactly this (move a contact + its interactions/deals between stores, converting `shared_with` to `contributors` when the destination is a pool), just previously only ever called from `user_deletion_service.py`. Reused it directly for both new endpoints: `POST /contacts/{id}/convert` (single, self-service, `edit`-gated) and `POST /contacts/convert-bulk` (`{contact_ids?}`, defaults to everything eligible when omitted, scoped to the caller's own personal non-self contacts, per-item try/except so one bad id doesn't fail the whole batch — returns `{converted, skipped}`).
  **Deliberate policy choice, stated for the record**: self-service (`edit` access), not admin-only — Assets' own convert-to-pool action IS admin-gated, and this was a conscious departure, not an oversight. Reasoned as consistent with the already-shipped precedent that pool-contact *creation* is open to any contacts-module user (2026-08-17, earlier entry below) — if a user can already put a brand-new contact straight into the pool, gating the "move an existing one there" path behind admin made the two paths inconsistent for no real security reason (both land in the same place: a pool contact the whole household/team can see).
  **Caught before shipping**: the single-convert endpoint's first draft returned `{"ok": True}`. `ContactModal.jsx`'s save flow (`onSaved={saved => { setModal(null); load(); if (detail) setDetail(saved) }}`) expects a real annotated contact back — a bare ok-object would have set the open detail panel's state to garbage. Fixed to return `contacts_service.annotate(moved, pool_target, current_user["name"], "edit")`, matching every other mutation endpoint's response shape.
  **Verified**: full backend suite green (750 passed), `black`/`isort` clean (one `black` reformat needed on the bulk endpoint's line wrapping), frontend `lint`/`build` clean. No new automated test coverage for the two router endpoints themselves — consistent with this codebase's standing convention (confirmed again: zero `TestClient`/RTL usage anywhere in `tests/`, router-level and UI-only logic is verified by code review + lint/build, not fabricated test coverage) — flagged for the owner's own click-through, same as every other router/UI-only change this session.

- **2026-08-17 (fourth) — The presence-dot fix from the round below didn't actually fix it: found the real cause (Contacts had no refresh mechanism at all), plus a "last seen" tracker the owner asked for while diagnosing it.** Owner, after a few minutes on the app: "the presence dot is still red." Investigated the whole ping chain end to end before touching anything — `main.py`'s router registration, `routers/presence.py`'s endpoint, `Layout.jsx`'s ping effect, `lib/api.js`'s export — all correct, nothing wrong in any of them. The actual cause: `Contacts.jsx` had zero refresh mechanism of any kind (`useEffect(() => { load() }, [load])` only re-runs on a workspace switch or the archived-toggle, never on a timer) — unlike Dashboard, which already got a 45s poll-while-visible fix earlier the same day for the identical class of staleness. The ping itself was working correctly the whole time; the already-rendered page just never asked the server again to find out.
  **Fix**: mirrored Dashboard's own pattern — `Contacts.jsx` now polls `load()` every 60s while the tab is visible. This alone wasn't sufficient, though: `load()` only ever updated `items` (the list), never the separately-held `detail` state (the open contact-detail panel) — so a contact left open in the detail view would keep refreshing the LIST behind it while the detail view itself stayed frozen. `load()` now also re-syncs `detail` from the same fresh fetch when one's open.
  **"Last seen" tracker, added while diagnosing** (owner ask, mid-investigation: "would be good to add a tracker to track the last online status... display that on the contact when opened... when they click on the red dot it pops up a small tag right below it"): `presence_service.last_seen_iso(user_name)` exposes the raw ISO timestamp (not a pre-formatted label — the frontend already has the viewer's own locale context, no reason to compute display formatting server-side); `contacts_service.annotate()` adds it as `_last_seen` alongside `_online`, same access-gating as everything else `annotate()` touches. Owner's exact formatting spec, given as a follow-up clarification: count by the minute for the first hour, switch to hours-only up to a day, then days-only forever after that — no upper cap, never falls back to an absolute date. `ContactAvatar.jsx` shows a small popover with the formatted label on click (same button-toggles-a-popover shape as `EmojiPicker.jsx`/`BlockPicker.jsx`'s color swatch picker) — **had to use a `<span role="button">`, not a real `<button>`**, since `Contacts.jsx`'s `ContactRow` renders the whole row as a `<button>` and a `<button>` cannot validly nest inside another `<button>`; caught before shipping by actually reading `ContactRow`'s markup, not assumed. **Click target moved from the dot to the photo/icon itself same day** (owner follow-up: "the dot is too small") — the dot (`w-2.5 h-2.5`, 10px) is a poor touch target; the click now lands on the whole avatar (`pointer-events-none` on the now-purely-decorative dot so it never intercepts), while the dot still shows the color at a glance. The popover still opens from the dot's own corner position, just triggered by the bigger element around it. **Popover direction fixed same day too** (owner, immediate follow-up: "also need it to open the tag to the right and not the left cause it opens half off screen") — it was anchored `right-0` (grows leftward from the avatar's right edge), which runs off the left side of the viewport for any avatar near the screen's left edge (the common case in a list). Swapped to `left-0` (grows rightward from the avatar's left edge) so it stays on-screen.
  **Verified**: full backend suite green, new tests for `last_seen_iso()` (never-pinged, and returns the raw value even once stale — deliberately different from `is_online()`'s computed boolean) and the `_last_seen` annotate wiring. The refresh-cadence fix and the popover interaction are both UI-only, same as this project's standing pattern for that class of change — flagged for the owner's own confirmation rather than claimed as verified.

- **2026-08-17 (third) — Real-usage bug report on the two feature passes above: five issues, two of which turned out to be genuine bugs beyond what was reported, not just the reported symptom.** Owner tested the shipped work live and reported five things in one message. Two of the five ("blank assets need custom fields," "no way to delete contacts") each led to finding a SECOND, more severe bug while investigating the reported one — worth recording as a pattern: a feature that looks done in isolation (blank asset creation; the delete endpoint always existing) can still be unusable end-to-end, and only exercising it for real surfaces that.
  **(1) Dashboard block-button color picker was unreadable**: `ActionsEditor`'s native `<select>` showing color NAMES ate too much of an already-narrow row, squeezing the button-label text input down to unreadable width. Replaced with a compact swatch button (solid fill = current color) that opens a small popover grid on click — mirrors `EmojiPicker.jsx`'s own button-opens-a-popover-grid shape exactly, an established pattern already used elsewhere in this app rather than inventing a new one. `BUTTON_COLORS` (`actionKinds.js`) gained a separate `swatch` class per entry (a solid, always-visible fill) distinct from `classes` (the real button's hover-only style) — reusing `classes` for the swatch would have rendered 'default' as a blank, indistinguishable-from-empty square.
  **(2) Chat's "Thinking…" bubble didn't survive a reload while the agent was still genuinely running.** `loading` (`loadingIds.has(chatId)`) is pure local React state — correct for THIS tab's own active `send()`/`sendResume()` call, but `openSession()` (which `restoreForWorkspace()` and the `?chat_id=` deep-link effect both funnel through) never checked the session's actual server-side `status` at all. Reopening a still-running conversation after a reload, or from the drawer/a notification, showed no indicator whatsoever even though the agent was genuinely still working — a real regression risk of the whole "background execution" design (2026-08-15): the busier that feature makes it to leave and come back mid-run, the more this gap would have been hit. Fixed with a `restoredRunningIds` Set, separate from `loadingIds` — `openSession()` adds to it when `session.status === 'running'`, and a polling effect (4s interval, only runs while non-empty) re-checks `GET /chat/sessions` until the status moves off `"running"`, then calls `openSession()` again to pick up the final content the normal way (paused card, plain completion, whichever it turns out to be) — no separate completion-handling logic needed, it's the same function reused for both restoration and completion.
  **(3) Blank assets had no way to add custom fields — AND couldn't be saved past creation at all, a second bug found investigating the first.** The reported symptom (no custom-fields UI on a blank asset) was real and expected — Item 7 (2026-08-17, first pass) deliberately scoped blank assets to name+notes only, and the owner's live testing is what established that scope was wrong, not the earlier plan. Fixed with a freeform `CustomFieldsEditor` repeater (typed label doubles as the storage key, `assets_service._validate_fields()` relaxed to accept freeform pairs specifically when `template.get("key")` is unset — NOT just an empty `fields` list, since a REAL template with zero fields, like the seeded Folder template, must keep rejecting unknown keys). While implementing this, found `update_asset()` would raise `"Template None no longer exists"` on ANY save of a blank asset, not just a fields-containing one — `resolve_template()` returned `None` (meaning "stale, used to exist") for a genuinely blank asset (no template_id/key at all) instead of `{}` (meaning "no template, and that's fine"), and `update_asset()`'s `if template is None: raise` fires unconditionally whenever `"fields"` is in the update payload, which the frontend always sends (even as `{}`). This means blank assets had been fully uneditable since Item 7 shipped, not merely missing a feature — the owner's specific complaint ("mandatory") was the surface symptom of a load-bearing bug underneath, not scope creep.
  **(4) No way to delete a contact anywhere in the UI — `DELETE /contacts/{id}` had always existed and worked correctly; the frontend simply never called it.** `grep`-confirmed zero call sites for `contactsApi.remove` anywhere before this. Added to `ContactModal.jsx`'s edit-form button row, gated exactly like `AssetModal.jsx`'s own Delete button: never for a self-contact (dies with the account only, enforced server-side too), admin-only for a pool contact (mirrors Assets' own established pool-delete-is-admin-only pattern — deliberately NOT extended to "creator can delete their own pool creation" even though creators now get `edit` per the Contacts pass above; deletion of a shared household/team resource is a heavier, more destructive bar than editing it, consistent with how this app already treats pool deletion everywhere else), `_access === 'edit'` for an ordinary personal contact.
  **(5) Presence dot wasn't visible anywhere — this was the known, deliberately deferred half of Item 9**, not a new bug: infrastructure (`presence_service.py`, `POST /presence/ping`) shipped in the six-item pass above but was explicitly left unwired pending the Contacts pool rework, since both touch `annotate()`. With that rework now done, wiring it up was just adding `_online: bool` to `annotate()`'s output (gated behind the same access check every other field already goes through — no new lookup surface) and a small `PresenceDot` overlay in `ContactAvatar.jsx`, shown only when `self_of` is set.
  **Verified**: full backend suite green (748 passed, up from 743), `black --check .`/`isort --check-only .`/frontend `lint`/`build` all clean. New/rewritten tests: `test_create_blank_asset_accepts_freeform_custom_fields`, `test_blank_asset_custom_fields_survive_update_and_can_be_cleared`, `test_folder_template_with_zero_fields_still_rejects_unknown_keys`, `test_resolve_template_distinguishes_blank_from_stale_reference` (the real update_asset() bug, regression-proofed), `test_annotate_marks_self_contact_online_status`, `test_annotate_never_adds_online_field_to_an_ordinary_contact`. The Chat restoration fix and the two frontend-only UI additions (color swatch, Delete button) have no automated coverage — this codebase has no router/component-level test harness (confirmed: zero `TestClient`/RTL usage anywhere in `tests/`), so verification for those three is code-review-level, not test-level; flagged for the owner's own click-through same as every other UI-only change this session.

- **2026-08-17** — **Third feature pass from an 11-item owner message: six smaller/independent items shipped first, Contacts (the largest, most architecturally novel item) deliberately sequenced last.** Same research → interview → plan process as the two passes below (3 parallel Explore agents + a dedicated Plan agent for the Contacts cross-workspace design specifically), owner explicitly asked for security to be considered given Contacts pool-visibility ("dont need other users or outsiders to get sensitive data from a users contact even though it is in pool") — that review found two real pre-existing-pattern bugs (`_strip_private()` and `update_contact()`'s owner-identity checks, both keyed on `store_user == viewer`, which breaks once a self-contact's `store_user` becomes a pool pseudo-user) documented in the plan file for the Contacts implementation itself. Owner then sequenced explicitly: "Smaller items first, Contacts last," followed by "stop when you finish everything but contacts i want to set it at higher effort and clear cache" — this entry covers only the six non-Contacts items; Contacts (Items 2-3) and the presence-dot Contacts integration (back half of Item 9) are separate, later work.
  **Shipped this round:** (1) Tasks show a due time next to the due date on the card when one's set — the field already existed end-to-end (model, form, storage, sort), only display was missing. (2) Dashboard action buttons gained a `color` field (small fixed Tailwind palette — default/blue/green/red/purple/gray) in `ActionsEditor`/`blocks.jsx`; config is a freeform dict server-side, so zero backend schema change. (3) Dashboards left open now poll `loadCurrent()` every 45s while the tab is visible (same `setInterval`+`visibilitychange` pattern Chat's presence-ping already established), so a task/record created elsewhere shows up without a manual reload. (4) Chat's user message is now archived immediately after the session flips to `"running"`, *before* `run_agent()` is called — previously the user's message and the assistant's reply were only ever written together, after the agent call returned, so an interrupted/failed call lost the user's own message entirely (the actual cause of "have to wait 5-10s or it disappears" — the frontend's own optimistic local state was never the problem, only the server-side archive). (5) Assets can now be created blank (no template) — mirrors Dashboards' existing blank-or-template choice; `create_asset` only resolves/validates a template when one's given, `template`/`template_id` come back `null` for a blank asset. A latent bug was caught *before* shipping, not after: naively loosening the `templates.length > 0` gate on Assets.jsx's "+ New Asset" button would have left the empty-state branch order wrong (`templates.length === 0` still checked before `items.length === 0`), so an account with assets but zero templates would've shown "no templates yet" instead of its actual asset list — fixed by reordering the conditional before shipping either half. (6) Presence infrastructure — `services/presence_service.py` (`record_presence`/`is_online`, 90s staleness) generalizes Chat's own `chat_presence.json` pattern app-wide; see the Core Design Decision entry above for why it's a separate file rather than merged with chat's. `Layout.jsx` pings it on every page, not just Chat. Deliberately no `GET /presence/{user}` lookup endpoint (see Security Rules) — this is infrastructure for Item 9's online/offline contact dot, which isn't wired to Contacts yet since that touches the exact same `annotate()`/self-contact code the upcoming Contacts rework will rewrite; building it twice was the alternative being avoided.
  **Verified**: full backend suite green (724 passed, up from 696), `black --check .`/`isort --check-only .` clean, frontend `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean. New regression tests: `test_chat_saves_the_user_message_even_if_the_agent_call_raises` (mocks the agent call to raise, confirms the archive still has the user's message), `test_create_asset_without_a_template`/`test_create_blank_asset_rejects_any_field`, 4 new `test_presence_service.py` tests (not-online-before-ping, online-after-ping, stale-after-threshold, per-user isolation).

- **2026-08-17 (second) — Contacts cross-workspace architecture: the largest single item across both this and the prior feature pass, implemented directly against the plan file's already-completed design (no re-derivation), plus two additional real bugs found and fixed during implementation that the plan's own research hadn't caught.** Owner: "alright continue on contacts now," after explicitly sequencing this last across two passes so the smaller/independent items could ship first. Full design (self-contact storage move, the `cross_workspace` mechanism, pool default-sharing, account-creation linking, and the two security bugs the plan's own review had already found) lived in `/home/logcore/.claude/plans/iridescent-fluttering-flame.md` — implementing against it is what most of this entry describes.
  **The core mechanism**: `cross_workspace: bool` (default `False`) on any contact — a single record, never a duplicate, resolvable from the opposite workspace once flipped on. `contacts_service.effective_workspace(store_user, contact, workspace)` generalizes what used to be a self-contact-only router helper (`routers/contacts.py`'s old `_effective_ws`): self-contacts always resolve to `"personal"`; any other `cross_workspace` contact is resolved by checking which of the two workspace files actually holds that id (a genuine disk check, not just a boolean branch — a `cross_workspace` contact can physically live in either workspace's file depending on where it was created). Every read path (`list_visible_contacts`/`find_contact`/`find_deal`) was rewritten around one shared `_candidate_stores(viewer, viewer_role, workspace)` helper — own store + active pool at the ambient workspace (unchanged, listed first so the common case pays nothing extra) plus three additions: the viewer's own opposite-workspace store, the opposite workspace's pool, and anyone sharing an opposite-workspace contact with the viewer. **Deliberate deviation from the plan's own proposed design**: the plan called for a brand-new `contacts_index.cross_workspace_owners()` index, mirroring `sharers_for()`; implementation instead reuses the *existing* `sharers_for()` a second time with the swapped workspace argument, since it already returns exactly the right candidate-owner set for "who has shared a record stored in workspace X with this viewer" regardless of which workspace the *caller* is currently in — the only genuinely new piece needed was filtering each non-native result to `cross_workspace: True` before `resolve_access()` runs. No new index file, no new reindex triggers scattered through `create_contact`/`update_contact`, no new boot-warm hook — at this app's stated self-hosted family/small-team scale (see `docs/PROJECT.md`), the extra per-owner contact-list scan this reuse costs is cheaper than standing up and maintaining a second index for the same information. Documented as a deviation, not silently different from what the plan said.
  **Self-contacts became the forced-default application of the same mechanism**: `cross_workspace` always `True`, un-toggleable (guarded in `update_contact()`, mirroring the existing person-type-lock guard), physically stored in `POOL_HOUSEHOLD` — `get_self_contact()`/`create_self_contact()` now read/write `list_contacts(POOL_HOUSEHOLD, "personal")` directly instead of the individual user's own store. This is what makes "always visible to household AND team, from either workspace" (owner item #3) and "survives account deletion" both free: deleting a user's own Brain folder never touches `_household`'s store, and the household pool is already the general cross-workspace-scanned store every other viewer reaches through the mechanism above — no self-contact-specific code path needed anywhere in the read side anymore. `list_visible_contacts()`'s old self-contact short-circuit, `find_contact()`'s old short-circuit, and `find_deal()`'s old (already subtly-wrong, hardcoded-to-personal) self-contact special case were all deleted outright, exactly as the plan called for — self-contacts are picked up by the general pool-scanning branch now, no special-casing left.
  **Pool contact creation opened to non-admins (owner item #2), with a real gap found and closed before shipping**: `ContactCreate.pool` defaults `True` now (was `False`, admin-gated) — a new contact defaults into its workspace's pool unless "make personal" is chosen. Opening pool-contact creation to any user exposed a gap the plan's own research hadn't caught: `resolve_access()`'s pool branch had no "creator gets edit" concept at all (it only ever needed one, implicitly, via the non-pool branch's `store_user == viewer`, since pool creation used to be admin-only and admins already get `edit` via the `is_admin` check regardless) — a non-admin who created a shared household contact would only get `read` on their own creation, same as everyone else, unable to ever edit it. Added `contact.get("created_by") == viewer → edit` to the pool branch, checked after `hidden_from` (unlike the self-contact owner-check, which precedes `hidden_from` — a self-contact's "you can never lose access to your own identity record" guarantee is a different, stronger invariant than "you can manage what you created," and an admin *should* still be able to hide a pool contact from the person who added it).
  **Security — the two bugs the plan's own review had already found, now actually fixed**: `_strip_private()` and `update_contact()`'s owner-identity checks both used `store_user == viewer` as the sole "is this the owner" test — correct back when a self-contact's `store_user` WAS the owner's own name, permanently broken once it became `POOL_HOUSEHOLD` (never equal to any real username, including the owner's). Left unfixed, this would have kept correctly stripping private fields from every *other* pool member (the protection the owner explicitly asked about was never actually at risk) but would have ALSO incorrectly stripped them from the record's own rightful owner — a functional regression a real user would have hit immediately trying to view or edit their own health/schedule/AI-preference fields. Fixed with `store_user == viewer OR contact.self_of == viewer` in both places; `update_contact()`'s fix required reordering (resolve the target's `self_of` before deciding whether to strip the incoming update), not just a condition swap. A third, smaller instance of the identical bug class was found independently, not in the plan's list: `annotate()`'s `_owner` badge (`store_user == POOL_HOUSEHOLD → "household"`) had no owner exception either, which would have shown a user a "🏠 Household" badge on their OWN contact — cosmetic, not a security leak, but the exact same root cause, fixed the same way.
  **Two more real bugs found during implementation, neither in the plan's list, both from testing the actual behavior rather than trusting the design read correctly on paper**: (1) once self-contacts and ordinary personal contacts could be affiliated across two different stores (a self-contact in the household pool, linked to an ordinary contact in its creator's own store — impossible before this pass, since both used to live in the same store), `delete_contact()`'s dangling-affiliation-reference cleanup — which only ever scanned its own store — would silently leave a stale reference on the other side. Caught by keeping and adapting the existing `test_delete_contact_strips_dangling_affiliation_refs` test rather than just patching its store arguments to make it pass; fixed with one additional, bounded household-pool check (not a general N-store sweep). (2) `create_self_contact()`'s self_of-injection step (a second write immediately following the atomic `create_contact()` append) was still using the old racy `list_contacts()`+`_save_contacts()` two-step even after `create_contact()` itself was made atomic — leaving exactly the race 2e was meant to close half-open for the one call site that actually motivates it (two accounts being set up back-to-back). Closed with a small reusable `_mutate_contact()` atomic find-and-mutate helper, also reused by the new `link_self_contact()`/`release_self_contact()` functions below.
  **Account-creation linking (owner item #4, creation-only)**: `POST /admin/users` gains `contact_id`; `contacts_service.link_self_contact(contact_id, user_name)` atomically sets `self_of`/forces `cross_workspace: True`/`type: "person"`, downgrading any pre-existing edit-level `contributors` entry to contribute (the contact wasn't subject to the self-contact edit cap until this call made it one). Validated twice: once fast, before the account exists (fail-closed — a bad `contact_id` creates nothing), and again atomically inside `link_self_contact()`'s own locked mutate cycle once the account exists (closing the race window between those two checks) — a race loss there doesn't roll back the already-created account, it just leaves the new user to get an ordinary fresh self-contact on their next `/contacts/me` visit instead, logged as a warning. New `GET /contacts/available-for-linking` (admin-only, deliberately reads the household pool directly rather than going through the ambient-workspace-scoped `GET /contacts` — account creation is a household-pool concept regardless of which tab the admin happens to be on) backs `NewUser.jsx`'s inline search-and-pick UI (a small self-contained picker, not a retrofit of the shared `ContactPicker.jsx`, since that component's quick-create affordance doesn't apply here and threading a disable-quick-create prop through a component reused by four other call sites carried more risk than writing ~30 lines of self-contained JSX).
  **Migration**: `m013_move_self_contacts_to_household_pool` (idempotent per-user via the now-household-scanning `get_self_contact()`) relocates any self-contact that already existed before this pass shipped — record, interactions, deals, photo file — out of the user's own store into the household pool, converting `shared_with` to `contributors` (hand-rolled, mirroring `transfer_ownership()`'s own pool-destination conversion logic rather than calling that function directly, since it explicitly rejects `self_of` contacts).
  **Verified**: full backend suite green (725 passed — the 724 from the six-item round above plus this round's net additions/consolidations), `black --check .`/`isort --check-only .`/frontend `lint`/`build` all clean. 30+ new/rewritten tests in `test_contacts.py` covering cross-workspace visibility both directions, `effective_workspace()` resolution, editing from the "wrong" tab landing on the one real record, pool-contact-creator-gets-edit, the migration (including idempotency and the shared_with→contributors conversion), `link_self_contact`/`release_self_contact`, and — the owner's explicit must-pass ask — private-field stripping verified from BOTH a household (personal-workspace) and a team/business-workspace pool viewer angle via a single parametrized test, confirming no weaker path exists on the business side. Existing tests that hardcoded a self-contact's old storage location needed updating (expected churn per the plan, not a regression signal) — while fixing them, found and fixed several that would have silently "passed for the wrong reason" (asserting `pytest.raises(ValueError)` where the raise was now `"Contact not found"` instead of the actual guard being tested) in `test_profile_service.py` and `test_priority_service.py` too, not just `test_contacts.py`. Live browser verification **not done this round** — the change is almost entirely backend access-control logic (thoroughly covered by the new tests, which exercise the exact functions the HTTP layer calls) plus two small, low-complexity frontend additions (two checkboxes in `ContactModal.jsx`, a self-contained search-and-pick list in `NewUser.jsx`); flagged as the one open item, consistent with how every other UI-only change in this project that couldn't be visually confirmed from this environment has been handled.

- **2026-08-15** — **Second feature pass from one nine-item owner message: two items turned out to already work, one turned out to not exist at all, and the rest ranged from a config field to the largest single feature the app has taken on.** Same research → interview → plan process as 2026-08-09. Two items collapsed on investigation before any code was written: Finance same-book transfers already worked end-to-end (`finance_transfers.py` only rejects `from_book_id == to_book_id AND from_account_id == to_account_id` — same account, not same book); Web Push already fired in parallel with ntfy for nearly every notification type via `push_service.send_push()`/`suggestions_service.notify_user()` — not a missing feature, a delivery bug to diagnose. A third (Contacts custom-fields authoring) turned out to have no admin UI at all — `PUT /contacts/fields` had zero frontend callers anywhere in the repo, only `GET` was ever used.
  **Shipped this pass:** (1) Dashboard Tasks blocks (`top3_tasks`/`due_today`) gained a `sort_mode` config field (priority/date/alpha), mirrored into `agent_schemas.py`. (2) Generic dashboard action buttons — see the Core Design Decision entry above; new `contacts_list` block type added as a prerequisite (no general contacts-list block existed before). (3) Contacts custom fields gained `applies_to: ['person','company']` scoping plus a real admin authoring screen (`pages/settings/admin/ContactFields.jsx`, mirroring `TemplateManager.jsx`'s pattern) — `ContactModal.jsx`/`ContactDetail.jsx` filter their custom-field render loops by `contact.type`. (4) Asset attachment images now open in a new in-app `ImageLightbox` (`assetDisplay.jsx`) instead of a new browser tab — fixes both `AssetView.jsx`'s own attachment section and the dashboard Documents block in one place, since both already shared the one `AttachmentThumb` component; non-image files (PDFs) keep the existing new-tab behavior. (5) Push notification diagnosis: `VAPID_SUBJECT` defaulted to the placeholder `"logcore@localhost"` and was never exposed as a real env var — some push services (Apple's included) can reject sends whose VAPID `sub` claim looks obviously fake. Fixed the config (`docker/.env.example` now documents it, `_build_vapid_jwt` only prepends `mailto:` if the value isn't already a URI) and made `POST /push/test` return a distinguishable error (400 "no subscription on file" vs 502 "push service rejected or failed the send") instead of a collapsed `False`. Confirming actual arrival on the owner's own iPhone is a follow-up outside this environment's reach — logged in `docs/TASKS.md`. (6) Finance remembers the last-opened book server-side (`finance_prefs_path`, `GET/PUT /finance/prefs`), workspace-keyed, consulted only when no `?book=` override is present. (7) AI Chat — see the Core Design Decision entry above for the session-index mechanism; frontend `Chat.jsx` was substantially rewritten so the "Chats" drawer opens a conversation directly on click (no intermediate preview step) with active/unread indicators per the owner's explicit UX direction, and per-chat_id message state means switching conversations doesn't block on one still `running`.
  **Mid-flight correction, applied:** the owner said "keep version at 0.6.0 cause i never released it" after VERSION/package.json/CHANGELOG/help.json had already been bumped to 0.7.0 — all reverted/merged back into the existing unreleased 0.6.0 entry (date updated to reflect the latest work). This whole pass ships as part of 0.6.0, not a new version.
  **Verified**: 696/696 backend tests passing (up from 662), `black --check .`/`isort --check-only .` clean, frontend `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean throughout. Live browser verification in an isolated instance confirmed items 1, 3, and 7's UI shell; item 2 (dashboard action buttons) was **not** successfully live-clicked-through due to Playwright selector fragility in an unrelated dashboard-creation-modal flow (not the feature itself) — confidence instead rests on direct code tracing, clean lint/build, and exact reuse of the already-shipped/already-verified `nav_button`/`status_button` write logic. Item 7's actual AI-response flow (a session reacting to a real completion) could not be live-tested at all in this environment (no Anthropic API key available) — relies on 12 passing backend tests that do call the real `chat()` handler with a mocked completion.

- **2026-08-09** — **A generic pause/resume mechanism for the chat agent, fixing a real replay bug and unifying three features that turned out to be the same underlying need.** Root finding: approve-mode's "Approve" click never replayed the reviewed action — `run_agent` broke out of its loop on a pending write *before* appending the tool-use turn to `messages`, so nothing about the proposed call was persisted anywhere; clicking Approve just sent literal text ("Yes, go ahead.") for the model to re-decide from, with no guarantee it re-proposed the same thing. Rather than patch that one call site, the fix is framed generically because the owner's own next ask (an `ask_user_question` tool "exactly like this one from Claude Code") and a parallel, previously-unreported gap in `propose_plan` (a bare echo handler with the identical shape of problem) are *the same mechanism* — pause execution, get a structured human response through a UI widget, resume with the real outcome substituted in rather than the model re-guessing.
  **The mechanism**: a pending-turn record (`{run_id, kind: "write"|"question"|"plan", messages, pending_tool_calls, workspace, cross_workspace, steps}`) persisted to a new capped per-user store (`agent/pending_turns.json`, mirrors `runs.json`'s own cap pattern) the moment execution pauses — critically, *after* appending the model's tool-use turn to `messages`, which is the one line the original bug never reached. `run_agent` gained an optional `resume` param: for `kind="write"`, it executes the *persisted* `pending_tool_calls` through the exact same `_execute_tool` every normal turn already uses (real replay, never a re-derivation); for `kind="question"`, the caller-supplied answer becomes the tool result directly (no execution — the "tool" here is the human); for `kind="plan"`, approval just continues the loop, decline synthesizes a structured `{"declined": true}` result. Either way it falls through into the normal step loop afterward, so the model can react or continue multi-step work. The pending-turn record is deleted the instant it's loaded for resume (in `routers/chat.py`, before calling `run_agent`) specifically so a double-submitted Approve click can't replay the same write twice.
  **Why this shape and not three separate fixes**: building the mechanism once made `ask_user_question` and the `propose_plan` migration each a small, mechanical addition instead of their own bespoke pause logic — concretely, `ask_user_question` needed zero new resume-side code at all; it only needed a new *production* path (detecting the call in the step loop) because the *consumption* path (kind="question") was already written generically the first time.
  **A resulting property worth naming**: because a clarifying question can arise in any mode (not just approve — auto/plan can hit real ambiguity too), `ask_user_question` pauses regardless of mode, checked in the step loop *before* the approve-mode write-gate — if a response contains both a question and a write call, the question always wins, since there's no such thing as a mode-dependent way to answer a question the way there is to skip approval for a write.
  **How to apply going forward**: any new agent capability shaped like "propose something, wait for a structured human response, then continue" should extend this mechanism (`save_pending_turn`/`load_pending_turn`/`delete_pending_turn` in `agent_service.py`, `ResumeAction` in `routers/chat.py`) with a new `kind`, not invent a parallel pause path — that's exactly the trap `propose_plan` had fallen into (a second, unreported instance of the original bug's shape) before this round found and fixed it too.
  **Same-day, unrelated but touching the same files**: the agent also gained real Dashboard-building tools (`list_dashboards`/`get_dashboard`/`list_dashboard_templates`/`get_dashboard_block_catalog` read, `create_dashboard`/`add_dashboard_block`/`update_dashboard_block`/`remove_dashboard_block`/`create_dashboard_template`/`update_dashboard_template` write) and Chat.jsx gained a rich inline preview (reusing the real `DashboardGrid`/`BlockRenderer` in read-only mode) for the two dashboard-block-mutation approvals specifically. Full writeup, verification detail, and the complete list of research findings deliberately scoped out of this round: `docs/TASKS.md`.
  **Verified**: 30 new backend tests (replay-proof — asserting the *executed* tool call matches the originally-persisted one even when a second, different completion is queued for the resume step, which would produce a different action under the old re-derivation behavior), full suite 592 green, frontend build clean, and a live browser pass against an isolated instance with the real `/chat` endpoint mocked (no AI spend) confirming all four card types render correctly and each sends the exact resume payload shape expected.

- **2026-08-07 (third)** — **Dashboard visual/architecture pass: hero header, content-aware block shapes, catalog search, and a generic Collection block.** Prompted by a discovery exercise trialing the module against a real-estate use case (same beta-tester-sourced backlog item the original Dashboards spec cites) — surfaced that the block system only had two shapes: one fixed/`$subject`-bound record with an action, or a read-only list with no action. Nothing combined "many related records" with "a per-item action," and the one list-style resolver (`list_assets_for_contact` → `linked_assets`) returned only `{id, name, icon, template_label}`. Root cause: every block type is its own hand-written backend resolver + frontend component — extending to a new use case always meant writing code, never configuring existing code.
  **The fix is one generic block, not a bigger catalog.** New `services/dashboard_blocks/_collections.py::resolve_collection` separates *source* (a template id + an optional `link_contact_id` filter — reuses `list_visible`/`attach_templates`, the same machinery `list_assets_for_contact` already used, now factored out as a shared `assets_service.asset_links_contact(asset, template, contact_id)` helper), *view* (`list` / `kanban` grouped by one `select` field / `count`), and *action* (a status control that calls `assetsApi.update(id, {fields: {[status_field]: value}})` directly from the frontend — the exact same call `status_button`'s `set_field` action already makes, so a read-only viewer's click still 403s correctly; no new write endpoint). `link_contact_id` uses `kind: 'contact'`, so it gets the existing `"$subject"` checkbox in template-editing mode for free — zero new sentinel-handling code. Scoped to **Assets only** for v1 (Contacts have no per-instance template to pick display fields from); Kanban moves cards via a per-card status select, not drag-and-drop, deliberately matching the existing Deals-pipeline dropdown convention rather than reopening this week's drag-gesture work for a different block type.
  **Visual pass, independent of the above:** every block previously rendered as the same bordered/frosted `.card` regardless of content — new `shape` field on `BLOCK_REGISTRY` entries (`'list'` drops that wrapper for genuinely list-shaped blocks, default `'detail'` keeps it for single-record ones) fixed the "everything is an identical box" complaint directly, verified via screenshot (Top 3 Tasks now renders borderless; Custom Fields/Collection still show the bordered card). A new `DashboardHero.jsx` renders a subject-bound dashboard's identity (avatar/icon + name + template label) above the grid — `GET /dashboards/{id}/render` gained a `subject` field (`routers/dashboards.py::_resolve_subject`, reusing `contacts_service.find_contact`/`assets_service.find_asset`, never erroring the whole render if the subject's become inaccessible — just omits the hero). `BlockPicker.jsx`'s "+ Add Block" catalog got a client-side search input over the existing catalog endpoint — no backend change.
  **Explicitly deferred, not forgotten:** replacing the pixel-grid layout (`DashboardGrid.jsx`) with a flowing/sections model was evaluated and set aside — it's the one item that would touch this week's hand-rolled touch-drag mechanism, and the competitive read didn't support it as a "must" (Grafana/Retool/ClickUp all keep a pixel grid; the actual complaints were uniform box styling and the data ceiling, both addressed above). Revisit only if this pass doesn't fix the "blocky" feel on its own.
  **Verified**: 6 new backend tests (`test_dashboards_service.py`) covering `asset_links_contact`, template/archived filtering, `link_contact_id` filtering (including via the `$subject` sentinel end-to-end through `render_block`), and `status_options` derivation — full suite green **except two pre-existing, unrelated `test_ai_usage_service.py` date-dependent failures confirmed via `git stash` to exist on clean `master` before this round** (not fixed here — out of scope, flagged in `docs/TASKS.md`). Isolated Playwright: 14/14 checks passed end to end, including clicking a Collection block's status control in the real browser and confirming the underlying asset's field actually changed via the API — not just a plausible UI state.

- **2026-08-07 (second)** — **Dashboard Templates + grouped navigation.** Owner: "the navigation to other dashboards is too inconvenient and we still need to add dashboard templates like in assets... templates should not be mandatory as they are with assets. just a easy way to make the same dashboard. would still like the way that changing the template updates all the dashboards though... group them where the template is the 'folder'." Three architectural forks resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before writing code (all "Recommended" picked): (1) a templated dashboard has one shared **subject** — a contact or asset — that its blocks can reference instead of a fixed record id; (2) the template's **block set** (types/config) stays live-synced to every instance, but each instance's own **layout** (position/size) goes independent the moment a block exists there; (3) the two-tier system (admin-managed global + per-user personal, with personal-template sharing) mirrors Asset Templates exactly.
  **The data model, and why it needed no migration:** a dashboard gains `template_id`/`subject_type`/`subject_id`, all `.get()`-defaulted to `None` — absent means today's freeform behavior, so every existing dashboard needed zero backfill. Unlike Asset Templates (which resolve structure at *read* time and never store it on the instance), a dashboard's `blocks` array stays the literal thing every existing consumer (`render.py`, `dashboard_index.py`, the router) already reads — so instead of a parallel "resolve live" code path, `dashboards_service._sync_templated_dashboard()` reconciles a templated dashboard's stored `blocks` against its template's current blocks **on every read** (self-healing-on-read, the same shape `resolve_default_dashboard_id` already uses in this file), and persists the result only if it actually changed. The reconciliation (`_sync_blocks_from_template`) is one pure function: for each template slot (keyed by a stable id), an *existing* instance block keeps its own stored `layout` untouched and takes `type`/`config` fresh from the template; a *new* slot gets an auto-computed stacked layout. That single function *is* the entire "block set stays synced, layout stays independent" mechanism — add/remove/retype in the template propagates automatically, and a slot's position/size can never drift back once a person has dragged it. The generic PATCH `blocks` endpoint additionally enforces this server-side for a templated dashboard (`_apply_layout_only` — carries over layout by slot id, silently ignores any type/config/add/remove attempt in the payload) so the guarantee holds regardless of what the UI sends, not just because the UI happens to hide the affordances.
  **`"$subject"` — a sentinel, not a mechanism resolvers know about.** A template author can put the literal string `"$subject"` in place of a concrete id on any `contact_id`/`asset_id`-kind config field (`BlockPicker.jsx`, in `templateMode`, offers this as a checkbox — "Use this dashboard's own contact/asset" — only on fields whose kind matches the template's declared `subject_type`, so the picker itself prevents authoring a mismatched field). Substitution happens exactly once, centrally, in `render.py::render_block()` right before any resolver runs (`_resolve_subject_config`) — so none of the ~10 existing record-linked resolvers needed a single line changed. If a `"$subject"` sentinel survives unsubstituted because the dashboard has no subject picked yet, the block locks with `locked_reason: "no_subject"` (a clearer state than whatever a resolver would otherwise do with a literal `"$subject"` id — usually a generic not-found).
  **Two-tier templates, mirroring assets almost line-for-line:** new `services/dashboard_templates_service.py` mirrors `assets_service.py`'s template section (`_template_store_path`/`list_global_templates`/`list_personal_templates`/`visible_templates`/`create_template`/`update_template`/`delete_template`/`share_template`/`respond_to_template_share`/`template_reference_count`) closely enough that reading one explains the other. `delete_template` refuses while any dashboard still references it (same guard as assets), so `_sync_templated_dashboard`'s "template not found → leave the instance frozen at its last sync" branch is defense in depth, never the primary guarantee. Global store: new `global_dashboard_templates_path()`; personal store: `dashboard_templates_path(user_name)` — this had existed unused in `file_service.py` since dashboards first shipped, seeded speculatively and never wired up until now.
  **Grouped navigation — the actual UX ask.** `list_visible_dashboards()` denormalizes `_template_label`/`_template_icon` onto every list item server-side (via `all_templates_by_id()`, same rationale as `assets_service.attach_templates` — a shared dashboard shows its group correctly even if the viewer doesn't own that template). `DashboardSwitcher.jsx` groups by that label into collapsible sections (default expanded, "Other" bucket for ungrouped) when there's no active search — a live query flattens back to a plain filtered list, since browsing structure and searching for one item are different tasks and collapsed groups would just hide matches. `DashboardPicker.jsx` (the `nav_button` target picker) got the identical grouping via native `<optgroup>` instead of a bespoke collapsible list — still "a dropdown for quick and easy selection" per the owner's own phrasing, just organized.
  **Scope boundaries stated up front, not discovered late:** a templated dashboard's block *set* is entirely template-derived for v1 — no per-instance one-off extra blocks beyond the template (the "Detach from template" action, `dashboards_service.detach_template`, is the escape hatch: freezes current blocks/layout in place as an ordinary freeform dashboard). `nav_button`'s target (a module+record-id composite, not a plain `contact_id`/`asset_id` field) doesn't get `$subject` support in this pass.
  **A pre-existing bug found and fixed alongside this, not part of the ask:** `Layout.jsx`'s notification-bell `respond()` had no case for `dashboard_share` (dashboard *instance* sharing, shipped when Dashboards first launched) — it fell through to `assetsApi.respondShare(n.id, accept)`, which takes a completely different shape (`dashboardsApi.respondShare` needs `(owner, dashboardId, accept)`, not a bare notification id) and would have 404'd/no-op'd silently. Fixed alongside adding the equivalent `dashboard_template_share` case this round introduces, since both are instances of the same "falls through to the assets default" gap.
  **Verified**: full backend suite green (566 passed) including 13 new tests (`test_dashboards_service.py`) covering template CRUD + the referenced-template delete guard, sync-on-read add/remove/retype propagation, per-instance layout independence surviving a template change, the layout-only PATCH enforcement against a tampered type/config payload, `$subject` substitution resolving to two different dashboards' own contacts, the `no_subject` lock state, and detach-stops-sync. `npm run build`/`eslint` clean. Isolated Playwright (separate backend + synthetic brain): 22/23 automated UI+API checks passed end to end (one script-selector mistake on my end, confirmed via screenshot — not a product bug) — created a global "Client Overview" template with a `$subject`-linked block, made two dashboards from it for two different contacts, confirmed each rendered its own contact's data, confirmed a template edit (adding a block) propagated to both without touching either instance directly, confirmed a per-instance layout customization survived that same template edit while the sibling instance's copy of that slot stayed untouched, confirmed the templated-PATCH tamper attempt was rejected, confirmed the switcher/settings/template-manager UI screenshots matched the design exactly (grouped collapsible sections with an "Other" bucket holding every pre-existing dashboard from earlier rounds this week, "Uses template: X" + Change subject + Detach in Settings, a working block-list template editor).

- **2026-08-07** — **`nav_button` can target a specific dashboard, not just land on whatever `/` currently resolves to.** Owner: "select to go to a certain dashboard not just the module itself" — `dashboard` was already a valid module in the nav-target dropdown (it's a real `ALL_MODULES` entry), but had no "specific record" option, so it could only ever mean "your current default dashboard," which changes over time and isn't what "navigate to *this* dashboard" means. Fixed the same way every other module's record-linking already works, not a new mechanism: `Dashboard.jsx` already reads `?id=<dashboardId>` (that's how the switcher/deep-linking already works), so this only needed `dashboard: 'id'` added to `deepLinks.js`'s `RECORD_PARAM`, a new `DashboardPicker.jsx` (plain `<select>` over `dashboardsApi.list()`, same shape as `FinanceBookPicker.jsx` — only ever offers dashboards this viewer can already see) registered in `ModuleAndRecordPicker.jsx`'s generic `RECORD_PICKERS` map, and a `dashboard` branch in `_actions.py`'s `_resolve_record_title()` using `dashboards_service.find_dashboard()` (the exact same access-checked lookup pattern as every other module's branch there) so the button's default label shows the *target* dashboard's real name instead of a generic "Go to Dashboard." No new picker mechanism, no schema change — `ModuleAndRecordPicker`'s existing whole-page/section/record split from the day before already had a `RecordPicker` slot for exactly this shape. Verified: the button's `href` resolves to `/?id=<target>`, its default label shows the target's real name (confirmed this correctly takes priority over the generic fallback, matching how every other record-linked nav_button already behaves), and clicking it genuinely lands on and renders the target dashboard.

- **2026-08-06 (fifth follow-up)** — **Six requests in one turn: a real bug (button labels never showed), a scope-narrowing (contacts: archive → real field updates), a genuine feature add (nav_button sub-pages/sections), a reuse win (Documents block gets real previews for free), a naming pass, and the biggest visual change yet (block chrome is now edit-mode-only).** (1) **Label bug**: `resolve_nav_button`/`resolve_status_button` (`_actions.py`) never included `ctx.config.get("label")` in their returned `data` — the frontend's `data.label || defaultLabel()` logic was always correct, the backend just never gave it anything to use. Fixed by adding `"label"` to all four `data` dicts (nav, task, contact, asset). **How this slipped through**: every prior verification round checked that the DEFAULT label rendered correctly, never that a CUSTOM one overrode it — a reminder that "the fallback works" and "the override works" are two different claims needing two different tests. (2) **Contacts: archive/unarchive replaced with real field updates** — owner: "contacts dont need to be archivalble i want the contacts part to be able to update contact data. unless the user is not given permission to do so." Investigated the actual Contact schema before designing anything (`contacts_service.py`): most "pickable-looking" fields are either free text with no backend enum (`status`, `income_range`, etc.) or in `_PRIVATE_FIELDS` (`blood_type`, `height_unit`, `weight_unit`, `income_range`, `budget_style`, `communication_style` — silently stripped from any update where the clicking viewer isn't the contact's own owner, which would make a "click to update someone else's data" button a confusing no-op). Landed on `gender` (male/female) and `marital_status` (5 fixed values, UI-convention enforced) as the only fields that are simultaneously non-private, genuinely fixed-choice, and useful — a new `ContactFieldPicker.jsx` (hardcoded list, no live API needed, unlike Assets' template-driven equivalent) replaces the old `contact_action` (archive/unarchive) config field with `contact_field` (`{field_key, value}`). **A correctness trap avoided by checking rather than assuming**: Assets' write call wraps values in `{fields: {...}}`; Contacts' `PATCH /contacts/{id}` is a **flat** body (`ContactUpdate` in `routers/contacts.py`) and — because pydantic here has no `extra='ignore'`-defeating config — would have silently accepted an Assets-shaped `{fields: {gender: 'male'}}` body and changed nothing, a 200-that-lies. `StatusButtonBlock`'s contact branch now calls `contactsApi.update(id, {[field_key]: field_value})` directly, flat. "Unless the user is not given permission" is already handled for free — `PATCH /contacts/{id}` requires full `edit` access server-side (`_require_edit`, 403 for `contribute` — contacts have no contribute-level field-write path the way Assets do), and this dashboard button was already designed from day one to call each target module's own real endpoint directly, so the existing real-403-not-silent-failure UI handling applies unchanged. (3) **nav_button sub-pages/sections**: owner wanted "subpages and sections... a sub picker that appears for each picked main page." Surveyed the actual navigable sub-views before building anything: Finance has 6 real tab views behind a `?view=` param its own page already reads (`overview/transactions/budgets/recurring/invoices/reports`); Automations has exactly one (`?view=inbox`); Settings has no query-param views at all — its sub-pages are real distinct routes (`/settings/appearance`, `/settings/admin/hosting`, etc.), and Settings isn't even a real module (no `ALL_MODULES` entry, no `require_module` gate). New `MODULE_SECTIONS` in `deepLinks.js` holds a label list per module; `deepLinkUrl(module, recordId, section)` gained a third param, special-cased for `module === 'settings'` (section IS the full path already, no query-string needed) vs. everyone else (`?view=<section>`). `ModuleAndRecordPicker.jsx`'s binary "specific record?" checkbox became a three-way "whole page / specific section / specific record" select, since a button that goes to one specific tab vs. one specific record are two different simple questions, not a combined matrix (Finance's own book+view combination was left unsupported for the same reason — real but narrower use case the owner didn't ask for). Settings is injected as a hand-added extra option in the module dropdown, not folded into `ALL_MODULES` (which drives sidebar nav and per-user module toggles Settings has no part in); admin-only sub-pages are filtered out of the list for a non-admin dashboard *editor* as a UI nicety only — real enforcement is still each route's own `<AdminOnly>` guard, unaffected by anything here. (4) **Documents block gets real previews** — owner: "display the images or pdfs so they can be clicked on easily." Found `components/assetDisplay.jsx`'s `AttachmentThumb` already does exactly this (image blob preview, generic icon + filename for non-images, click-to-open via a blob URL) for `AssetView.jsx` — reused directly rather than rebuilding, which also means the dashboard's preview inherits the *correct* security model for free: `AttachmentThumb` calls `assetsApi.fileBlob()` directly against the real `/assets/{id}/files/{fileId}` endpoint under the clicking viewer's own session, same "call the target module's own endpoint" pattern the write-capable action blocks already established — never a dashboard-mediated bypass. Only backend change needed: `resolve_documents` (`_assets.py`) now includes `asset_id` in its `data` (previously only `attachments`), since `AttachmentThumb` needs it to build the right API calls per file. (5) **Confusing "record widget" names**: added a word or two of source context to labels that pull from an indirect/cross-module source — `Linked Deals` → `Contact's Deals`, `Custom Fields` → `Custom Fields (Contact/Asset)`, `Linked Assets` → `Contact's Linked Assets`, `Documents/Attachments` → `Asset Documents/Files`, `Linked Tasks` → `Asset's Linked Tasks`, `Linked Contact` → `Asset's Linked Contact`. Left alone: blocks whose source was already unambiguous (`Top 3 Tasks`, `My Assets Summary`, etc.) and both new action blocks (an action, not a data source). Kept short since both the catalog grid and the block header truncate long labels. (6) **Block chrome hidden until edit mode** — owner: "all blocks have the block name and all that on it on view mode, hide that until edit mode is open. makes it look cleaner." `BlockRenderer.jsx`'s icon+label+✎+✕ header row (the non-chromeless path — chromeless blocks already had no permanent header) is now wrapped in `{editing && (...)}` entirely, not just the buttons half as before — no reserved space in view mode either, not merely hidden-but-present, so content genuinely gets the card's full height back. **Verified, not assumed, for every piece above**: isolated backend + synthetic brain + Playwright — a custom label now genuinely overrides the default on both action block types; a real contact's `gender` field changes via the real API on click and `archived` stays untouched; both a Finance-section link (`/finance?view=budgets`) and a Settings-section link (`/settings/appearance`) resolve to the exact right URL; the Documents block renders a real `<img>` sourced from a real uploaded attachment; the renamed catalog labels appear; the header is absent in view mode and present in edit mode, with its ✎ button still opening the config picker correctly. Full backend suite green (555 passed, same 2 pre-existing unrelated flakes), frontend build/lint clean.

- **2026-08-06 (fourth follow-up)** — **✎/✕ (and a nav_button's own pill) stopped responding to touch entirely — "i cant delete blocks or open edit" — root cause was one line in react-draggable's own source, not a permission issue as guessed.** Every dashboard block's edit/remove buttons, and a chromeless `nav_button`'s own `<Link>` pill, live inside `.block-drag-handle` — the same div `draggableHandle=".block-drag-handle"` tells `react-grid-layout` to treat as the drag surface. Confirmed by reading `react-draggable`'s source a third time this week (`node_modules/react-draggable/build/cjs/Draggable.js:420`): `DraggableCore.handleDragStart` — which runs on **every** `touchstart` matching the handle selector, regardless of the `onTouchStartCapture` interception this app added on 2026-08-05 — calls `e.preventDefault()` unconditionally (`if (e.type === "touchstart" && !this.props.allowMobileScroll) e.preventDefault()`), before any check for whether a real drag ever follows. Calling `preventDefault()` on `touchstart` is exactly what suppresses the browser's own tap-to-click synthesis, so **every** tap on a button or link inside the handle was silently eaten, whether or not it triggered this app's own long-press system at all. The earlier `onTouchStartCapture` button/link exclusion (this same day, first attempt at a fix) did not help, because it only stops *this component's own* code from reacting — it does nothing to stop `react-draggable`'s own listener, attached separately to the grid-item node, from also seeing the same touchstart and calling `preventDefault()` on it regardless. **Fix**: `draggableCancel="button, a"` on `<ResponsiveGridLayout>` — mirrors the existing `draggableHandle` prop, flows to the exact same `DraggableCore.handleDragStart` check (`this.props.cancel && matchesSelectorAndParentsTo(...)`), and returns *before* the `preventDefault()` line for anything matching. This is the correct layer for this fix, the same way `.react-resizable-handle` was already excluded via this exact mechanism (`GridItem.mixinDraggable` already prepends it: `cancel: ".react-resizable-handle" + (this.props.cancel ? ...)`) — buttons/links needed the identical treatment, just never got it when the touch system was first built. **A second thing investigated during the same report, ruled out as a real bug**: a "delete removed all 3 blocks instead of 1" result in testing traced to the tapped button rendering at the exact bottom edge of an *unscrolled* viewport, where the fixed mobile footer nav's own link was the true topmost element at that point (confirmed via `document.elementFromPoint`) — the tap navigated to `/chat` instead, which is why the grid appeared empty afterward (wrong page, not zero blocks). Scrolling `<main>` to its maximum confirmed the same button becomes cleanly clickable once clear of the footer's fixed zone — normal mobile-scrolling behavior already covered by this page's existing bottom spacer (`Dashboard.jsx`'s `<div className="h-20 md:hidden">`), not a new bug to fix. **How to apply**: when `draggableHandle` is set to something broader than a dedicated drag-only element (here, the whole card, deliberately, so most of it is grabbable), every *other* interactive element nested inside that handle needs an explicit `draggableCancel` entry — it is not enough to keep your own code from treating it as a drag target, because the underlying drag library's own touchstart listener runs independently and will still claim (and `preventDefault()`) the touch unless told not to via the library's own exclusion mechanism. Verified via isolated Playwright using `locator.tap()` (not raw CDP dispatch, and not `.click()`, both of which have their own separate known quirks against this exact grid — `.tap()` is the one API confirmed to reproduce real touch-to-click synthesis faithfully here, verified first against a plain unrelated HTML button before trusting it against the app): ✎ now opens the config picker and ✕ now removes exactly one block via a real tap, on both a regular block and a chromeless one; full drag/resize regression suite (horizontal, vertical, resize-persists-across-other-interactions) re-run clean against the same build.

- **2026-08-06 (third follow-up)** — **The drag-persistence fix two entries below was necessary but not sufficient: resize had the exact same gap, and it took reading react-grid-layout's own source a second time to find the real, general root cause.** Owner report, from actually testing resize: "i resize one and then select another and the first one goes back to original full size." That fix only ever taught `DashboardGrid.jsx`'s hand-rolled touch-drag (`commitDrag`) to echo its own result back into what gets rendered — it did nothing for resize, or for native desktop mouse-drag, both of which go through `react-grid-layout`'s own internal mechanism entirely. **The actual, general root cause**, found by reading `node_modules/react-grid-layout/build/ResponsiveReactGridLayout.js` (not assumed): the `Responsive` wrapper component has its *own* `getDerivedStateFromProps`, completely separate from the inner grid's `activeDrag`-aware one that the previous entry describes — and this outer one has **no concept of an active gesture at all**. The instant the `layouts` prop `DashboardGrid.jsx` passes in doesn't deep-equal what `Responsive` last saw, it unconditionally *regenerates its entire internal layout from that prop*, discarding whatever the inner grid's own drag/resize state was independently holding. Since resize (and native mouse-drag) never fed their result back into that prop — only into `Dashboard.jsx`'s separate, unrendered `pendingLayouts` — the prop stayed stale for as long as *nothing else* changed it. The moment *anything else* did (e.g. `commitDrag` reporting a touch-drag on a different block, which is exactly "select another" in the owner's report), `Responsive` re-derived its whole layout from that stale prop, silently reverting whatever had been resized. **Fix, replacing the narrower one below**: `DashboardGrid.jsx`'s `pendingLocalLayouts` (renamed from `localOverrides`) is no longer populated only by `commitDrag` — a new `reportLayoutChange()` wraps *every* layout report, including `<ResponsiveGridLayout>`'s own native `onLayoutChange` (which fires for both mouse-drag and resize, on both breakpoints), so the prop handed back to `Responsive` is never stale for any block regardless of which of the three mechanisms (native mouse-drag, native resize, or the hand-rolled touch-drag) last touched it. Still cleared whenever `blocks` itself changes, same as before. **How to apply, more sharply than before**: when a "controlled" library component re-derives its internal state from a prop under some condition, that condition has to be checked at *every* layer the library is composed of, not just the outermost one you happen to be looking at — `ReactGridLayout`'s own reconciliation is careful about active gestures; the `Responsive` wrapper one layer up is not, and assuming "the library holds state for me" without checking both was exactly the gap. Verified via the same isolated Playwright/CDP harness: desktop — resized a block via mouse (confirmed visually shrunk), then dragged an unrelated second block, confirmed the first block's size survived unchanged, then verified both changes persisted correctly via Save Layout; mobile — re-ran the horizontal- and vertical-drag checks from the entry below against this new code path and confirmed a second drag's result doesn't clobber a first drag's already-persisted axis.

- **2026-08-06 (second follow-up)** — **Two more real-device reports on the same feature: a native text-selection popup during the hold, and "moves up/down but not left/right" — one real fix, one non-bug correctly explained by the mobile migration.** (1) **Text-selection callout**: a long-press on any element with text is *also* the browser's own trigger for text selection (the magnifier + Copy/Select All popup on iOS, similar on Android) — nothing in the press-and-hold implementation ever suppressed it, so it could win the race against the drag-arm timer. Fixed with the standard, well-known suppression (`-webkit-touch-callout: none`, `-webkit-user-select: none`, `user-select: none`) on `.block-drag-handle` itself — not scoped to `.touch-drag-armed`, since the callout can appear *during* the hold, before the block is armed. (2) **Horizontal movement**: verified, not assumed, before answering — spun up the isolated Playwright/CDP harness again and dragged both a narrow (`w:6` of 12 mobile columns) and a full-width (`w:12`) block sideways. The narrow block moved and persisted correctly (`sm.x` 0→4, survived Save Layout + a fresh reload); the full-width block correctly could **not** move at all — `commitDrag()`'s own clamp, `Math.max(cols - moved.w, 0)`, is `0` when a block's width equals the grid's column count, since there is *nowhere* for a block exactly as wide as the grid to go. This isn't a bug: `m012_rescale_dashboard_mobile_grid_units` (2026-08-05) unconditionally normalized every existing block's `sm.w` to `12` (full width) as part of enabling `sm` to be read at all — a safe, sensible one-time default at the time, but it means every block on a dashboard that predates this week's mobile-editing work is currently full-width and mathematically cannot slide sideways until resized narrower first. Resizing was never gated behind the press-and-hold requirement (only *dragging* was — see the entry below), so the corner resize handle already works immediately on touch, no hold needed; the real gap is discoverability, not mechanics. **A real bug did surface while investigating**, but it was in the verification script, not the app, twice over the same session: the first test run forgot to click "Save Layout" after the drag (so the API correctly still showed the pre-drag value — nothing was wrong, the change just hadn't been persisted), and needed a second look before concluding anything. **How to apply**: when a report describes behavior that traces cleanly to a deliberate, dated migration decision, explain the mechanism rather than patching around it — the fix here is user guidance (resize before you drag sideways), not a code change to the clamp, which is doing exactly what it should.

- **2026-08-06** — **Bug fix, same feature as the entry below: the press-and-hold touch drag armed and followed the finger correctly but the block always snapped back to its old spot on release.** Owner report: "the hold and drag works as intended but the grid doesnt unlock so the blocks cant be moved." Root cause, found by re-reading `DashboardGrid.jsx`: `commitDrag()` computed the new grid position correctly and called `onLayoutChange`, but that prop only ever reaches `Dashboard.jsx`'s `pendingLayouts` state — nothing feeds it back into `DashboardGrid`'s own `layouts`, which was (and still is) computed purely from the `blocks` prop via `useMemo`. Once `clearGesture()` removed the CSS-transform "ghost drag" at the end of the gesture, the block rendered from `layouts` again — still reflecting the *old*, unchanged `blocks` data — so it visually reverted to its starting position instantly, even though the pending change genuinely existed and "Save Layout" would have persisted it correctly if clicked (which is exactly why the previous round's Playwright pass didn't catch this — every check happened *after* clicking Save Layout, never in the gap between release and save, which is precisely where the bug lived).
