Desktop mouse dragging never had this problem because `react-grid-layout`'s own drag mechanism holds the dropped position in its own internal state independent of props; this hand-rolled touch path had no equivalent. **Fix**: `DashboardGrid.jsx` now keeps a `localOverrides` state (`{[breakpoint]: {[blockId]: {x,y}}}`), merged into `layouts` inside the same `useMemo`, set by `commitDrag()` alongside the existing `onLayoutChange` call, and cleared whenever the `blocks` prop itself changes (Save Layout persisting, or switching dashboards) since the server-authoritative data has caught up by then and the override would just be redundant. **How to apply**: any hand-rolled interaction that bypasses a library's own state management (as this touch-drag necessarily does — see the entry below for why) has to independently replicate whatever visual-persistence guarantee the library normally provides for free; a callback into the parent is not enough if the component's own rendering doesn't also reflect the same change. **How to test for this class of bug going forward**: check the intermediate/uncommitted state, not just the state after an explicit save — a passing "final value is correct after Save" assertion says nothing about whether the UI looked broken in between. Verified via the same isolated Playwright/CDP methodology: block's `getBoundingClientRect()` now shows the new position immediately after `touchend` (before any Save Layout interaction), holds stable a further 800ms, and round-trips correctly through Save Layout + a fresh page reload. and followed the finger correctly but the block always snapped back to its old spot on release.** Owner report: "the hold and drag works as intended but the grid doesnt unlock so the blocks cant be moved." Root cause, found by re-reading `DashboardGrid.jsx`: `commitDrag()` computed the new grid position correctly and called `onLayoutChange`, but that prop only ever reaches `Dashboard.jsx`'s `pendingLayouts` state — nothing feeds it back into `DashboardGrid`'s own `layouts`, which was (and still is) computed purely from the `blocks` prop via `useMemo`. Once `clearGesture()` removed the CSS-transform "ghost drag" at the end of the gesture, the block rendered from `layouts` again — still reflecting the *old*, unchanged `blocks` data — so it visually reverted to its starting position instantly, even though the pending change genuinely existed and "Save Layout" would have persisted it correctly if clicked (which is exactly why the previous round's Playwright pass didn't catch this — every check happened *after* clicking Save Layout, never in the gap between release and save, which is precisely where the bug lived). Desktop mouse dragging never had this problem because `react-grid-layout`'s own drag mechanism holds the dropped position in its own internal state independent of props; this hand-rolled touch path had no equivalent. **Fix**: `DashboardGrid.jsx` now keeps a `localOverrides` state (`{[breakpoint]: {[blockId]: {x,y}}}`), merged into `layouts` inside the same `useMemo`, set by `commitDrag()` alongside the existing `onLayoutChange` call, and cleared whenever the `blocks` prop itself changes (Save Layout persisting, or switching dashboards) since the server-authoritative data has caught up by then and the override would just be redundant. **How to apply**: any hand-rolled interaction that bypasses a library's own state management (as this touch-drag necessarily does — see the entry below for why) has to independently replicate whatever visual-persistence guarantee the library normally provides for free; a callback into the parent is not enough if the component's own rendering doesn't also reflect the same change. **How to test for this class of bug going forward**: check the intermediate/uncommitted state, not just the state after an explicit save — a passing "final value is correct after Save" assertion says nothing about whether the UI looked broken in between. Verified via the same isolated Playwright/CDP methodology: block's `getBoundingClientRect()` now shows the new position immediately after `touchend` (before any Save Layout interaction), holds stable a further 800ms, and round-trips correctly through Save Layout + a fresh page reload.

- **2026-08-05 (second follow-up)** — **Mobile dashboard dragging refined to press-and-hold, and Action Buttons became bare chromeless pills.** Two owner reports after using the mobile-parity + Action Buttons round below on a real touch device (trackpad/emulated, not yet a real iOS device): "the mobile drag portion would be good to restrict it to press and hold for a few seconds... so users can still swipe the whole screen to scroll while in edit mode," and the action buttons should be "a tiny button only block, no other text or anything only the pill button so there can be many of them added on a small space." **Superseded by this entry**: the "full parity, `touch-action:none` on the drag handle" decision in the entry below — that made *any* touch on an editing block start a drag, since `touch-action:none` blocks the browser's own scroll gesture recognition entirely regardless of how far the finger actually moves. **The fix required hand-rolling touch dragging, not a CSS tweak, because `react-draggable` (confirmed by reading its actual source in `node_modules`, not assumed) has no concept of a start delay**: `DraggableCore.handleDragStart` checks its `disabled`/`handle` props exactly once, synchronously, at the browser's own `touchstart` event, and only THEN attaches the document-level `touchmove`/`touchend` listeners that do the actual dragging — flipping `disabled`/`isDraggable` to `true` later, mid-gesture, does nothing for a touch sequence already in progress, because there's no second `touchstart` to re-evaluate against. That rules out the tempting "just add a timer that sets `isDraggable: true`" approach entirely. **What actually shipped** (`DashboardGrid.jsx`): a single delegated, capture-phase `touchstart` listener on the grid container calls `e.stopPropagation()` for every touch that starts on `.block-drag-handle` (except ones on `.react-resizable-handle`, left alone) — capture-phase on an *ancestor* is enough to keep the event from ever reaching `react-draggable`'s listener on the grid-item node itself, so `react-draggable` never sees a block-drag touch at all, on any device. In its place, a plain state machine: `touchstart` arms a 550ms timer (10px movement tolerance); if movement exceeds that before the timer fires, the gesture is abandoned untouched — no `preventDefault` was ever called, so the browser's native scroll (which never got blocked, since `touch-action` on `.block-drag-handle` is no longer unconditionally `none`) handles it exactly like touching any other part of the page. If the hold completes, the block is "armed" (`touch-action` switches to `none` **only** for that one block, and only via a class driven by React state — safe to flip at that exact moment because the finger has necessarily stayed still through the whole hold, so no native scroll has started yet to interrupt), and further finger movement drives a CSS `transform` "ghost drag" on an *inner* wrapper — not the grid-item root itself, which would silently lose the transform, since `GridItem.createStyle()` (confirmed in `node_modules/react-grid-layout`) overwrites `transform`/`width`/`height`/`position` on that exact node every render via `useCSSTransforms`. The real grid position is only computed and committed (with a manual bounds + overlap check, mirroring `preventCollision`) on release, through the same `onLayoutChange` contract `Dashboard.jsx` already had — no parent-side changes needed. Resize is untouched (still instant on the small corner handle; a touchstart there was never ambiguous with a scroll attempt the way dragging the whole card was). **Action Buttons**: `blockRegistry.js` entries can now set `chromeless: true`; `BlockRenderer.jsx` checks it and skips the card/header wrapper entirely for that block, rendering just the component's own content plus a small absolutely-positioned ✎/✕ overlay while editing (the only remaining way to configure/remove a block with no header row). `NavButtonBlock`/`StatusButtonBlock` were restyled onto a new `.btn-pill` class (always fully rounded, independent of the user's corner-style theme setting — the pill shape is the point) and `StatusButtonBlock` dropped the record-title line it used to show above the button, moving that context into the button's `title` tooltip instead so the block is genuinely just the button. **How to apply**: before wiring up ANY drag/touch library's `disabled`/`enabled` prop as something a timer flips mid-gesture, check whether that library resolves draggability once at gesture-start (most do, including this one) — if so, a timer-driven toggle is a dead end and the gesture needs to be intercepted and hand-rolled from the point of interception onward, not fought after the fact. Verified via Playwright/CDP `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` (isolated backend + synthetic brain, same methodology as every round this week): a quick swipe (touchstart → immediate large movement → touchend, all under 200ms) left the block's stored `layout.sm` completely unchanged; a held-then-moved sequence (700ms hold, then movement) both showed the `touch-drag-armed` class/transform appear mid-gesture and produced a real, persisted `layout.sm.x` change matching the expected column delta after "Save Layout." Both pill blocks confirmed to render with no `.card` ancestor and a `border-radius` over 9999px, and to keep the ✎/✕ overlay while editing. **Not verified, stated plainly**: the actual feel of a held touch on real iOS Safari — whether the hold reads as comfortable/too long/too short, and whether anything about this interacts with the standalone-PWA scroll-hijack history documented elsewhere in this file. That needs the owner's device, same as every mobile-PWA claim in this doc.

- **2026-08-05** — **Dashboard mobile editing went from "disabled" to full parity with desktop, and a new Action Buttons block category shipped, both from the same live interview.** Earlier the same day, the desktop grid/picker rework had deliberately left mobile drag/resize disabled pending the owner seeing the desktop work first — with it confirmed working, the owner asked for full touch drag/resize on mobile too, an explicit, informed choice made after being told plainly that this app has a real, four-times-repeated history of iOS-standalone-PWA-only touch/scroll bugs (`docs/MEMORY.md`'s own iOS-PWA entries) that Chromium/Playwright has never once been able to reproduce or verify a fix for. **What actually changed, mechanically**: the data model already had an independent `layout.sm` slot per block since Custom Dashboards shipped — `DashboardGrid.jsx` just never read it (hardcoded an auto-stack instead). Enabling it was `cols.sm: 2→12`, wiring `sm` to read/write its own saved layout the way `lg` already did, and dropping the `isMobile` gate on `isDraggable`/`isResizable`. `touch-action: none` on the drag/resize handles was added proactively as the standard mitigation for the touch/scroll ambiguity — **this cannot be verified by any tool available in this environment; a real on-device pass is still the open item**, stated plainly rather than implied-closed by a clean automated test. A second migration, `m012_rescale_dashboard_mobile_grid_units`, was needed because `sm` had never been read before — every stored value was a `w:2`-scale artifact (some untouched since before `m011` shipped, some blindly ×3'd BY `m011` itself since that migration rescaled whatever `layout.sm` it found without knowing it was dead data). Rather than guess which provenance applied to a given block, `m012` **normalizes** `x`/`w` to a fixed full-width value instead of multiplying — correct regardless of prior value, and (unlike `m011`) genuinely idempotent as a bonus property. **Action Buttons** (`nav_button`, `status_button`) is a new block category (`category: "action"`) letting a dashboard navigate to an app page/specific record, or change a task's status / archive a contact or asset / set an asset's own select-field value — all owner-required to be pick-based, never typed. The status/enum tension (contact `status` is genuinely freeform text, not an enum) was resolved by excluding it and using contact/asset **archive/unarchive** instead — the only zero-typing binary that exists there, and arguably the semantically-right "status" concept anyway. The security-critical call, validated by an independent Plan-agent review then spot-checked directly against the code: **no new unified write endpoint** — `status_button`'s click calls each target module's own existing, already-differently-gated endpoint directly from the browser (`tasksApi.update`, `contactsApi.archive`, `assetsApi.update`), so the dashboard's own access model and its `share_underlying_data` toggle are structurally never in the write path at all (see the new Core Design Decisions entry above for the full reasoning). `BlockPicker.jsx` gained one new generic mechanism to support this — `showIf: {key, equals}` conditional field visibility — needed because `status_button`'s task/contact/asset branches need different key sets, not just different values under one key; no new picker `kind`s were needed for the enums/toggles themselves, only two genuinely multi-stage ones (`moduleAndRecord` for nav targets, `assetSelectField` for the asset-field-value case). **A real gap found while building nav buttons, not before**: only 3 of the 7 modules with an existing record picker (Assets, Contacts, Finance) actually had a `useSearchParams` handler to land a deep link on — Tasks, Calendar, and Notes got the same handler added (mirroring `Assets.jsx`'s exact pattern), and Automations was scoped to module-page-only since it has no per-workflow detail view to land on yet. **How to apply**: when a new block or feature needs to *write* to another module's data, the reflex should be "which existing endpoint already does this correctly," not "build a generic action-runner" — the differing per-module access thresholds (contacts need strict `edit`, assets need the stricter `can_manage`) are exactly the kind of knowledge that rots the moment it's duplicated into a second place. Every claim in this entry — the endpoint gates, the deep-link coverage gap, the `record_ref_fields` sentinel limitation fixed alongside it — was verified against the actual code, not assumed from the plan. Full backend suite green (555 passed, 2 pre-existing unrelated `test_ai_usage_service.py` UTC-day-boundary flakes deselected — see that file's own known issue, not touched this session); both features live-verified end-to-end (isolated backend + synthetic brain + Playwright): a `status_button` click was confirmed to genuinely flip a task from `pending` to `done` with `completed_at` set via the real `PATCH /tasks/{id}` path, and a `nav_button` click was confirmed to land on the exact right asset's detail modal through the pre-existing deep-link mechanism.
- **2026-08-04** — **Custom Dashboards: standalone freeform dashboards fully replace the record-owned "Record-Scoped Dashboards" spec from the day before, plus a new access-bypass mechanic (`share_underlying_data`) that needed to be exactly right.** A live multi-round interview reframed a realtor's "one dashboard per client/property" request into a much bigger, general-purpose want: unlimited, user-created, standalone dashboard objects (like Notes/Assets — never owned by a Contact/Asset) built from a freeform draggable grid of typed blocks pulling live data from any module in the app, including Smart Home, Automations, AI Chat/Usage, and eventually admin-curated external sources. This **directly contradicted** the prior day's owner-approved spec (1:1 per-record, access inherited from the record, no independent sharing model) — flagged explicitly mid-design by a Plan-agent review, and the owner confirmed full supersession rather than a hybrid. **Core security rule**: a dashboard is a read-through view, never an access bypass — every block re-resolves the *current viewer's own* access to whatever it points at, through that module's existing gate (`find_asset`, `find_contact`, `_resolve_book_access`, `find_note_store`, etc.), never the dashboard owner's. **The one deliberate, owner-requested exception**: a per-dashboard `share_underlying_data` toggle (owner-only to set, default off) that renders every block *as the dashboard owner* would see it for anyone the dashboard is shared with — direct owner ask, in response to "hide admin-only blocks... but let me share the data anyway if I want to." Implemented as a single two-pass function (`services/dashboard_blocks/render.py`: resolve as viewer → if that fails AND the toggle is on AND the viewer isn't the owner AND they have dashboard-level read access, re-resolve the SAME resolver as the owner's identity) rather than per-block-type special-casing, so it structurally can never show a viewer more than the owner independently can see — if the owner's own access is later revoked, the block locks for every shared viewer too, automatically, with no extra code. Admin-only block types (e.g. an "AI Usage — All Users" overview) get a registry-level `admin_only` gate (hidden from the block picker, greyed out if already present) **plus** an independent `is_admin` check inside the resolver itself — both matter, because Pass 2 of the toggle re-runs the resolver as the *owner's* identity, and a non-admin owner (with an admin-only block added via an edit-share) must never have it render just because the toggle is on. Verified with a dedicated test matrix in `tests/test_dashboards_service.py` (16 tests) covering every branch: no access regardless of toggle, toggle off, toggle on with/without owner access, admin-only with an admin vs. non-admin owner, viewer-is-owner (toggle never consulted). **`owner` is stored explicitly on every dashboard record** (unlike Assets/Finance, where ownership = store location) — this is load-bearing for pool dashboards, which have no store-location owner otherwise; `share_underlying_data`'s Pass 2 needs a specific named identity to re-resolve as, and pool dashboards would have nothing to re-resolve as without it. **Block-resolver registry pattern**: a `BlockSpec` dataclass (type, category, resolver fn, `admin_only`, `record_ref_fields`) in a single `REGISTRY` dict, with `record_ref_fields` (e.g. `{"asset_id": "assets"}`) letting `dashboard_index.py`'s reverse "referenced by" lookup work generically off every block's declared config keys — the indexer never hardcodes per-block-type logic. **Migration `m010_seed_home_dashboards`** seeds one "Home" dashboard per existing user × workspace, replicating today's fixed Dashboard.jsx widgets as blocks, and sets it as their `default_dashboard_id` (new workspace-keyed field on the auth record, same shape as `shortcuts`) — paired with a **self-healing floor-of-one rule**: a user's last dashboard in a workspace can never be deleted and is automatically their default, computed at read/delete time rather than a stored "protected" flag. **Deliberately deferred** (sequenced last in the build plan, not cut): dashboard templates, the "referenced by" UI hooks on non-Assets/Contacts view pages, Module Engagement and External Data block types (both need new backend infra — an event counter and an SSRF-hardened fetch service respectively), and time-series trend blocks (Net Worth/Spending/Completion Stats — `finance_reports.py` only computes single-period snapshots today, no history endpoint exists yet). **Known risk, not yet verified**: the new `react-grid-layout` grid is fresh surface area in the same mobile-shell region (`Layout.jsx`) that has a well-documented, repeatedly-recurring history of iOS-standalone-PWA-only bugs invisible to Chromium/Playwright — drag/resize was deliberately disabled below the `sm` breakpoint (mobile gets a read-only stacked view) specifically to shrink that risk surface, but this has NOT been confirmed on a real device yet. **How to apply**: when a later design pass directly contradicts a very-recently-approved spec for the same feature name, don't silently pick one — surface the conflict explicitly (a Plan-agent review is a good way to catch this) and get an explicit supersession decision before writing code, even under time pressure to keep interviewing. Full backend suite 553 green (16 new), frontend `vite build` + `eslint` clean.
- **2026-08-03 (second follow-up)** — **Two owner-reported bugs from the photo/career-history follow-up, fixed same day.** (1) `AffiliationEditor`'s `add()`/`remove()` in `ContactModal.jsx` destructured the link/unlink endpoint's response as `const [updated] = await contactsApi.linkAffiliation(...)` — but `POST/DELETE /contacts/{id}/affiliations/{other_id}` returns the updated contact **object directly** (`routers/contacts.py` unpacks the service's `(a, b)` tuple and returns only `a`), not an array. Array-destructuring a plain object throws (objects aren't iterable), silently swallowed by the surrounding `catch {}` — the link/unlink always succeeded server-side, the UI just never found out, so the new pill only appeared after closing and reopening the whole modal (a fresh `GET` re-fetches the real state). Fixed to `const updated = await ...`. **How to apply:** when a service function returns a tuple but the router only forwards one element of it, the frontend caller must match the router's actual response shape, not the service's — this class of mismatch won't surface in backend tests (which call the service directly) or a raw `curl` smoke test (which prints the body but nothing destructures it), only in the real frontend code path. (2) `ContactModal`/`ContactDetail`'s `fullPage` mode (used for the Profile page) had no equivalent of the mobile-footer bottom spacer already added to `Contacts.jsx`'s list — the modal-card version was never affected (it's a floating overlay with its own `max-height`/scroll), but `fullPage` renders as normal page content inside `<main>`, which the fixed mobile footer nav floats over. Added the same `h-20 md:hidden` spacer to both. (3) The Contacts list's per-row icon never showed an uploaded photo (person/company/gender emoji only) — extracted the photo-fetch-as-blob logic (duplicated three times across `ContactDetail`, `ContactModal`'s `PhotoUploader`, and now needed a fourth time) into a shared `components/contacts/ContactAvatar.jsx` (`useContactPhotoUrl()` hook + `<ContactAvatar>` component), used by all three call sites plus the list row. **Why blob-fetch and not a plain `<img src="/api/v1/contacts/{id}/photo">`:** auth here is a cookie the browser would send automatically, but the endpoint's access resolution also depends on the `X-Workspace` header, which a plain `<img>` tag can't set — a self-contact resolves fine either way (cross-workspace short-circuit), but a business-only regular contact viewed while `<img>` silently defaults to "personal" would 404. Relaunched via `bash launch.sh --skip-build` (frontend-only changes; `dist/` is a bind-mounted volume, not baked into the image, so a full image rebuild was never necessary — `--skip-build` still restarts the container to pick up the fresh build cleanly).
- **2026-08-03 (follow-up)** — **Self-contact sharing capped below edit; Work & Career became a real resume; several free-text fields became pickers; photo upload added; profile opens full-page.** A second pass on the same-day Profile/Contacts merge, driven by direct owner feedback rather than a fresh interview (small, mostly-independent asks handled with reasonable defaults instead of another interview round; two genuinely schema-defining ones — career history shape, full-page trigger scope — got a focused 3-question `AskUserQuestion` round first). **Locked decisions**: (1) self-contact sharing is capped at read/contribute — `update_access()` rejects any `shared_with` entry with `access: "edit"` when `self_of` is set, so literally nobody but the owner can ever be granted edit on their own contact, addressing "make sure a user's contact can't be edited by another user" directly rather than relying only on the pre-existing owner-always-edit resolution behavior; (2) career history is a **full resume list** (not a single current/past toggle) — `career_history: [{id, title, company_id, industry, education, years_experience, skills, start_date, end_date, archived}]`, validated as a whole array, managed client-side (mark current archived + set end_date, append a fresh entry), superseding the flat `employer`/`industry`/`education`/`years_experience`/`skills` fields entirely; (3) full-page profile applies **only to the viewer's own profile** — someone else's self-contact shared with you still opens in the normal modal card, keeping the change scoped to `pages/Profile.jsx` + a `fullPage` prop on `ContactDetail`/`ContactModal` rather than a new per-contact route. **Reasonable defaults chosen without asking** (stated plainly, not hidden): height/weight/education/experience/blood-type "pickers" are `<select>` dropdowns and native `<input type="time">`/`type="number">`, not custom scroll-wheel widgets — this codebase has no scroll-wheel-picker precedent anywhere and building one would be exactly the kind of over-engineering SOUL.md pushes back on for a UI need dropdowns already satisfy; height/weight are stored canonically as `height_cm`/`weight_kg` with a separate `height_unit`/`weight_unit` display preference (ft/in or cm; lbs or kg) rather than two parallel unit-specific fields; `employer` became a `ContactPicker` bound to the **pre-existing but previously unused** `company_id` field rather than a new field. **Structural pieces**: `phones` changed from `list[str]` to `list[{country_code, number, extension}]` (digits-only, capped lengths) — `_validate_phones()` still accepts a bare string for backward compat (legacy data, CSV import, automation) and wraps it, so nothing else needed to change; `emails` gained real format validation (`_validate_emails()`), rejecting the whole save (400) on an invalid address instead of silently storing it — direct owner ask ("make email not able to save unless a valid email is entered"). Photo upload (`POST/GET/DELETE /contacts/{id}/photo`) mirrors the existing `/auth/me/background` pattern exactly (content-type allowlist, 5 MB cap, one file per record) and applies to **any** contact with edit access, not just self-contacts, consistent with "make each contact a profile in a way." Default icon now varies by `gender` (👨/👩) when no photo is set, person/company unaffected. **A real, uncaught-by-pytest bug found by the live HTTP smoke test (not the unit suite)**: the photo upload endpoint 500'd on first use because `Contacts/photos/` doesn't exist until something writes into it — `write_json()` auto-creates parent dirs for the JSON stores, but raw `Path.write_bytes()` does not; fixed with an explicit `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` before the write, verified by a byte-for-byte upload/fetch round-trip afterward. **How to apply**: any new raw-file (not `write_json`/`write_markdown`) write path under a Brain subdirectory needs its own explicit `mkdir` — don't assume the atomic-write helpers' auto-mkdir behavior extends to direct file I/O. 12 new `contacts_service` tests (career history add/archive, gender/height/weight/blood-type/time validation, phone wrapping + country-code/extension, email accept/reject, photo set/clear, sharing-cap rejection), full suite 539 green; frontend build/eslint clean; every new mechanism (sharing cap, photo round-trip, career archive-and-new-role, structured phone/email validation) re-verified live over HTTP against a running instance, not just pytest. Also fixed in the same pass: the Contacts list's last row being hidden behind the mobile footer nav (explicit bottom spacer, mobile-only) and the contact editor scrolling horizontally (`.modal-card` gained `overflow-x-hidden`, matching the same convention `<main>` in `Layout.jsx` already uses) — both pre-existing, unrelated to the schema work, fixed because they were reported in the same turn.
- **2026-08-03** — **Profile is retired as its own file/form — every user's Profile IS now their own Contact record (a "self-contact"), and every Contact got the same read-first card treatment.** Closes a 2026-07-30 backlog item, scoped through a multi-round live interview (owner explicitly asked not to have anything assumed) plus a Plan-agent audit that surfaced real structural risks before any code was written. **Locked decisions**: (1) one unified record, not two synced ones — Contacts is the single source of truth, Profile.jsx becomes a thin view into it; (2) sensitive fields (health, finances, AI preferences, daily routine) are **always private, never shareable**, regardless of what sharing is configured on the contact — basic fields (name, email, phone, tags, birthday, address, occupation, etc.) follow normal Contacts sharing; (3) **one self-contact per user, shared across BOTH workspaces** — a deliberate exception to the otherwise-universal per-workspace split, physically stored in the **personal** store; (4) `priority_order` is the one field that keeps its per-workspace split, stored workspace-keyed (`{"personal":[...], "business":[...]}`) inside the unified record; (5) only the owning user ever sees their own contact pinned in their own list — no "everyone's self-contacts" view; (6) removed the embedded Goals page, `big_goal`, and `savings_goal` from the old Profile (all three were redundant with the real Goals module); (7) `partner`/`children` free-text fields became a **general, bidirectional `affiliated_contact_ids`** mechanism (not partner/children-typed) — deliberately generalized so it substantially satisfies the separately-backlogged company↔person affiliation-linking item too; (8) the card shows everything populated with no manual collapse — an explicit, accepted tradeoff that a fully-filled-out profile may scroll; (9) CRM features (Deals/Pipeline/Interactions) apply to self-contacts with no special-casing; (10) `GET/PATCH /contacts/me` are gated by **login only, not `require_module("contacts")`** — Profile never had a module gate before this merge (guests included), so this preserves that guarantee rather than silently taking a user's own profile away when Contacts is disabled for them. **Structural guarantees, not UI convention**: `self_of`/`affiliated_contact_ids` are never accepted through the public `ContactCreate`/`ContactUpdate` models — only `create_self_contact()` and the dedicated affiliation endpoints can set them; `_strip_private()` runs inside both `annotate()` **and** `find_contact()` (the agent's `get_contact` tool reads `find_contact()`'s raw tuple without calling `annotate()` — stripping only in one place would have left a real leak there); `update_contact(..., viewer=)` strips private-field keys from any incoming PATCH where the acting viewer isn't the contact's own owner, closing a blind-write path where an edit-level sharee could inject data into fields only the owner will ever read back; `update_access()` explicitly rejects adding a self-contact's own `self_of` user to `hidden_from` as defense-in-depth on top of `resolve_access()`'s existing (already-correct) owner-always-edit rule; `delete_contact()`/`set_archived()`/`transfer_ownership()` all reject outright on a `self_of` record. **The one correctness fix that had to ship with this, not after**: `user_deletion_service._contacts_eligible()` now skips `self_of` contacts — without it, a self-contact that was ever shared would surface in the admin deletion-review page needing a transfer/delete decision, but the new guards above would then raise mid-`execute()`, after other transfers in the same non-transactional batch had already run; verified live (HTTP smoke test) that deleting a user whose shared self-contact previously required a decision now completes cleanly with an empty `eligible_items` list. **Cross-workspace mechanics**: `list_visible_contacts()`/`find_contact()` short-circuit on the viewer's own self-contact regardless of the `workspace` param (other users' self-contacts are NOT made cross-workspace-visible — a deliberately narrow scope, known limitation); a self-contact's Interactions/Deals are anchored to `effective_ws = "personal"` in the router (`_effective_ws(contact, workspace)`) regardless of the active tab, so logging an interaction from the business tab doesn't create an invisible parallel store — verified live that an interaction logged under `X-Workspace: business` is visible under `X-Workspace: personal` on the same self-contact. **Migration** `m009_migrate_profiles_to_self_contacts` is idempotent per-user (skips if a self-contact already exists — safe against the lazy `create_if_missing=True` auto-creation path racing it, since migrations run in `main.py`'s lifespan before the app accepts any request) and never touches/deletes the old `profile.json`/`Profile.md`; verified live against a synthetic legacy `profile.json` that field mappings (`dob→birthday`, `phone→phones[0]`, flat `priority_order`→`priority_order.personal`) land correctly. **A real bug the pytest suite didn't catch but a live HTTP smoke test did**: `ContactCreate.model_dump()` (no `exclude_unset`) sends every optional field including the new `priority_order: None` default on every create call, which crashed `_validate_priority_order(None)` — pytest's unit tests never hit this because they call `contacts_service.create_contact()` directly with hand-built dicts that never include an explicit `None` for that key; fixed by checking `data.get("priority_order") is not None` instead of `"priority_order" in data`. **How to apply**: (a) when merging two mature modules, run the actual HTTP path end-to-end before calling it done — a Pydantic model's `model_dump()` behavior on unset-vs-explicit-None fields is a real gap unit tests calling service functions directly won't surface; (b) any future "one record needs to behave differently under two workspace contexts" need should follow the `effective_ws` anchoring pattern here, not invent a new one — this is exactly the trap flagged for the separately-planned Record-Scoped Dashboards feature's `Dashboard/instances.json` storage. Compatibility with that feature was designed in up front (documented in the approved plan) since it was being scoped the same day: Dashboards need zero special-casing for self-contacts, inherit access for free through `find_contact()`, and should apply the same `effective_ws` check for any dashboard instance keyed to a self-contact's `record_id`. 6 new `contacts_service` tests plus the write-guard regression test (527 total, full suite green); frontend `vite build` and `eslint` both clean (only pre-existing prop-types-style warnings, same convention as the rest of the codebase).
- **2026-08-02** — **Notes' admin-pool-management bug was frontend-only; audited the other 3 sharing modules and only Notes had it. Added a minimal archive flag to Notes as a standalone feature (no delete-restriction attached).** Owner reported admins couldn't edit/delete notes shared into the household/team pool. Root cause: `resolve_access()` in `notes_service.py` already returns `"edit"` for `is_admin` on pool notes (correct, unchanged) — the bug was `Notes.jsx`'s `ContextMenu` computing `own = !node._owner`, which treats EVERY non-owned item (both pool items AND a personal share-to-you) as "not mine, offer Leave," hiding Rename/Move/Share/Delete from admins on pool items entirely. Fixed by adding an explicit `isPool` check (`_owner === 'household' || 'team'`) and gating management on `own || (isPool && _access === 'edit')`, matching the pattern Assets/Finance/Contacts already use. **Audited the other 3 modules on request — none had the bug**: `AssetModal.jsx`/`AssetView.jsx` already split `isForeign`/`isPool` and gate via `_access`; `BookSettings.jsx`/`Finance.jsx` gate the Settings-modal entry point (which holds Delete book) behind `canEdit = _access === 'edit'`; `Contacts.jsx` uses `canEdit = _access === 'edit'` directly with no `_owner`-based blanket check at all. **How to apply**: when auditing a module for this class of bug, check whether pool ownership is distinguished from a generic "foreign" owner before gating management UI — never gate on "is `_owner` set" alone. Owner also asked for pool hard-delete to require 2+ admins to agree (with a single-admin fallback and an approval mechanic to design) — scoped via interview, then **explicitly dropped mid-interview** ("dont add the 2 admin approval... No change to hard-delete"). Net: pool hard-delete behavior is unchanged everywhere; only the Notes frontend bug got fixed. Separately, the owner kept wanting a minimal archive flag for Notes (parity with Assets/Finance/Contacts, which already archive) as a standalone feature, explicitly NOT tied to any delete restriction: `Notes/_archive.json` sidecar, `set_archived()`/`load_archived()`, `list_notes()`/`list_visible_notes()` gain `include_archived` (default `False`, always annotates `archived: bool`), new `POST /notes/archive` (edit-level gate, same as delete). `transfer_ownership()` (from the 2026-07-31 user-deletion feature) was extended to carry archived-path entries across a move, matching how it already carries `_shares.json` entries. **Same-day correction**: initially built per-node (mirroring Assets' explicit "archive doesn't cascade" rule), but the owner clarified they wanted it to cascade like *sharing* instead — `_is_archived_path()` now walks up ancestors the same way `_entry_for_path` does for shares, so archiving a folder archives everything inside it. A child can't be individually un-archived while its parent stays archived — intentional, matches the same limitation sharing already has. **How to apply**: Notes has two different precedents for "does an action on a folder cascade" — sharing cascades (ancestor-walk), Assets' archive doesn't (strictly per-node); don't assume one pattern carries to a new feature without checking which one the requester actually means. 8 new tests, suite 516 green (2 pre-existing unrelated `test_ai_usage_service.py` UTC-day-boundary flakes, documented since 2026-07-30, confirmed unrelated to any file touched here).
- **2026-07-31** — **Admin user-deletion is a review-and-decide flow, not a blind cascade delete — and pool destinations require a share→contributor conversion, not a straight field carry-over.** Scoped via a live interview (the departing-user's Brain folder was already fully `rmtree`'d on delete — the real, already-tracked bug was dangling references left in *other* stores' `shared_with`/`contributors`/`hidden_from`, never cleaned up). Locked decisions: (1) the review only shows items the user *owns* that are *already shared* — unshared items still die silently with the account; (2) a tree/book/folder always moves as one atomic unit, no splitting a subtree across destinations; (3) every eligible item needs an explicit decision (transfer-to-user / transfer-to-pool / delete) before deletion proceeds, enforced server-side (the execute endpoint recomputes eligibility fresh, never trusts the client); (4) existing shares to OTHER people survive a transfer unchanged for a named-user destination. **Real conflict found mid-build** (by a Plan-agent code audit, not assumed): pool items (`_household`/`_team`) never read `shared_with` at all — only `contributors` — so "shares survive transfer" would be false-in-practice for a pool destination. Resolved: pool transfers auto-convert each `shared_with` entry into an equivalent `contributors` entry (same `target`/`access`/`caps`, dropping the accept-handshake `accepted` key, since pool grants need no handshake). (5) new-owner gets one batched notification per delete-run, not one per item; (6) references to the departing user as a *recipient* elsewhere are stripped automatically, no admin decision, and shown read-only on the review page for visibility; (7) bare `DELETE /auth/admin/users/{id}` now 409s when eligible items exist — no silent bypass. **Cross-module stale pointers are explicitly out of scope** (Asset `contact` field, Deal `linked_asset_ids`, Invoice `contact_id`) — left as-is per the app's existing stale-id tolerance, tracked as a `docs/TASKS.md` follow-up rather than attempted here. Implementation: `transfer_ownership()` added to all 4 sharing-capable module services (Assets/Finance/Contacts/Notes) as a sibling to Assets' `convert_to_pool()` — same physical-move pattern, but preserves share fields instead of stripping them; `services/user_deletion_service.py` orchestrates preview/validate/execute, "destroy last" ordering (transfers/deletes → reference-cleanup scan → index rebuild → notify → only then delete the account). 15 new tests, full suite 510 green. **How to apply:** the `transfer_ownership()` pattern is the precedent for any future "move ownership between stores" need; never assume a pool item honors `shared_with` — check `_resolve_book_access`/`resolve_access`/`list_visible`'s pool branch before assuming an access field carries the same meaning across personal and pool storage.
- **2026-07-30** — **AI usage metering is instrumented at the `ai_provider.py` choke point, not per-caller; automation/n8n AI spend is architecturally invisible and deliberately not tracked.** Every AI call in the backend funnels through exactly two functions, `chat_completion()`/`agent_completion()` in `services/ai_provider.py` — confirmed by grep, no direct SDK usage exists anywhere else. Both now take `user_name`/`workspace` kwargs and call `ai_usage_service.record_tokens()` internally right after a successful provider response, so every current AND future call site inherits metering automatically instead of each caller having to remember to record it. There are exactly 3 real call sites that spend tokens today: `POST /chat` (via `run_agent`'s tool loop), `POST /chat/save-memory`, and custom AI-powered suggestions (`suggestions_service._run_custom_async` — the single dispatch point reached by the scheduler, the `POST /suggestions/{id}/run` button, AND the chat agent's own nested `run_suggestion` tool call). `daily_digest`/`overdue_alert`/`weekly_review`/`goal_drift` are pure templated string formatting, not AI calls, despite older docs implying otherwise. **Automation (n8n) AI usage cannot be counted**: `n8n_service.write_n8n_env()` only forwards `N8N_`-prefixed env vars into `docker/n8n.env` (a deliberate 2026-07-19 security fix, see rule above) — if an admin opts an Anthropic key back in via `N8N_ENV_ALLOWLIST` or n8n's own credential store, workflow nodes call the provider **directly from inside the n8n container**, never touching Python. There is no callback path today; owner decision was to skip automation tracking entirely rather than fake a placeholder. **Storage** (`brain/_system/ai_usage.json`, never in a user's portable Brain folder) is **daily buckets only** — `{defaults: {period, message_limit, token_limit, warn_pct}, users: {name: {period, mode, message_limit, token_limit, soft_accepted_period, alert_state, days: {YYYY-MM-DD: {personal:{messages,input_tokens,output_tokens}, business:{...}}}}}}` — with daily/weekly(ISO)/monthly totals always derived on read by summing the right date range, matching Finance's "derived values computed on read" rule. This means a user's cap **period** (daily/weekly/monthly, picked per-user) needs zero migration to change, and the already-scoped future demo per-user daily cap needs no new schema. **Cap model**: mode (`off`/`soft`/`hard`) is per-user only, defaulting to `off` (unlimited) for everyone; message/token limits are a global default with per-user override; a cap check sums personal+business combined (one human, one budget) even though the display keeps them split. Hard blocks chat + save-memory + suggestions outright; soft only interactively gates `/chat` (a "Continue anyway?" confirm card) since save-memory and scheduler-triggered suggestions have no UI to confirm through — once accepted, the acceptance holds for the rest of that period. Warn/over notifications reuse `suggestions_service.notify_user()` and dedup via `alert_state[period_key]` exactly like `finance_planning_service`'s budget-alert escalation (none→warn→over, once each). **How to apply:** any new AI-consuming feature that calls `chat_completion`/`agent_completion` gets usage tracking and cap enforcement for free from the choke point — the only thing a new caller must add is its own `ai_usage_service.check_usage()` gate before calling and `record_message()` after, if it should count as a distinct "message."
- **2026-07-20** — **Notification-channel rotation ships as the revocation mechanism; ntfy publisher auth is worth building only once ntfy is actually exposed.** Grew out of an owner support conversation: ntfy channel IDs are the only lock on a user's notification stream (no ntfy-side login), so `rotate_notification_channel()` + a monthly staleness reminder (`run_channel_rotation_reminders`, 30-day window, dedup via `channel_reminder_at`, resets on real rotation) gives users a way to retire a leaked ID without an account system. Initially planned to also harden ntfy itself (read-only default + authenticated publisher) preemptively, then deferred that after the parallel security-hardening session bound ntfy to `127.0.0.1` — reasoned the exposure surface was closed. **That reasoning was wrong**: the `tunnel` service runs `network_mode: host`, so it shares the host's loopback and can still reach `127.0.0.1:5680` — a Cloudflare Public Hostname mapped to `http://localhost:5680` re-exposes ntfy to the internet regardless of the loopback bind (see the matching Known Gotchas entry). Owner confirmed intent to map a public hostname, so the ntfy auth hardening was built after all (`provision_ntfy_auth()` in `launch.sh`, rule 12 above). **How to apply:** when a "loopback-only" binding is used to reason about whether a bundled service is exposed, check whether anything in the stack shares the host network namespace before concluding the exposure is actually closed.
- **2026-07-18** — **Full-circle linking round 2: many-to-one pointers live on the many side; rollups are derived scans; cross-module writes stay best-effort-guarded.** A deal bills **many invoices**, so `deal_id` lives ON the invoice (public `InvoiceCreate` field) and the deal's invoice list is `list_invoices_for_deal()` — a scan over `list_visible_books` skipping contribute books without `see_balances` (same rule as `_require_full_read`); the old `deal.invoice_id` is legacy, never written. Payment transactions carry `deal_id` (internal-only on tx, like `invoice_id`) and auto-link the deal's asset ONLY when exactly one is linked (`_deal_single_asset`, try/except-guarded like `_suggest_payee_contact` — Finance must work without Contacts). `contacts_service.find_deal()` is the deal-by-id gate (access inherits from the parent contact — deals never grow their own sharing). Asset templates gained a `contact` field type (stores the id, no cross-store existence check — UI validates at pick time, stale ids render "(contact)"); the reverse listing (`list_assets_for_contact`) filters the viewer's `list_visible` by contact-type fields — never a stored index. **Invoice status is a free dropdown** (any of draft/sent/paid/void anytime); the payment auto-flip (full→paid, removal→sent) still applies after manual changes. Job P&L (invoiced/collected/asset-expenses/net) is computed server-side in the contact finance view per deal — the deal panel is the job dashboard; the invoice shows context only. **How to apply:** new many-to-one links follow this shape (pointer on the many side, derived scan for the list); cross-module lookups inside another module's write path must be guarded best-effort, never load-bearing.
- **2026-07-18** — **Cross-module links are one-way pointers with viewer-scoped reads (Deal→Asset, Transaction→Asset, Deal→Invoice).** Deals carry `linked_asset_ids` (link/unlink is a dedicated mutation, NOT part of `_validate_deal` — an unrelated deal PATCH can never clobber links; gated at **contribute** like deal create/advance; the asset only needs to be *visible* to the linker via `assets_service.find_asset`, mirroring how Tasks link assets). Finance transactions carry `asset_id` (user-settable via the public Pydantic models, unlike internal-only `invoice_id`/`client_id`; whitelisted in `add_transaction`/`update_transaction`, `bulk_add` always None — imports never originate from an asset pick). **Cross-module reads never get their own access system**: `list_transactions_for_asset()` iterates `list_visible_books()` and applies the same contribute-caps rule as the tx-list endpoint (own entries only without `see_all_tx`) — same doctrine as the contact money view. **Deal→invoice stays human-in-the-loop**: 🧾 passes query params, the user picks the book, InvoiceModal pre-seeds via the existing find-or-create-client-by-`contact_id` path, and only a confirmed save writes `deal.invoice_id` back (reserved field, now populated). **How to apply:** new cross-module references follow this shape — plain id field on the referencing record, dedicated link/unlink mutation, reads resolved through the *viewer's* existing access gate on the referenced module, never a parallel one.
- **2026-07-18** — **Setup wizard asks nothing about Life Priorities.** Owner decision (first hosted-beta onboarding): the wizard is a single About You page; priorities are seeded with the defaults and edited on the Profile page. Don't reintroduce a priorities step in setup — onboarding friction beats scoring precision, and the Profile page + Getting Started checklist cover discovery. (Supersedes the 2026-07-14 ↑/↓-reorder + Skip wizard work.)
- **2026-07-17** — **Help system: one authored source, read by the page AND the AI. Help is NOT a module.** Authored content lives in `app/backend/content/help.json` (shipped/versioned, not user-editable Brain data) so it stays correct for the running version and can't be tampered with per-instance. `services/help_service.py` serves it three ways: the Help page (`GET /help/content` → `pages/Help.jsx`), the module-page ⓘ buttons (deep-link `/help#<id>`), and the AI (read-only `get_help` tool in `_READ_TOOLS`/`_RESEARCH_TOOLS` + a `capabilities_index` injected into the chat system prompt so the AI proactively points users to the right module and cites `/help#<section>`). Help is a **hardcoded footer nav entry like Settings** (no `require_module`, no `ALL_MODULES` entry, unguarded route) so every user sees it. **Feedback delivery was deliberately left out** (backend has zero mail infra) — the page just instructs users to email support@logcoretech.com with `mailto:` buttons; a `SUPPORT_WEBHOOK_URL` relay is the future path. **What's-New broadcast**: `whats_new_service.announce_if_updated()` at boot notifies every user's inbox once on a version bump that has a matching `whats_new` entry (fresh installs set a silent baseline; idempotent), and `get_banner()` drives a `WHATS_NEW_DAYS`-day banner. **How to apply**: keep `help.json` current when a module's UX changes and add a `whats_new` entry each release (the Stop hook nudges both); never fold Help into the module system.
- **2026-07-14** — **Contacts (CRM) is a standalone module; the Contact is the canonical person/company.** New module `contacts` (both workspaces, member-on/guest-off via **m008**), NOT folded into Finance. Finance payees and invoice clients both **link to a Contact** (`payee_contact_id` on transactions; book clients populate the reserved `contact_id`; the invoice client form was replaced by a contact picker — no legacy migration since Finance was still owner-testing). Storage `ws_path/Contacts/{contacts,interactions,deals,pipeline}.json` + instance-level `_system/contact_fields.json` (admin custom fields, Asset-template pattern) + pool stores in `_household`/`_team`. **Sharing mirrors finance/assets** (`contacts_service.resolve_access`: read/contribute/edit, by-name overrides group, `hidden_from` beats shares, personal = accept handshake, pool = contributors no-handshake) routed through `contacts_index.py` (disposable cache, warmed at boot). **contribute = log interactions + create/advance deals only** (never edit-core/delete/reshare — enforced in the router). **Security decisions:** the n8n automation API is **write-focused with a single-contact dedup lookup — NO list/export** (a leaked token can't dump PII); agent reads free, writes approval-gated, and **create tools search first** (`find_match`) to avoid duplicates; the contact "money view" (`GET /contacts/{id}/finance`) is computed against the **viewer's own finance access** and never exposes books they can't see. Deals stage "Won" (case-insensitive) is terminal; "Create invoice from deal" links to Finance (deep-prefill deferred). **How to apply:** any future person/org reference links to a Contact; new automation surfaces on PII default to write-only + single lookup.
- **2026-07-14** — **Goals are never auto-deleted; they carry a mandatory target date.** `recurring_service.process_user` skips `type in ("recurring","goal")` so completed goals persist in `tasks.json` (the Goals "Done" view); a **Clear completed** button archives them to history (`task_service.cleanup_done_goals` / `POST /tasks/goals/cleanup`). The Goals page filters by a **timeline window** (Today/Week/Month/Quarter/Year, bucketed by `due_date`; legacy dateless → "Undated") and the "X of Y" progress reflects the window. **Creating a goal requires a `due_date`** (`TaskCreateBase.goal_requires_due_date` validator + TaskModal); the chat agent is instructed to ask for a date and never invent one. **How to apply:** goal lifecycle is user-controlled, not swept.
- **2026-07-14** — **Bundled n8n runs only when needed (backend-controlled).** `n8n_service.reconcile()` starts `logcore-n8n` on the first stored workflow, stops it when the last is gone AND no external instance, and stops it when an **external** n8n is attached (URL not in `_BUNDLED_HOSTS`); an admin **force_on** flag (in `n8n_config.json`) keeps it up. Triggers: `POST /n8n/config` save, workflow import, workflow delete, + a boot reconcile (`job_n8n_reconcile`, boot+100s). Container control uses the Docker socket (same mechanism as `restart_n8n`/tunnel); all container ops are try/excepted so dev/test without Docker never breaks. `save_config` now **merges** (preserves force_on). **How to apply:** don't assume the bundled n8n is always up; gate on `is_configured()`/`reconcile()`.
- **2026-07-14** — **Notes sharing = sidecar index, folder-cascade, keeps .md portable.** Share metadata lives in `Notes/_shares.json` per store per workspace (`{shares: {"<path>": {shared_with[], hidden_from[], contributors[]}}}`), never in the note files. A share on a folder **cascades to its subtree** (`_entry_for_path` walks ancestors, most-specific wins). Pool notes live in `_household`/`_team` Notes stores. read/contribute/edit (contribute = edit content), personal = handshake, pool = contributors; `notes_index.py` routes cross-store visibility (warmed at boot). Router routes **every** read/write through `find_note_store` access resolution (`_validate_path` rejects traversal before touching disk). `list_notes` gained `create_default` (False for pool/foreign stores so we never write a Getting Started note into someone else's store). **How to apply:** notes content stays plain .md; sharing/metadata is always a sidecar, never frontmatter.
- **2026-07-14** — **Finance default categories + tax buckets are workspace-aware.** `finance_service.default_categories(ws)` / `default_tax_categories(ws)` — personal keeps Groceries+Salary (existing tests depend on it) and gets more income options; business gets accounting categories + Schedule-C tax buckets. Recurring bills + planned one-offs now carry `deductible`/`tax_category`; `match_bill` propagates them onto the matched ledger tx. **Default priority "God" → "Religion"** in every auto-generation source (existing users' saved priorities untouched; `Religion` added to `CATEGORY_COLORS`, `God` kept for back-compat).
- **2026-07-13** — **Finance module COMPLETE (all 5 phases).** Additional locked rules from the build: (1) **`month_end()` for month ranges** — never fabricate `-31` date strings (`2026-02-31` raises; bit us in monthly report/P&L/budget status). (2) **Finance never consults `pool_edit`** — pool book write grants are explicit per-book `contributors` entries (finance is too sensitive for a blanket grant). (3) **Caps enforcement is server-side only**: `_strip_balances()` on responses, `created_by` filter on tx lists, sign gating on writes, `_require_full_read()` on reports/planning/invoicing reads, net-worth skip — never render-only. (4) **Invoice `status` stores only user lifecycle**; totals/paid/balance/overdue always derived (`annotate_invoice`), auto-`paid` at zero balance, reopen on payment delete. (5) Finance share-routing uses `finance_index.py` (disposable derived cache, same philosophy as assets_index/RAG rule). (6) Planning/invoicing services re-load books via `finance_service.fresh_book()` — never validate against caller-held dicts (stale-dict bug class, hit twice). (7) Bank sync + CSV + manual writes all funnel through `finance_planning_service.on_transactions_added()` (bill matching + budget alerts) — new import paths must call it.
- **2026-07-12** — **Finance module locked in (Phase A shipped; plan approved for B–E).** Interviewed owner → decisions: SimpleFIN bank sync from day one but **admin-managed** (users request a connection, admins enter the setup token in an Admin → Bank Connections card; token stored per-user at `USERS/{name}/Finance/simplefin.json`, read-only by protocol, never in git/logs); accounting (invoices/clients-AR/tax/receipts) folded into this module, **CRM stays a separate future module** (clients carry a reserved `contact_id` link); asset-style sharing at BOTH levels (book + per-account overrides) with contribute caps (default = employee expense-submission: add expenses only, no balances, own entries only); projected balance from recurring+planned items with **deviation alerts** (fraud detection). Hard rules: **money = signed integer cents** (sign is the type, no floats, `Decimal` at import boundaries); **derived values always computed on read** (balances, reports, AR, projections — only bank-reported `synced_balance_cents` and alert-dedup state are stored); **transactions shard per book per year** (`transactions_{YYYY}.json`, cross-year date edit moves shards); **`_resolve_book_access()` is the single access gate** for routers AND agent tools — extend it in Phase E, never bypass; **personal books invisible to admins**; **guest role gets finance disabled by default** (m007 + `_guest_map`); pool books do NOT consult `pool_edit` — finance pool write grants will be explicit per-book contributors (Phase E). Full plan: `~/.claude/plans/before-building-a-workflow-valiant-willow.md`.
- **2026-07-12** — **Automation Inbox lives INSIDE the Automations module — no new module.** Owner weighed a standalone module vs folding it in; folded in because the page already workspace-switches, `automations`/`automations_business` gating comes free, and the bell/push + deep-link pattern already exists. Rejected: results-as-assets (transient review items aren't ownable objects), notifications-only (no persistence/status lifecycle/dedup). **Named inboxes are first-class**: admin creates them, routes `workflow_key`s to them, each carries its own `notify` + `reviewers` lists; unmatched keys land in an auto-created General inbox. Acting is admins + picked reviewers (personal scope: owner). Storage follows the pools model (business = `_team`, personal = own Brain; cap 500/scope, reviewed trimmed first). Dedup on `(workflow_key, external_id)` + a `seen` endpoint so n8n skips already-submitted listings **before** paying for AI qualification. Machines keep the `X-Automation-Token`; humans act via JWT (same split as assets). **How to apply:** future workflow-output features extend this inbox (per-workflow custom action sets are the backlogged generalization); don't create parallel result stores.
- **2026-07-12** — **Employees are real users; the automation token is machine-only.** Humans (crew/employees) authenticate with their own accounts so every asset write is attributed in `history` and revocable; the n8n `X-Automation-Token` is a shared machine super-credential (writes attributed `"automation"`, can target any store) and must never be handed to a person. **Why:** identity, per-person audit, instant revocation come free from the existing multi-user system; per-employee API tokens would be a parallel auth system. **How to apply:** any future "external actor writes to LogCore" feature must decide: human → user account + permission model; machine → automation token.
- **2026-07-12** — **Assets `contribute` access level is caps-configurable per entry, not a fixed policy.** Share entries (`shared_with`) and pool `contributors` entries with `access:"contribute"` carry `caps: {fields:[...], add:[comments|files|children]}` — the owner picks exactly what each person can change/add (UI: checkbox panel per entry). Enforcement lives in the router (PATCH filters to capped field keys — name/parent/notes always rejected; upload needs `files`; create-under needs `children`) on top of `find_asset()`'s `can_contribute` caps resolution (`normalize_caps()` defaults missing caps to comment-only). Pool contributors skip the accept handshake (pool assets are already workspace-visible); personal contribute shares use the normal request handshake and cascade/inheritance deep-copies `caps`. Contribute viewers get `_access:"contribute"` + `_caps` annotations and work inline in AssetView — they never see the editor. **How to apply:** new asset write paths must check `can_contribute` caps when `can_edit` is false, and new annotation surfaces must include `_caps`.
- **2026-07-12** — **By-name contributor entries downgrade pool_edit managers (second half of the specificity rule).** Owner's bug persisted after the share fix because the tester held the `pool_edit: ["household"]` grant — the pool branch computed `can_edit` from the grant BEFORE consulting contributors, so caps were dead code for any manager. Now (non-admin only): a **by-name** contributor entry on a pool asset downgrades a pool_edit manager to contribute-with-those-caps on that asset (`_contributor_caps(named_only=True)` decides; group/whole-team entries never downgrade managers; admins are never restricted). **How to apply:** the specificity ladder for pool assets is admin > by-name contributor entry > pool_edit grant > group contributor entry > read — keep new access logic consistent with it.
- **2026-07-12** — **Comments visibility: two different toggles, don't conflate them.** (1) Per-user collapse — plain component state in AssetView (any user, resets when the asset is reopened; never persisted). (2) `comments_hidden` kill-switch — on the asset record, set from the EDIT page by edit-level users (`can_edit`, not just manage), hides the section + blocks posting for everyone incl. automation.
- **2026-07-12** — **Grant resolution is specificity-based: a by-name entry fully overrides group/role entries.** Owner-reported bug: with a group entry (household edit or contribute w/ old caps) AND a per-user entry (new caps), the old union/edit-wins resolution blended them — changing one member's caps appeared to do nothing, and a group edit made per-member restriction impossible. `_resolve_grant()` in `assets_service.py` now splits matching entries into by-name vs group; the by-name set, when non-empty, is the ONLY set considered (within a level: edit > contribute > read; caps union only across same-level contribute entries). Same rule in `_contributor_caps` for pools. **How to apply:** never resolve asset grants by flat union across specificity levels; the specific entry is authoritative.
- **2026-07-12** — **Asset comments are an audit log: admin-only deletion; owners hide, not delete. Mutes are per-user and subtree-wide.** `DELETE /assets/{id}/comments/{cid}` requires admin (not author, not owner). Owners/pool-managers toggle `comments_hidden` on the asset (`PUT /assets/{id}/comments/visibility`) — hides the section for ALL users, blocks posting (service raises on `add_comment`), keeps data. Comment-notification opt-out is per user at `USERS/{name}/Assets/comment_mutes.json`; muting a node covers its whole subtree via an ancestor walk at delivery time (`_muted_node`), so new children are automatically covered; UI = bell button in AssetView (edit-level users only). **Why:** employees' notes must not be silently destroyable by whoever they report on; muting is a personal preference so it can never live on the shared asset record.
- **2026-07-12** — **Goal-type tasks are excluded from daily task surfaces at the scoring layer.** `priority_service._pending_non_goal()` filters `type=="goal"` out of `get_top3`/`get_all_scored` — so the dashboard Top 3, scored list, and morning digest skip goals; Tasks.jsx and Dashboard.jsx also filter client-side. Goals remain: Goals page, dated entries on the calendar, and `goal_drift` suggestions. A dashboard Goals widget is backlogged under the customizable-dashboard item.
- **2026-07-12** — **`hidden_from` supports dynamic `role:<feature_role>` entries; comments notify the edit audience.** Role hide entries follow the role (future hires included) unlike share targets which snapshot members — validated against features.json roles; viewer's `feature_role` must be passed as `viewer_role` into `list_visible`/`find_asset` (routers + agent tools do). Asset comments (`comments`, cap 100) are the multi-user-safe "leave notes" (the `notes` textarea is last-write-wins); posting requires edit-level or the `comments` cap, and each comment notifies edit-level users except the author via `suggestions_service.notify_user()` (in-app `open_asset` action + push with `/assets?asset=<id>` deep link — Assets.jsx consumes that query param).
- **2026-07-11** — **Asset API responses that feed the edit modal must carry `_owner`/`_access`.** `POST /assets` now annotates its response exactly like `list_visible()`/`find_asset()` do (store is a pool → `_owner: "team"|"household"`; store is another user's → `_owner: <name>`; both with `_access: "edit"`; own store → no annotation). **Why:** the create modal flips straight into edit mode on the create response (`setAsset(created)`); a bare record made the frontend treat a fresh pool asset as personal — it rendered the share selector and sent `shared_with` on save → 400 ("Pool assets are workspace-visible"), and a child created under an edit-shared foreign asset would call updateAccess → 403. **How to apply:** any endpoint that returns an asset the frontend will hold as modal state (create, future clone/duplicate, etc.) must annotate `_owner`/`_access` when the record's store isn't the requesting user's own.
- **2026-07-11** — **Default 📁 Folder template is seeded by migration `m006`, not hardcoded.** Global template `key: "folder"`, no custom fields (name + notes are core asset fields) — exists so users can group/organize assets with zero template setup. Seed is key-based and runs once: if an admin deletes or replaces it, it never comes back. Don't special-case `folder` anywhere in code — it's an ordinary global template.
- **2026-07-11** — **Assets are read-first: view before edit.** Clicking an existing asset opens a clean read-only overview (`components/AssetView.jsx`), not the editor. `AssetModal` holds a `mode` state — `'view'` for an existing asset, `'edit'` for a new one — and returns `<AssetView>` early when `mode==='view'`. The **✎ Edit** button (rendered only when `!readOnly`) flips `mode` to `'edit'`; the editor form is otherwise unchanged. Cancel from the editor returns to the view for an existing asset (`initialAsset` truthy) but closes for a brand-new/just-created one. Shared display helpers (`AttachmentThumb`, `formatChanges`, `fieldDisplay`) were extracted to `components/assetDisplay.jsx` so `AssetView` and `AssetModal` reuse them **without importing each other** (would be a circular import — keep new shared asset-display bits in that file, not in either component). Child drill-in: `AssetView`→`onOpenAsset(child)`→`Assets.jsx` re-targets the modal, and `<AssetModal key={modal.asset?.id||'new'}>` forces a fresh remount so `mode` resets to view. **Why:** owner's brother asked for an easily-readable page on first click with an opt-in switch to the editor. **How to apply:** any future per-object modal that mixes viewing and editing should default to a read view and gate edit behind a button + permission check, reusing this split.
- **2026-07-07** — **docs_reminder.sh expanded: full doc checklist + test-coverage nudge.** Base checklist now always includes CHANGELOG.md, PROJECT.md, and TESTING.md (TESTING.md also added to the mtime skip-check). New conditional **TESTS MISSING** item fires when `app/backend/` code (excluding `tests/`, `automations_stubs/`) changed since last stop but nothing in `app/backend/tests/` did — the Stop hook is the enforcement layer for "always test new backend behavior." `.pytest_cache` and venv dirs added to the changed-file exclusions (pytest runs were being counted as code changes).
- **2026-07-09** — **Assets Phase 2: per-user templates + request-based share handshake.** (1) **Actionable notifications** — `suggestions_service.add_notification(action={type,payload})` + `resolve_notification()`; a notif with `action` and `status!="resolved"` renders Accept/Decline in Layout's NotifBell → `POST /assets/shares/respond {notif_id, accept}` dispatches on `action.type`. (2) **All sharing is a per-user request.** Share entries are `{target, access?, accepted:[names]}`; `_share_access` grants only when viewer ∈ `accepted`. **Legacy entries without an `accepted` key stay open** (pre-Phase-2 shares work until re-shared). `update_access` resolves each NEW target (user | `team`=business-ws users | `household`=all users | role=users with that `feature_role`) and notifies each non-owner, non-accepted member. Accepting adds the viewer to `accepted` across the shared subtree (`_apply_share_response`); decline/leave removes them (silent — owner re-shares from the member list). Cascade + create-inheritance deep-copy `accepted` so the "group" grows. (3) **Per-user templates** — templates gain `id` (uuid), `owner` (`_global`|user), `shared_with`, `restrict_roles` (global only). Global store `_system/asset_templates.json`; personal `USERS/{name}/Assets/templates.json` (workspace-agnostic). **Assets reference `template_id`** (legacy `template` key still resolves to global via `resolve_template`). API embeds the resolved template as `_template` on each asset so a recipient can render a shared asset whose template they don't own. `visible_templates(viewer)` = role-permitted global + own + accepted-shared. `m005` backfills ids. Template sharing reuses the handshake (`action.type:"template_share"`); global templates can't be left (restricted by role instead). TemplateManager (now open to all users) has My/Global/Shared sections. Endpoints: template CRUD/`/access`/`/leave` **by id**, `/assets/{id}/leave`, `/assets/roles`.
- **2026-07-09** — **Frontend theme/crash gotchas.** (1) `applyAccentColor(hex)` sets INLINE `--accent-*` on `<html>` which overrides the `:root` brand-orange defaults; passing a falsy value now REMOVES those inline props (reset) — required so logout reverts the accent (else the login page shows the last user's accent). The Login submit button is hardcoded to literal `#f97316` (Tailwind `orange-*` is remapped to the accent var, so it isn't fixed on its own). (2) Theme prefs (background/accent/dark/density/corner) MUST be persisted to `localStorage` in `login()`/`refreshUser()` — the pre-React FOUC script in `main.jsx` reads them; if absent, the background flashes the default until `/me` resolves. (3) List endpoints consumed with `list || []` can crash on `.filter` if the API returns a truthy non-array (object) — use `Array.isArray(x) ? x : []`. Bit us at `Layout.jsx` NotifBell when a deployed backend returned a non-array from `/suggestions/notifications`. (4) Vite `build.sourcemap` is ON while debugging so the global `window.onerror` logger in `main.jsx` maps minified crashes to real file:line.
- **2026-07-09** — **Assets sharing is per-node + cascade + create-inheritance (Phase 1b).** Supersedes the earlier subtree-inherited sharing. `_share_access`/`_is_hidden_from` resolve **only the node itself** (no ancestor walk). `update_access(cascade=True default)` writes the audience to the node AND all descendants; `cascade=False` = this node only (UI toggle shown only when the node has descendants). Creating an asset **under** a parent copies the parent's `shared_with` + `hidden_from` onto the child (snapshot) AND the child is created in the **parent's store** (`POST /assets` routes into `find_asset(parent).store` when `parent_id` is set, requiring edit access) — the "group" mechanic: an edit-shared user grows a shared subtree owned by the original owner. Net: default still feels subtree-like (cascade default + inheritance) but each node lists its own audience, so a node shared without its parent shows at top level. Shares created before this change keep only their node's audience until re-shared. Frontend: filter/search always render the **foldered tree** (childrenMap promotes orphans to root); Move + create-parent use shared `components/AssetTreePicker.jsx`; `TagInput` suggestions are inline (not absolute) so a scrolling modal can't clip them; `EmojiPicker` popover is `right-0`, icon text capped 3 chars. Auth: `logout()` resets theme so the login page is clean without a reload; the 30s `/me` poll no longer ejects the session on a transient failure; `main.jsx` has a global error/unhandledrejection console logger.
- **2026-07-08** — **Assets v2 rules (Phase 1 shipped).** (1) **Archive is per-node, not inherited** — `_is_archived(asset)` checks only the node's own flag; an archived parent's active children float to top level (`childrenMap` promotes children whose parent isn't visible to roots). Archiving a node with active descendants prompts 3 choices: cascade / only-this / cancel (`set_archived(cascade=bool)`, `count_active_descendants()`). (2) **Owners can delete their own personal assets** (`find_asset` → `can_delete=True` for own; pool stays admin-only); still blocked with children. (3) **Share resolution is index-routed** — `services/assets_index.py` maintains `_system/assets_share_index.json` (owner→workspace→share targets), a disposable derived cache rebuilt at startup (`_warm_share_index` in main.py) and incrementally on `update_access`/`convert_to_pool`/`delete_asset`; `list_visible`/`find_asset` read only owners in `sharers_for(viewer, ws)`. Never a source of truth; `rebuild_share_index()` rescans. (4) **`GET /assets/members`** exposes member display names (names only) to any Assets user for share/hide selectors — flagged for a future permissioned/opt-in upgrade. (5) **Moves are same-owner only** (structural — `update_asset` sets a parent only within the asset's own store); ownership change stays admin Convert. (6) **Frontend crash rule:** `api.js` `request()` must NOT hard-log-out on a lone 401 — re-verify via `/auth/me` first so a transient/background 401 can't eject the user. Reusable `.modal-overlay`/`.modal-card` (index.css) give ALL modals iOS safe-area + dvh; `viewport-fit=cover` is required in index.html.
- **2026-07-07** — **Assets module design locked (MVP shipped).** (1) **Templates are instance-level and admin-only** (`brain/_system/asset_templates.json`) — every viewer and n8n sees the same ordered typed field structure; template `key` is immutable (asset records + workflows reference it). (2) **Assets are per-user per-workspace** (`ws_path/Assets/assets.json`) with **subtree sharing** (`shared_with`: team/household/named user, read|edit) and **`hidden_from` exclusions that beat shares** — both enforced server-side in `list_visible()`/`find_asset()`, never render-only. (3) **Pool conversion is a physical move** to `_household`/`_team` stores (records + attachment dirs) so pool objects survive account deletion; pool writes reuse the `pool_edit` grant model. (4) **Archive-first lifecycle**: hard delete is admin-only + confirm and 409-blocked with children — never cascade. (5) **Status is a template select field, not a core field** (UI badges any select field keyed `status`). (6) **Vite bundle moved to `/static`** (`build.assetsDir`) because the `/assets` page route was shadowed by the old static mount — never mount static files on a path that is also an app route. (7) **Automation API token** (`brain/_system/automations_config.json`, `X-Automation-Token`, admin reveal/rotate in Admin → n8n) is the shared auth mechanism for n8n→LogCore writes; the future Automation Inbox must reuse it.
- **2026-07-06** — **Approve mode is the chat default; write-gating is allowlist-based.** New chat mode `approve`: reads execute freely, any write tool call pauses the run (`awaiting_approval` status, `pending_write` steps, nothing executed that turn). Approval is stateless by design — the UI re-sends "Yes, go ahead." as a one-turn `auto` request rather than persisting pending tool calls server-side (same trust model as plan mode's confirm flow; no new state machinery). `_READ_TOOLS` in `agent_service.py` is the read-only allowlist — any tool not in it is approval-gated, so NEW TOOLS ARE WRITE-GATED BY DEFAULT; add genuinely read-only tools to the set explicitly (guarded by `test_new_tools_are_write_gated_by_default`).
- **2026-07-06** — **Chat archive format is line-marker markdown and must round-trip.** Archives store messages as `**You**: `/`**AI**: ` first-line markers with continuation lines below (multi-line content is written verbatim). Any parser of these files (frontend `parseSavedChat`, future consumers) MUST accumulate continuation lines — a line-filter parse truncates messages, and because auto-save overwrites the archive with current state, a lossy parse becomes permanent data loss on continue. `HistoryMessage.content` cap is 30,000 (not 5000): long agent responses must both save and re-validate when a continued chat is sent back to `/chat`; user input stays capped at 5000 via `ChatRequest.message`.
- **2026-07-06** — **Agent writes referencing a member name must resolve, never guess.** `_resolve_member_name()` in `agent_service.py` (exact → first-name → prefix, case-insensitive; ambiguous/unknown → error listing candidates so the agent asks the user). Applied to `add_shared_task`/`update_shared_task` `assigned_to`; apply the same helper to any future agent tool that takes a member name (e.g. team-pool tools when added).
- **2026-07-06** — **RAG index design rule locked (pre-build):** when RAG over the Brain is built, the vector index must be a *disposable derived cache* — embedded file-backed store (e.g. Chroma), rebuildable from Brain files at any time, never a source of truth, no new stateful service in the Docker stack. This is the condition under which RAG is compatible with the no-database philosophy (PROJECT.md already permits "databases as generated indexes"). Local-embeddings option should pair with the Ollama work.
- **2026-07-06** — **Product milestones v0.2→v1.0 adopted** (from the mobile roadmap/vision session), mapped to business phases (tracked outside this repo); sequencing driven by demand. Backlogged by that rule: quick capture, offline-first sync, journal insight loops. The v1.0 trust stack (app 2FA, automated backups+restore, audit logging, <10-min onboarding) gates the Show HN post.
