Roadmap-doc corrections: proactive digest, per-workspace AI context, basic recurring tasks, workspaces/Team/HA/n8n were already shipped.
- **2026-06-25** — Feature Flags + RBAC system added. `guest` is the security default for new users; `member` is the internal fallback when a feature role goes missing. Both are protected built-in roles.
- **2026-06-25** — Shared React state lifted to `Admin` component so `UsersCard` and `RolesCard` see the same roles list in real time without separate fetches.
- **2026-06-25** — Auth role and feature role are kept fully separate systems. Auth role controls endpoint access; feature role controls module visibility.
- **2026-06-29** — Automations module added. n8n is bundled as `logcore-n8n` Docker service. Personal workflows stored in `brain/USERS/{name}/Automations/workflows.json`; business workflows in `brain/_system/automations_index.json`. Business workflow JSON files are NOT committed to the repo — admins import them via the Admin UI on managed servers. This keeps self-hosters from receiving business content automatically (the trade-off of open-source).
- **2026-06-29** — Infisical secrets are written to `docker/n8n.env` at startup (when Infisical is configured) so n8n workflows can reference them as `{{ $env.VAR_NAME }}`. Admin → n8n card can trigger a re-sync and restart.
- **2026-06-29** — Setup wizard `show_profile_type` is now gated on `features.json` existence (first-user only). Subsequent users skip the Personal/Business question — their choice would be a no-op since `init_features()` is idempotent.
- **2026-06-29** — `automations` and `automations_business` are two separate module IDs. `automations` gates the page + Personal tab; `automations_business` gates only the Business tab within the page. Default: personal members get `automations` on, `automations_business` off; business members get both on. This allows granular per-user tab control without a second nav entry.
- **2026-06-29** — Home Assistant integration added as `home` module. Config stored in `brain/_system/ha_config.json` (url + token). Service: `ha_service.py`. Router: `routers/home.py`. Frontend: `pages/Home.jsx` (domain tabs, entity tiles, scenes, automations). Dashboard widget shows pinned favourite entities with quick toggle. 4 AI tools added to agent_service.py (`get_home_state`, `control_home_device`, `activate_scene`, `trigger_home_automation`); only injected into tool list when HA is configured. User favourites stored per-user at `brain/USERS/{name}/Home/favourites.json`.
- **2026-07-01** — Workspace switching is fully wired end-to-end. Calendar/Tasks/Notes include `workspace` in their useEffect deps so they re-fetch on workspace switch. Layout.jsx `useEffect([workspace])` now also redirects to `/` if the current page's module has a `workspace` constraint that doesn't match the new workspace (handles Journal → business, Household → business, Team → personal). Calendar uses `poolApi` (sharedApi for personal, teamApi for business); toggle shows 🏠/🧑‍🤝‍🧑 accordingly; EventModal uses generic `poolSaveApi`/`poolLabel` props. Chat is fully workspace-aware: `_build_context`, memory writes (`append_memory`, `rewrite_memory`), saved chat paths, and agent brain tools (`search_brain`, `list_brain_files`, `read_brain_file`) all use `ws_path()`. Optional cross-workspace AI search toggle in Chat toolbar — only shown when user has both workspaces.
- **2026-06-30** — Workspace-based module visibility added. `household`, `journal`, `home` are personal-only; `team`, `automations_business` are business-only. Filtering is frontend-only (Layout.jsx `visibleModules` filter + Automations.jsx tab guard). Backend `require_module()` still enforces access regardless of workspace. Admin can override per-user via `disabled_modules`.
- **2026-06-30** — Admins always get both workspaces. First registered user (admin) is given `["personal", "business"]` at registration. Existing admins receive a lazy one-time migration in `get_current_user()`: if `"business"` is missing from their workspaces, it's written to `auth.json` on their next authenticated request.
- **2026-06-30** — `disabled_modules` is now computed at login. `/login` and `/register` call `get_effective_disabled()` before returning, so the frontend has correct role-based restrictions immediately without waiting for the first `/me` poll. Workspace switching triggers an immediate `/me` re-fetch (Layout.jsx `useEffect([workspace])`) so module list updates instantly.
- **2026-06-30** — Shortcuts Settings page now has independent Personal and Business panels. Each panel only shows modules valid for that workspace (enabled + correct workspace field). `cleanShortcuts(ws, user)` in `Settings.jsx` strips invalid IDs at init/sync time so slots are truly empty, not just hidden. Both workspaces are saved in one `PATCH /auth/me` call. Previously, the shortcuts picker showed all modules regardless of disabled state or workspace, allowing users to pin inaccessible modules.
- **2026-06-30** — n8n `is_configured()` guard added to all action endpoints. `n8n_service.is_configured()` checks that `api_key` is non-empty. `run`, `activate`, `deactivate`, and `logs` endpoints return 503 with a human-readable message if n8n is not configured, instead of propagating httpx network errors.
- **2026-06-30** — Claude Code hook system overhauled. `docs_loader.sh` now injects SOUL.md inline + Read instructions (~2.5KB) instead of dumping all file contents (was ~48KB, exceeded Claude Code's 2KB injection preview limit). `docs_reminder.sh` now skips the reminder when tracked docs were modified since last stop (mtime check vs `/tmp/logcoreos_last_stop`). `safety_check.sh` expanded with `git branch -D` and `git stash drop/clear` blocks.
- **2026-07-01** — Workspace-aware priorities. `profile_service.get_priority_order(user_name, workspace)` now accepts a workspace param. Personal profile lives at `brain/USERS/{name}/profile.json`; business at `brain/USERS/{name}/Business/profile.json` (follows `ws_path()`). `priority_service.get_top3/get_all_scored` pass `workspace` to `get_priority_order`. Pool pseudo-users (`_household`, `_team`) have their own `profile.json` at `brain/USERS/_household/profile.json` and `brain/USERS/_team/profile.json` — admin-managed via `GET/PUT /api/v1/priorities/pool`. `GET /api/v1/tasks/assigned` returns pending pool tasks where `assigned_to == current_user`, tagged with `_source: "household"|"team"`. Tasks.jsx routes completions using `_source`, not `_household` flag.
- **2026-07-01** — `docs_reminder.sh` made dynamic. Now detects which project files changed since last stop using `find -newer` + `python3 os.utime` reference file; builds targeted checklist (MAP.md, API.md, AGENTS.md, PROJECT sections) based on which file types changed. `docs_loader.sh` adds a REQUIRED step 2 when a pending reminder is present, making docs updates an explicit required action rather than passive context.
- **2026-07-01** — UI conventions locked in: sign-out button lives in the sidebar (below Settings, above collapse toggle) and mobile drawer footer — NOT on the Settings page. Settings page Account card only has Export Brain. Brain editor (`/brain`) shows "← Settings" back button in the file list view header.
- **2026-07-02** — Instance-wide workspace visibility. Admin → Workspaces card writes `enabled_workspaces` into `auth.json` runtime settings (via existing `GET/PATCH /auth/admin/settings`). `auth_service.enabled_workspaces()` returns it (default both, never empty). `get_current_user()` intersects each user's `workspaces` with the enabled set (persisted admin migration only adds `business` when enabled), so a hidden workspace disappears for **everyone including admins**; the active-workspace localStorage value auto-resets via Layout.jsx's existing effect. Persisted per-user workspaces are untouched, so re-enabling restores access. `PATCH /admin/users/{id}/workspaces` rejects instance-disabled workspaces.
- **2026-07-02** — Per-user pool-management grant (`pool_edit`). Household/team writes (create/edit/delete events, create/edit/delete/assign tasks) now require admin **or** a dedicated per-user `pool_edit` grant list (`["household","team"]`), enforced by `require_pool_edit(pool)` in `routers/auth.py` applied per-endpoint on all POST/PATCH/DELETE in `shared.py`/`team.py` (GET stays open to members). **Why a dedicated field, not a `disabled_modules` module:** `get_effective_disabled()` unions role-disabled with per-user-disabled — it can only *add* restrictions, never grant something the role disabled. A default-off, grant-per-user permission is the opposite shape, so it cannot live in the module system. Grant is default `[]` (matches prior behavior where only admins could write); admins always bypass. Admin UI: "Can manage" row (Household/Team checkboxes) in UsersCard → `PATCH /admin/users/{id}/pool-edit`. `pool_edit` is returned by `/me` (frontend `user.poolEdit`) and in admin user fields. New `GET /shared/members` + `GET /team/members` (gated by the same grant) feed the assign dropdown for granted non-admins — resolves the old "assignment for non-admins" backlog item.
- **2026-06-29** — Business workflow auto-sync via stub files. Stub files (`*.stub.json`) committed to `app/backend/automations_stubs/` contain only name + key + tags — no workflow logic. On startup (90 s delay for n8n to boot) and every 6 hours, the app fetches actual workflow JSONs from `WORKFLOWS_BASE_URL/{key}.json` using `WORKFLOWS_TOKEN` (both from Infisical), reconciles against n8n (create new / update changed via content hash / delete removed stubs). Recommended source: private GitHub repo + fine-grained PAT (`Contents: Read-only`). Self-hosted instances without those Infisical secrets skip silently. Admin can force an immediate sync via the "Sync Workflows Now" button in Admin → n8n card or `POST /api/v1/automations/n8n/sync-workflows`.
- **2026-08-24** — **Mod Store universal module system, phase 1: registry infrastructure (zero behavior change, no real modules yet).** New `app/backend/module_registry.py` — `ModuleManifest` dataclass + `discover_manifests()`/`active_manifests()`/`register_routers()`, importlib-based discovery of `module_packages/<id>/manifest.py`. **This mechanism is safe only because module_packages/ is first-party, reviewed code — never a community-submission surface**; opening it up would need real sandboxing, not just a checkbox. Design locked with the owner: EVERY module eventually converts into this format, including core ones (Tasks/Chat/Dashboards-the-feature) — a locked module just carries `uninstallable=True` on its manifest, no separate "not really a module" category. `dashboard_blocks/` and `agent_service.py`'s tool-orchestration engine are the one exception: pure cross-module infrastructure with no router of their own, never a module. Rollout is one module at a time, each fully shipped before the next — journal is increment 1 (not yet done), home is increment 2, then Calendar/Tasks/Team+Household/Chat/Notes/Automations/Dashboards/Assets/Contacts/Finance in roughly that order (full plan: `/home/logcore/.claude/plans/i-want-you-to-composed-scone.md`).
  **Real bugs this pass's tests caught** (all fixed): (1) `discover_manifests()` was blindly `importlib.import_module()`-ing every directory under `module_packages/`, including `__pycache__` once Python compiled bytecode for it — now requires an actual `manifest.py` file and skips `_`-prefixed dirs before attempting import. (2) Route-ordering: the SPA catch-all (`/{full_path:path}`) used to be registered via a plain `@app.get` decorator at *import time*; module routers can only be registered inside `lifespan()` *after* `run_migrations()` (a locked module's install-state depends on its own upgrade migration having already run) — Starlette matches routes by insertion order, so the catch-all had to move into `lifespan()` too, positioned after `register_routers()`, or it would shadow every dynamically-registered module route. (3) A locked module's registration failure must crash boot loudly (`LockedModuleRegistrationError`), not log-and-skip like an optional module — the lenient path would leave the app looking healthy while something as load-bearing as Tasks was silently missing.
  **Enforcement-gap fixes landed this pass** (generic, manifest-driven, all per-user not instance-wide): `routers/brain.py`'s skip-list, `agent_service.py`'s `_brain_skip()`, `_get_tools()`'s module-tool filtering (new `_module_tool_dispatch()`/`_module_tool_schemas()`), `dashboard_blocks/registry.py`'s `BlockSpec.module` + `catalog()`, `dashboard_blocks/render.py`'s `render_block()` (module check threaded through **whichever identity that pass resolves as** — viewer at pass 1, owner at pass 2's `share_underlying_data` exception, not a flat owner-only check). `features_service.get_effective_disabled()` is the one choke point unioning role-disabled + user-disabled + not-installed, so every consumer gets "not installed" for free. `help_service.capabilities_index()` now describes every module regardless of state (installed/disabled/not-installed) for AI awareness — a purely informational channel; the real tool list stays hard-gated. `routers/export.py` is a deliberate no-op (a user's own data export should never depend on a module being installed).
  **Shape changes**: `features_service.ALL_MODULE_IDS` (list) → `all_module_ids()` (function) — 4 call sites, not the 2 originally assumed (`features_service.py`, `routers/features.py`, `routers/auth.py`'s `VALID_MODULE_IDS` **× 2 separate validators**, `routers/chat.py`). `migrations/runner.py`'s inline loop refactored into `_run_list()`, shared by core `MIGRATIONS` and a new `_module_migrations()` pass that runs for every *discovered* module regardless of install state (same as core migrations always run regardless of feature toggles).
  40 new tests across `test_module_registry.py`/`test_mod_store_service.py`/`test_features_service_modules.py`/`test_brain_module_gating.py`/`test_dashboard_block_module_gating.py`/`test_agent_module_tools.py`/`test_help_service_modules.py`, using a new `fake_module` fixture (`tests/conftest.py`) that writes real `module_packages/<id>/` packages to disk for one test (real importlib discovery, not mocked) and cleans up after. Full suite: 832 passed.
- **2026-08-24** — **Journal converted into `module_packages/journal/` — Mod Store increment 1, backend half complete.** Moved (not copied): `routers/journal.py` → `module_packages/journal/backend/router.py`, `services/journal_service.py` → `.../backend/service.py`, `dashboard_blocks/_journal.py` → `.../backend/dashboard_block.py` (now `module="journal"`), its 3 agent tool schemas + executor cases → `.../backend/agent_tools.py` (`TOOL_SCHEMAS` + `execute()`), its `content/help.json` section → `manifest.help_section`. Deleted from core: the 2 `main.py` registration lines, `"journal"` from `features_service._CORE_MODULE_IDS`/`_BUSINESS_MEMBER`, the `journal_service` import + tool schemas/cases + `_RESEARCH_TOOLS` entries in `agent_service.py`, `_journal` from `dashboard_blocks/registry.py`'s hardcoded resolver tuple, journal's `content/help.json` section, `brain/USERS/_template/Journal/` from the setup template. Verified end-to-end (not just unit tests): a real `TestClient` lifespan boot with journal installed correctly registers its router, its dashboard block, and its agent tools, and a live write→read round-trip through the moved service works.
  **Real gap found and fixed mid-conversion**: `write_journal_entry` wasn't in `_RESEARCH_TOOLS`/`_READ_TOOLS` (core's "new tools are write-gated by default" rule) — the phase-1 module-tool plumbing had no way for a module to declare which of its tools are read-only. Fixed: `ModuleManifest` gained `read_only_agent_tools` (a subset of `owned_agent_tools`); `module_registry.read_only_agent_tool_names()` unions it across active modules; both `agent_service.py` call sites (research-mode filtering, approve-mode write-gating) now check `_RESEARCH_TOOLS | read_only_agent_tool_names()` / `_READ_TOOLS | read_only_agent_tool_names()` instead of the bare core sets. Without this, `read_journal_entry` would have silently stopped working in research mode and started requiring approval in approve mode — a real functional regression, not just a missed nice-to-have.
  **Test infrastructure fix, affects every future module conversion**: a module's own tests (`module_packages/<id>/tests/`) need the same `brain`/`fake_module` fixtures as `app/backend/tests/`, but conftest.py fixtures only inherit down a directory tree, not across sibling trees. Moved `tests/conftest.py`'s content to a new `app/backend/conftest.py` (the true common ancestor) and deleted the old one. Added `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` `testpaths = ["tests", "module_packages"]` to `pyproject.toml` so a bare `pytest`/`pytest -v` discovers both trees — the old `pytest tests/ -v` convention still narrows to core-only since an explicit path on the command line overrides `testpaths`. Journal's moved test file (`module_packages/journal/tests/test_journal_service.py`) is the first real proof this works — confirmed 18/18 passing standalone and as part of the full suite.
  **The `m015_backfill_journal_installed_from_existing_data` migration lives in the module's own `manifest.py`** (not `migrations/runner.py`'s core `MIGRATIONS` list), namespaced `"journal:m015_..."`, discovered via the existing module-migration pass from phase 1. Guards on `_system/features.json` existence (same idiom as `m007`/`m008`) to distinguish upgrading instances (mark installed unconditionally — journal was always on) from fresh installs (stay uninstalled — the actual goal). `on_install()`/`on_new_user()` hooks (also in the manifest) replace the old unconditional template copy: `on_install` backfills `Journal/` for every existing non-pseudo user at install time, `on_new_user` (called generically by `routers/setup.py` for every currently-active module right after the template copytree) does the same for signups that happen after journal's already installed — one code path for both cases instead of two that could drift.
  **Not moved, deliberately** (matches the plan's own accepted exceptions): `lib/api.js`'s journal client, `deepLinks.js`'s journal entry, and cross-reference prose mentioning journal in *other* help.json sections (e.g. Chat's blurb) — all frontend/cosmetic, not functional. Frontend conversion (moving `pages/Journal.jsx`, the dynamic `import.meta.glob` loading mechanism, the Mod Store admin UI) is separate, still pending.
  Full suite: 838 passed (832 from phase 1 + 6 new integration tests in `test_journal_module_conversion.py`, covering the migration fresh/upgrade split, `on_install`/`on_new_user` backfill including the pseudo-user exclusion, and a full install→uninstall→reinstall round-trip proving data survives the whole cycle).
- **2026-08-24** — **Journal's frontend converted — Mod Store increment 1 fully complete (backend + frontend).** `src/lib/moduleRegistry.js` uses `import.meta.glob('/src/module_packages/*/manifest.js', { eager: true })` to auto-discover every converted module's frontend manifest at build time — confirmed via a real `npm run build`: `Journal.jsx` and `JournalEntryBlock.jsx` land in their own separate lazy-loaded chunks (`dist/static/Journal-*.js`, `dist/static/JournalEntryBlock-*.js`), not bundled into the main JS. `constants.js`'s `ALL_MODULES`, `deepLinks.js`'s `MODULE_ROUTES`, and `blockRegistry.js`'s `BLOCK_REGISTRY`/`CONFIG_FIELD_SCHEMAS` all merge in `MODULE_PACKAGES` generically now — no per-module edit to any of them going forward. `App.jsx` gained one additive loop over `MODULE_PACKAGES` (each wrapped in `<ModuleRoute>` + `<Suspense>`) instead of journal's old hardcoded import+route.
  **New `activeModuleIds` in `AuthProvider`** (`lib/auth.jsx`), fetched from `GET /mod-store/active` on the same mount+30s-poll cadence as `refreshUser()`. This closes a real correctness gap `disabledModules` alone can't: between an admin clicking Install and clicking Restart Now, `disabledModules` already says "not disabled" (the marker flipped) but the backend process hasn't registered the router yet — `ModuleRoute` now requires *both* `!disabledModules.includes(id)` *and* (for a `isPackageModule` id) `activeModuleIds.includes(id)` before allowing the route. Nav visibility (`Layout.jsx`) needed **zero changes** — its existing `disabledIds` filter already worked correctly once `ALL_MODULES` included package-derived entries, since `disabledModules` already correctly includes "not installed" thanks to the phase-1 backend work.
  **Real gap caught by actually running the app, not just building it**: journal was fully functional as a module but was never added to `content/mod_store_catalog.json` — `get_catalog()` only ever iterates catalog *entries*, so it would never have appeared in the Mod Store UI at all despite being completely wired up. Added `{id: "journal", status: "available", ...}`. Verified the full real cycle against a live `uvicorn` instance (not just pytest): register → journal absent from `disabled_modules`? no, present (correct, fresh install) → install → `/mod-store/active` still empty, `disabled_modules` now empty but router not yet live → restart (process kill+relaunch) → `/mod-store/active` includes journal, `GET /api/v1/journal` returns real `application/json` (not the SPA-fallback HTML that silently 200s for any unmatched path — a genuine gotcha: an unregistered API path here does NOT 404, it falls through to `index.html`, so a bare HTTP status check can't distinguish "real endpoint" from "SPA catch-all," content-type/body must be checked) → wrote and read back a real entry → uninstalled → `disabled_modules` immediately includes journal again (data safety doesn't wait for restart, as designed) → entry file confirmed byte-identical on disk, completely untouched.
  **lib/api.js's `get`/`post`/`put`/`patch`/`del` are now exported** (were private module-scope consts) — a module's own `frontend/api.js` (e.g. `module_packages/journal/frontend/api.js`) builds on these shared session-handling/error-normalization helpers instead of reimplementing fetch/credentials/header logic per module, mirroring how a module's *backend* service imports `file_service.py`'s shared helpers rather than reimplementing atomic writes.
  **Not exercised, disclosed honestly**: no browser-automation tooling (Playwright/chromium-cli) is available in this environment, so the actual React rendering/click-through was never visually verified — what's confirmed is the real HTTP/API cycle end-to-end plus the production build's code-splitting, not a screenshot-verified UI interaction.
  **Safety note**: a `pkill -f "uvicorn main:app"` during manual verification nearly matched a long-running, pre-existing instance on port 8000 (a different, real dev instance under the same OS user) — it survived only because permission was denied on that specific PID. Broad process-pattern kills are dangerous even when scoped to "look for this app's process name"; killed/restarted only a disposable test instance on its own port (8123) by exact PID for the rest of that session.
- **2026-08-24** — **Mod Store admin UI shipped (`pages/settings/admin/ModStore.jsx`) — the full install/uninstall/restart cycle is now clickable, not just curl-able.** Wired into `AdminMenu.jsx` + `App.jsx` at `/settings/admin/mod-store`. Three explicit states per row (`rowState()`: not_installed / pending_install / pending_uninstall / active / always_active / coming_soon / error) computed from `installed` (the marker, in the catalog response) crossed with `active` (from `GET /mod-store/active`) — matches the two-signal design from phase 1, not a bare toggle. A page-level "Restart Now" banner appears whenever any row is in a pending state (install OR uninstall pending — the router can be stale in either direction until a restart). Confirm-before-uninstall and the online-users-before-restart warning both use plain `window.confirm()`, matching this codebase's existing convention (`UserDetail.jsx`'s bank-disconnect confirm) rather than a custom modal.
  **Verified against real backend responses, not assumed** — ran the actual `rowState()` function (extracted straight from the shipped file via Node, not reimplemented) against real `GET /mod-store/catalog`+`/active` responses at each stage: fresh (not_installed) → installed-not-restarted (pending_install) → restarted (active). Also verified the online-users conflict path against a real 409: registered a second user via `POST /auth/admin/users` (self-registration closes after the first user — a real gotcha hit while testing, not a bug), logged them in, pinged presence, then confirmed `POST /mod-store/restart` returns exactly the `{detail: {message, online_users}}` shape `modStore.restart()` (`lib/api.js`) expects, and that `force: true` correctly bypasses the check and proceeds to the (in this sandbox, Docker-less) restart attempt, which 502s only because no real `logcore-app` container exists here — the expected, already-documented limitation.
  This closes out Mod Store increment 1 completely — journal is a fully working, fully clickable, fully tested example of the conversion pattern. Increment 2 (Home) and the rest of the rollout order are tracked in `docs/TASKS.md`.
